# كتاب المواعظ — al-Mawāʿiẓ (The Exhortations)

**Compiler:** al-Shaykh al-Ṣadūq, Abū Jaʿfar Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn Bābawayh al-Qummī (d. 381 AH)
**Purpose:** feasibility review of AI-translated hadith books — this is a machine draft, not a publishable translation.

**Size:** 429 segments, ~12,573 Arabic words → ~22,075 English words. 333 rows carry translator notes.

## How this was produced

| Step | Detail |
|---|---|
| Source | Scanned edition in *Muṣannafāt al-Shaykh al-Ṣadūq* (Dār al-Amīn), vol. 1, pp. 293–383, via the eShia image library ([pdf.lib.eshia.ir/97102](https://pdf.lib.eshia.ir/97102/1/293)). No clean typed Arabic edition was found online — the Ghaemiyeh digital copies have their Arabic destroyed by an encoding error. |
| Transcription | Arabic read from the page images by Claude (vision), preserving the printed diacritics. Editor's footnotes and running headers omitted. |
| Translation | Claude, from the transcribed Arabic only — no existing English translation was consulted. |
| Markers | `[؟]` in the Arabic = uncertain reading of the scan. **Notes** under a row = the translator's flagged ambiguity or alternative reading. |

Page numbers (p. N) refer to the printed edition, so any row can be checked against the scan at `https://pdf.lib.eshia.ir/97102/1/N`.

## Transcription spot-check

The opening of the Prophet's counsel to ʿAlī (pp. 294–296) also exists as typed, vocalized text in the Rewayaat corpus ([Man Lā Yaḥḍuruh al-Faqīh #807](https://hadith.academyofislam.com/hadith/Man-La-Yahduruh-al-Faqih-Volume-4-Saduq:807)). Comparing word-by-word, ignoring diacritics:

- 190 of 204 words agree outright.
- Every one of the remaining differences was checked against the scan, and **each is a genuine textual variant, not a transcription error**: الغيظ / غيظاً, ولا يهتمّ / ولا يهمّ, شرّه / فحشه, صلواته / صلاته, فقال / قال. The edition's own footnotes record the Faqīh readings for most of them.

This covers only about 200 words, so it says nothing about diacritics. Printed diacritics were preserved where visible, but at this scan resolution they are the weakest part of the transcription.

---


## The Author's Introduction

<p dir="rtl" lang="ar"><b>مقدمة المؤلّف</b></p>

<table>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">الحمد لوليّه ومستحقّه وأفضل الصلوة والسلام على أشرف خلقه محمّد خاتم الأنبياء والمرسلين العظام، وعلى بضعته الطاهرة وأوصيائه المعصومين الكرام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Praise belongs to the One who is its rightful Master and who alone deserves it. The best of blessings and peace be upon the noblest of His creation, Muḥammad, the Seal of the prophets and of the great messengers, and upon his pure daughter [lit. 'the pure part of him'] and his noble, infallible successors.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> بضعته ('a part of him') is the standard allusion to Fāṭima (a.s.), from the hadith 'Fāṭima is a part of me'. 'Successors' translates أوصيائه (his appointed legatees, i.e. the Imams). The first clause could also be read 'Praise belongs to Him to whom it is due and who deserves it' (وليّ = the one entitled to it).</sub><br><sub>p. 293</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">أما بعد، فهذه لآلي غوالي وجواهر زواهر، وصايا خرجت من عمّان النبوّة ومعدن الرسالة، محلّ البركات الإلهيّة، ومنزل الرحمات الغير المتناهية بالأصالة صلى الله عليه وسلّم وعلى خلفائه الطاهرين، أكرم البرايا على الله، وإليه التقطا حلّ المشكلات وفيصل القضايا، سيدنا ومولانا أمير المؤمنين، وإمام المسلمين، علي بن أبي طالب صلوات الله وتسليماته عليه، وعلى عترته المعصومين، فطوبى لمن وعاها وأوعاها خزانة قلبه، ومرحباً بمن سعى في حفظها بمقتضى سلامة عقله ولبّه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">To proceed: these are precious pearls and shining jewels. They are counsels that came forth from the ocean of prophethood and the mine of the divine message: the seat of divine blessings and the station where endless mercies descend in the first instance (Allah bless him and grant him peace, and his pure successors, the most honored of creatures before Allah). They were addressed to [? التقطا, marked 'sic' by the editor] the resolver of difficulties and the one who decides disputes, our master and lord Amir al-Mu'minin (Commander of the Faithful) and Imam of the Muslims, ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, the blessings and greetings of peace of Allah be upon him and upon his infallible offspring. So blessed is the one who takes them in and stores them in the treasury of his heart, and welcome to the one who strives to memorize them, as the soundness of his intellect and understanding requires.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> (1) وإليه التقطا [؟]: the edition prints التقطا and its footnote says only كذا (sic). The word is corrupt. A plausible intended reading is وإليه المُلتجأ ('and to him is the recourse [for] resolving difficulties'), which describes ʿAlī. Another is that the counsels were directed 'to him'. I rendered it as 'addressed to' and flagged it. (2) عمّان النبوّة: I took عمّان as 'ocean/sea' (the sense in later literary Arabic and Persian), not the place-name. (3) بالأصالة ('originally, in the first instance, inherently') is ambiguous. It may modify 'the station of endless mercies' (the Prophet is the primary locus of mercy), or it may modify the counsels' origin. (4) لآلي غوالي and الغير المتناهية are non-classical spellings and constructions, kept as printed. (5) وعاها وأوعاها: 'takes them in [understands them] and stores them'.</sub><br><sub>p. 293</sub></td>
</tr>
</table>


## The Counsels of the Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.) to Amir al-Mu'minin (a.s.)

<p dir="rtl" lang="ar"><b>وصايا رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله) لأمير المؤمنين (عليه السلام)</b></p>

<table>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١] روى حمّاد بن عمرو؛ وأنس بن محمّد، عن أبيه جميعاً، عن جعفر بن محمّد، عن أبيه، عن جدّه، عن عليّ بن أبي طالب صلوات الله عليه، عن النبي (صلى الله عليه وآله) أنّه قال له:<br>يا عليُّ، أوصيك بوصيّة فاحفظها فلا تزال بخير ما حفظت وصيّتي.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[1] Ḥammād ibn ʿAmr, and Anas ibn Muḥammad from his father, both narrated from Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad, from his father, from his grandfather, from ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib (the blessings of Allah be upon him), from the Prophet (s.a.w.), that he said to him:<br>O ʿAlī, I am giving you a counsel, so keep it. You will not cease to be in a good state as long as you keep my counsel.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> جميعاً means that both lines (Ḥammād directly, and Anas via his father) go back to Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad (al-Ṣādiq, a.s.). The vocative in this first counsel appears to be printed with a damma (عليُّ), while the later ones carry only a shadda (عليّ); I transcribed it as printed. 'Keep' (احفظ) can also mean 'memorize' or 'preserve'.</sub><br><sub>p. 294</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من كظم الغيظ وهو يقدر على إمضائه أعقبه الله يوم القيامة أمناً وإيماناً يجد طعمه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever swallows his anger while he is able to act on it, Allah will give him in return, on the Day of Resurrection, security and a faith whose taste he will find.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This saying continues onto p. 295 (وإيماناً يجد طعمه). The editor's footnote gives the Faqīh reading غيظاً for الغيظ, which does not change the meaning. إمضائه: 'carrying it out, giving effect to it'.</sub><br><sub>p. 294</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من لم يحسن وصيّته عند موته كان نقصاً في مروءته ولم يملك الشفاعة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever does not make a good will at his death, that is a deficiency in his manly virtue, and he will not possess intercession.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> مروءة: honor, decency, manly virtue. The editor's footnote explains ولم يملك الشفاعة as: he is not entitled to intercede for anyone, nor to be interceded for, because he neglected to do good to himself by leaving nothing for charity in his third [of the estate]. The phrase could therefore also mean 'nor will intercession avail him'.</sub><br><sub>p. 295</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أفضل الجهاد من أصبح ولا يهتمّ بظلم أحد.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the best jihad (striving in the way of Allah) is that of one who rises in the morning with no intention of wronging anyone.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> يهتمّ بـ here most likely means 'to resolve on, intend' (from همّ), i.e. not even contemplating injustice. The alternative reading is 'not concerned with anyone's wrong against him', but that fits less well. The Faqīh reads ولا يهمّ. Literally the text says 'the best jihad is one who...'; the implied 'that of' has been supplied.</sub><br><sub>p. 295</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، مَن خاف الناس لسانه فهو من أهل النار.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever is feared by people because of his tongue is among the people of the Fire.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Literally 'whose tongue people fear'.</sub><br><sub>p. 295</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، شرّ الناس من أكرمه الناس إتّقاء شرّه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the worst of people is the one whom people treat with respect to guard against his evil.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor's footnote notes a variant: فحشه ('his obscenity') for شرّه.</sub><br><sub>p. 295</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، شرّ الناس من باع آخرته بدنياه، وشرّ من ذلك من باع آخرته بدنيا غيره.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the worst of people is one who sells his Hereafter for his worldly life, and worse than that is one who sells his Hereafter for someone else's worldly life.<br><sub>p. 295</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من لم يقبل العذر من متنصّل صادقاً كان أو كاذباً، لم ينل شفاعتي.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever does not accept the excuse of someone who apologizes, whether he is truthful or lying, will not attain my intercession.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> متنصّل: the editor glosses it as one who disowns his offense and apologizes for it.</sub><br><sub>p. 295</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إنّ الله عزّ وجلّ أحبّ الكذب في الصلاح، وأبغض الصدق في الفساد.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, loves lying [when it is] for setting things right, and hates truthfulness [when it is] for corruption.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> في الصلاح may mean 'in reconciliation (between people)' or more broadly 'for a good end'. Likewise في الفساد may mean 'in sowing discord' or 'for an evil end'. The past-tense verbs أحبّ/أبغض express a standing attribute, so they are rendered in the present. The editor's footnote explains this as a case of conflicting obligations, where the weightier one takes precedence.</sub><br><sub>p. 295</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من ترك الخمر لغير الله سقاه الله من الرحيق المختوم.<br>فقال عليّ (عليه السلام): لغير الله؟!<br>قال: نعم، والله صيانة لنفسه يشكره الله على ذلك.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever abandons wine for [a reason] other than Allah, Allah will give him to drink of the sealed nectar.<br>ʿAlī (a.s.) said: 'For other than Allah?!'<br>He said: 'Yes, by Allah. [If he gives it up] to protect himself, Allah will thank him for that.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> الرحيق المختوم echoes Q 83:25 (يُسقون من رحيق مختوم, 'they will be given to drink of a sealed nectar'). The punctuation of the reply is ambiguous. I read نعم، والله as the oath 'Yes, by Allah', followed by صيانةً لنفسه ('out of protecting himself'). Another reading is 'Yes; and Allah will thank him for that, [since he did it] to protect himself'. The editor's footnote says صيانة لنفسه means guarding his honor, or guarding himself because wine harms him.</sub><br><sub>p. 296</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، شارب الخمر كعابد وثن.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the drinker of wine is like a worshipper of idols.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor's footnote says the likeness lies in the punishment, not in its measure.</sub><br><sub>p. 296</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، شارب الخمر لا يقبل الله عزّ وجلّ صلواته أربعين يوماً، فإن مات في الأربعين مات كافراً.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, does not accept the prayers of the wine-drinker for forty days, and if he dies within those forty [days], he dies a disbeliever.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor's footnote glosses this as 'he dies like a disbeliever'. The author's own comment follows in the next segment.</sub><br><sub>p. 296</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">ـ قال مصنّف هذا الكتاب: يعني إذا كان مستحلاً لها ـ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">— The compiler of this book says: This means if he regarded it as lawful. —<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This is al-Ṣadūq's gloss on the preceding saying. The pronoun لها refers to wine (الخمر, feminine).</sub><br><sub>p. 296</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، كلّ مسكر حرام، وما أسكر كثيره فالجرعة منه حرام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, every intoxicant is forbidden, and whatever intoxicates in large quantity, a single mouthful of it is forbidden.<br><sub>p. 296</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، جُعلت الذنوب كلّها في بيت، وجعل مفاتحها شرب الخمر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, all sins have been placed in a house, and its keys have been made the drinking of wine.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> مفاتحها has a feminine pronoun, referring back to الذنوب ('the keys to them'), not to بيت. The Faqīh reads مفاتيحها (same meaning).</sub><br><sub>p. 296</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، يأتي على شارب الخمر ساعة لا يعرف فيها ربّه عزّ وجلّ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there comes upon the drinker of wine an hour in which he does not know his Lord, Mighty and Majestic.<br><sub>p. 296</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إنّ إزالة الجبال الرواسي أهون من إزالة ملك موكّل لم تنقص أيامه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, removing firmly rooted mountains is easier than removing a reign that has been appointed [for a set term] whose days have not run out.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> ملك is unvocalized. It can be read مُلك ('a reign, dominion'), as translated, or مَلِك ('a king'). موكّل: 'entrusted, appointed'. The editor notes the Faqīh reading مؤجّل ('given a fixed term'), which supports the sense of a reign whose term has not expired. تنقص: the editor notes the Faqīh reads تنقض ('have not expired/ended'), which gives nearly the same meaning.</sub><br><sub>p. 296</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من لم ينتفع بدينه ولا دنياه فلا خير في مجالسته، ومن لم يوجب لك فلا توجب له ولا كرامة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there is no good in sitting with one who benefits neither from his religion nor from his worldly life. And whoever does not acknowledge your due, do not acknowledge his, and [show him] no honor.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Following the editor's footnote, يوجب لك means 'recognize your right and hold you in esteem'. Literally: 'whoever does not make [anything] obligatory for you, do not make [anything] obligatory for him'. ولا كرامة is idiomatic ('and no honor [is due to him]'). The editor notes the Faqīh/Biḥār reading تنتفع بدينه ولا دنياه فلا خير لك ('from whose religion and worldly life you do not benefit, there is no good for you [in sitting with him]'). That reading may be the more natural sense.</sub><br><sub>p. 297</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ينبغي أن يكون في المؤمن ثمان خصال: وقارٌ عند الهزاهز، وصبر عند البلاء، وشكر عند الرخاء، وقنوع بما رزقه الله عزّ وجلّ، لا يظلم الأعداء، ولا يتحامل على الأصدقاء، بدنه منه في تعب، والناس منه في راحة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the believer should have eight qualities: composure in times of upheaval, patience in affliction, gratitude in times of ease, and contentment with what Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has provided him. He does not wrong his enemies, and he does not impose unfairly on his friends. His body is wearied by him, while people are at ease from him.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> الهزاهز: the editor glosses it as trials that throw people into turmoil. يتحامل على: the editor glosses it as 'to act unjustly toward someone, or burden him beyond his capacity'. The text lists eight qualities, but the last four are verbal and nominal clauses rather than nouns.</sub><br><sub>p. 297</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أربعة لا تردّ لهم دعوة: إمام عادل، ووالد لولده، والرجل يدعو لأخيه بظهر الغيب، والمظلوم، يقول الله عزّ وجلّ: وعزّتي وجلالي لأنتصرنّ لك ولو بعد حين.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there are four whose supplication is not turned back: a just leader, a father [praying] for his child, a man who prays for his brother in his absence, and the wronged one, [to whom] Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says: 'By My might and My majesty, I will surely avenge you, even if after a while.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> إمام عادل: 'just ruler/leader'. The generic word imam here need not mean one of the Twelve Imams. بظهر الغيب: without the other's knowledge, behind his back. لأنتصرنّ لك: 'I will surely take your side / exact justice for you'.</sub><br><sub>p. 297</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثمانية إن أهينوا فلا يلومنّ إلاّ أنفسهم: الذاهب إلى مائدة لم يدع إليها، والمتأمّر على ربّ البيت، وطالب الخير من أعدائه، وطالب الفضل من اللئام، والداخل بين اثنين في سرٍّ لم يُدخلاه فيه، والمستخفّ بالسلطان، والجالس في مجلس ليس له بأهل، والمقبل بالحديث على من لا يسمع منه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there are eight who, if they are humiliated, should blame none but themselves: one who goes to a meal he was not invited to; one who gives orders to the master of the house; one who seeks good from his enemies; one who seeks favor from the base; one who intrudes between two people in a secret they did not include him in; one who treats the ruler with contempt; one who sits in a gathering he is not fit for; and one who turns to speak to someone who will not listen to him.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor notes the Faqīh/Biḥār reading في سرٍّ against the base text's اثنين وستر. ليس له بأهل: 'for which he is not qualified / not of its rank'.</sub><br><sub>p. 297</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، حرّم الله الجنّة على كلّ فحّاش بذيّ لا يبالي ما قال، ولا ما قيل له.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, Allah has forbidden Paradise to every foul-mouthed, obscene person who does not care what he says or what is said to him.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The Faqīh reads فاحش for فحّاش (same meaning, less intensive).</sub><br><sub>p. 297</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، طوبى لمن طال عمره وحسن عمله.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, blessed is the one whose life is long and whose deeds are good.<br><sub>p. 297</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لا تمزح، فيذهب بهاؤك، ولا تكذب فيذهب نورك، وإيّاك وخصلتين: الضجر والكسل، فإنّك إن ضجرت لم تصبر على حقّ، وإن كسلت لم تؤدّه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, do not joke, or your dignity will depart; and do not lie, or your light will depart. Beware of two traits, irritability and laziness: if you are irritable you will not be patient with a right [duty], and if you are lazy you will not fulfill it.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This saying continues onto p. 298 (وخصلتين ... تؤدّه). الضجر: restlessness, irritability, impatience or boredom. حقّ: a right, a duty, or the truth; the Faqīh/Biḥār reading لم تؤدّ حقّاً ('you will not fulfill a right') supports 'a right/duty'. The negative imperative لا تمزح may mean 'do not joke [excessively]'; the text itself has no qualifier.</sub><br><sub>p. 297</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لكلّ ذنب توبة إلا سوء الخلق، فإنّ صاحبه كلّما خرج من ذنب، دخل في ذنب آخر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, for every sin there is repentance except bad character, for whenever the one who has it gets out of one sin, he enters into another.<br><sub>p. 298</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أربعة أسرع شي عقوبة: رجل أحسنت إليه فكافأك بالإحسان إساءةً، ورجل لا تبغي عليه وهو يبغي عليك، ورجل عاهدته على أمر فوفيت له وغدر بك، ورجل وصل قرابته فقطعوه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, four [cases] bring punishment faster than anything: a man to whom you did good and who repaid your kindness with harm; a man whom you do not transgress against while he transgresses against you; a man with whom you made a pact over some matter, and you kept faith with him while he betrayed you; and a man who kept ties with his relatives while they cut him off.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> شي is printed without hamza (for شيء). Each item names a situation, and the swift punishment falls on the wrongdoer in it (the ingrate, the aggressor, the betrayer, the relatives who cut ties), not on the man named. Literally: 'Four are the swiftest of things in punishment'.</sub><br><sub>p. 298</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من استولى عليه الضجر رحلت عنه الراحة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever is overcome by irritability, ease departs from him.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> الضجر: restlessness, impatience, irritability.</sub><br><sub>p. 298</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، اثني عشرة خصلة ينبغي للرجل المسلم أن يتعلّمها على المائدة: أربع منها فريضة، وأربع منها سنة، وأربع منها أدب.<br>فأمّا الفريضة: فالمعرفة بما يأكل، والتسمية، والشكر، والرضا.<br>وأمّا السنّة: فالجلوس على الرجل اليسرى، والأكل بثلاث أصابع، وأن يأكل ممّا يليه، ومص الأصابع.<br>وأمّا الأدب: فتصغير اللقمة، والمضغ الشديد، وقلّة النظر في وجوه الناس، وغسل اليدين.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there are twelve practices that a Muslim man should learn [to observe] at the table: four of them are obligatory, four are sunna (the Prophet's recommended practice), and four are good manners.<br>As for the obligatory: knowing what he is eating, saying the name [of Allah], giving thanks, and being content.<br>As for the sunna: sitting on the left leg, eating with three fingers, eating from what is in front of him, and licking the fingers.<br>As for good manners: taking small mouthfuls, chewing thoroughly, looking little at people's faces, and washing the hands.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> اثني عشرة is grammatically irregular (expected اثنتا عشرة), printed as is. سنة in the first line is printed without shadda; السنّة later has it. المعرفة بما يأكل: 'knowing what he eats', i.e. whether it is lawful and where it comes from; it could also mean recognizing Allah as the provider of the food. الرضا: contentment with what is provided.</sub><br><sub>p. 298</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، خلق الله عزّ وجلّ الجنّة من لبنتين، لبنة من ذهب ولبنة من فضّة، وجعل حيطانها الياقوت، وسقفها الزبرجد، وحصاها اللؤلؤ، وترابها الزعفران والمسك [الأذفر]، ثم قال لها: تكلّمي، فقالت: لا إله إلاّ الله الحيّ القيوم، قد سعد من يدخلني.<br>قال الله جلّ جلاله: وعزّتي وجلالي لا يدخلها مدمن خمر، ولا نمّام، ولا ديّوث، ولا شرطيّ، ولا مخنّث، ولا نبّاش، ولا عشّار، ولا قاطع رحم، ولا قدريّ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, created Paradise from two bricks, a brick of gold and a brick of silver. He made its walls of rubies, its roof of chrysolite, its pebbles of pearls, and its soil of saffron and [pungent] musk. Then He said to it: 'Speak.' It said: 'There is no god but Allah, the Ever-Living, the Self-Subsisting. Whoever enters me has attained felicity.'<br>Allah, majestic is His majesty, said: 'By My might and My majesty, none shall enter it who is addicted to wine, nor a talebearer, nor a dayyūth (a man who tolerates indecency among the women of his household), nor a shurṭī (an enforcer in the ruler's police), nor a mukhannath (an effeminate man), nor a grave-robber, nor an ʿashshār (an unjust collector of tithes/tolls), nor one who severs ties of kinship, nor a Qadarī.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This saying runs across pp. 298–299. [الأذفر] is in brackets in the edition; the editor's footnote says it was supplied from the Faqīh (أذفر: musk with a strong, spreading scent). الزبرجد is often rendered 'chrysolite' or 'emerald/peridot'. The editor's footnotes: الشرطي is one of the ruler's agents (police), singled out for their oppression of people; العشّار is one who takes a tenth from people's goods by the ruler's command. قدريّ: the editor's long footnote discusses the term. It was used polemically in both directions: for those who deny divine decree (the Muʿtazilite view of human autonomy) and for those who hold absolute divine compulsion. Imami reports apply it to both the determinist (jabrī) and the delegationist (tafwīḍī). I left the term transliterated so it does not take a side.</sub><br><sub>p. 298</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، كفر بالله العظيم من هذه الأمّة عشرة: القتّات، والساحر، والديّوث، وناكح المرأة حراماً في دبرها، وناكح البهيمة، ومَن نكح ذات محرم، والساعي في الفتنة، وبائع السلاح من أهل الحرب، ومانع الزكاة، ومن وجد سعة فمات ولم يحجّ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, ten of this community have disbelieved in Allah the Almighty: the talebearer; the sorcerer; the dayyūth; the one who has anal intercourse with a woman unlawfully; the one who has intercourse with an animal; the one who has intercourse with a woman within the forbidden degrees of kinship; the one who stirs up sedition; the one who sells weapons to the people at war [with the Muslims]; the one who withholds zakat (obligatory alms); and the one who had the means but died without performing the hajj (pilgrimage).<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This saying continues onto p. 301 (والساعي في الفتنة ... ولم يحجّ); I completed it from that page and stopped. القتّات: the editor glosses it as نمّام (talebearer), or one who eavesdrops on people's conversations without their knowledge. حراماً may mean 'unlawfully', i.e. a woman who is not his wife. It may also qualify the act itself as forbidden, which would include his own wife; the edition does not settle this. بائع السلاح من أهل الحرب: من is read as 'to' (selling to hostile belligerents). A less likely reading is 'one of the people at war who sells weapons'. ذات محرم: a woman he is permanently forbidden to marry. كفر ('has disbelieved') is probably meant as a severe expression, i.e. committing a sin akin to disbelief, rather than formal apostasy in every case.</sub><br><sub>p. 300</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لا وليمة إلاّ في خمسة: في عرسٍ، وخرس، وعذار، ووكارٍ، وركاز؛ فالعرس التزويج، والخرس النفاس بالولد، والعذار الختان، والوكار في شراء الدار، والركاز يقدم من مكّة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there is no walīma (celebratory feast) except on five [occasions]: for an ʿurs, a khurs, an ʿidhār, a wakār, and a rikāz. The ʿurs is marriage; the khurs is childbirth (the confinement upon delivering a child); the ʿidhār is circumcision; the wakār is on buying a house; and the rikāz is [for one who] arrives back from Mecca.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Page 301 opens with the tail of a list continued from p.300 ('والساعي في الفتنة ... فمات ولم يحجّ'), skipped per boundary rule. 'والركاز يقدم من مكّة' is elliptical, literally 'and the rikāz: he arrives from Mecca', i.e. the feast held when a pilgrim returns. A footnote marker sits between 'و' and 'خرس' in print; dropped.</sub><br><sub>p. 301</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">قال مصنّف هذا الكتاب: سمعت بعض أهل اللغة يقول معنى الوكار: يقال للطّعام الذي يدعى إليه الناس عند بناء الدار وشرائها، الوكيزة والوكاز منه، والطعام الذي يتّخذ للقدوم من السفر يقال له: النقيعة، ويقال له: الركاز أيضاً، والركاز: الغنيمة، كأنّه يريد أنّ في اتّخاذ الطعام للقدوم من مكّة غنيمة لصاحبه من الثواب الجزيل.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">The author of this book says: I heard one of the experts in the [Arabic] language say, regarding the meaning of wakār: the food to which people are invited upon building a house or buying it is called al-wakīza, and al-wakāz is from it. The food prepared for [someone's] arrival from a journey is called al-naqīʿa, and it is also called al-rikāz. Al-rikāz means booty (ghanīma); it is as though he means that preparing food upon arrival from Mecca brings its owner a gain in the form of abundant reward.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Printed 'الوكيزة والوكاز' (with zāy); the editor notes al-Faqīh has 'الوكيرة والوكار' (with rāʾ), which matches the word being explained (wakār). 'والوكاز منه' is terse: read as 'al-wakāz is [derived] from it'; alternatively 'al-wakīza, and al-wakāz [is a form] of it'. 'لصاحبه' = for the one who holds the feast.</sub><br><sub>p. 301</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">ومنه قول النبي (صلى الله عليه وآله): الصوم في الشتاء الغنيمة المباركة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And from this [sense of the word] is the saying of the Prophet (s.a.w.): Fasting in winter is the blessed booty.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The author continues his lexical note: ghanīma (booty, gain) in the sense just explained. Printed as its own paragraph.</sub><br><sub>p. 301</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لا ينبغي للعاقل أن يكون ظاعناً إلاّ في ثلاث: مرمّة لمعاش، أو تزوّد لمعاد، أو لذّة في غير محرّم.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, it is not fitting for the intelligent person to be journeying except for three [purposes]: mending [his] livelihood, or gathering provision for the Return (the Hereafter), or a pleasure in what is not forbidden.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> ẓāʿin = one who sets out on a journey (the editor glosses it as rāḥil). maʿād = the Return, i.e. the afterlife.</sub><br><sub>p. 301</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثلاث من مكارم الأخلاق في الدنيا والآخرة: أن تعفو عمّن ظلمك، وتصل من قطعك، وتحلم عمّن جهل عليك.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, three things are among the noblest traits of character in this world and the Hereafter: that you pardon whoever wronged you, maintain ties with whoever cut you off, and are forbearing toward whoever behaves ignorantly (rudely) toward you.<br><sub>p. 301</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، بادر بأربع قبل أربع: شبابك قبل هرمك، وصحّتك قبل سقمك، وغناك قبل فقرك، وحياتك قبل موتك.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, hasten [to make use of] four before four: your youth before your old age, your health before your sickness, your wealth before your poverty, and your life before your death.<br><sub>p. 302</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، كره الله عزّ وجلّ لأمّتي العبث في الصلاة، والمنّ في الصدقة، وإتيان المساجد جنباً، والضحك بين القبور، والتطلّع في الدور، والنظر إلى فرج النساء لأنّه يورث العمى.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has disliked for my community: fidgeting (idle play) during prayer; reminding [the recipient] of one's favour in giving charity; coming to the mosques in a state of janāba (major ritual impurity); laughing among the graves; peering into [people's] houses; and looking at the private parts of women, for it causes blindness.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor: al-Faqīh reads 'فروج' (plural). 'المنّ في الصدقة' = making the recipient feel the favour, reproaching him with it.</sub><br><sub>p. 302</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره الكلام عند الجماع لأنّه يورث الخرس.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked speaking during sexual intercourse, for it causes muteness.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This and each following 'وكره' line continues 'Allah has disliked [for my community]' from the previous saying. The unvowelled 'كره' could also be read as passive (kuriha, 'it is disliked'). The text does not say who is made mute (the speaker or the child conceived).</sub><br><sub>p. 302</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره النوم بين العشائين، لأنّه يحرم الرزق.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked sleeping between the two evening prayers (maghrib and ʿishāʾ), for it deprives [one] of provision.<br><sub>p. 302</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره الغسل تحت السماء إلاّ بمئزر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked bathing under the open sky except with a waist-wrap.<br><sub>p. 302</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره دخول الأنهار إلاّ بمئزر، فإنّ فيها سكّاناً من الملائكة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked entering rivers except with a waist-wrap, for there are inhabitants in them from among the angels.<br><sub>p. 302</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره دخول الحمّام إلاّ بمئزر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked entering the bathhouse except with a waist-wrap.<br><sub>p. 302</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره الكلام بين الأذان والإقامة في صلاة الغداة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked talking between the adhān (call to prayer) and the iqāma (call to begin the prayer) in the dawn prayer.<br><sub>p. 302</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره ركوب البحر في وقت هيجانه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked sailing the sea when it is stormy.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Literally 'riding the sea at the time of its agitation'.</sub><br><sub>p. 302</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره النوم في سطح ليس بمحجّر وقال: من نام على سطح غير محجّر، فقد برئت منه الذمّة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked sleeping on a roof that has no parapet. And he said: Whoever sleeps on a roof without a parapet, [Allah's] protection is quit of him.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'وقال' presumably refers to the Prophet, who is speaking the counsel. 'برئت منه الذمّة' = he forfeits protection, no one is answerable for him. The editor supplies nothing here, so 'Allah's' is my addition in brackets.</sub><br><sub>p. 302</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره أن ينام الرجل في بيت وحده.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked that a man sleep in a house alone.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> bayt can also mean a room.</sub><br><sub>p. 302</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره أن يغشى الرجل إمرأته وهي حائض، فإن فعل وخرج الولد مجنون أو به برص فلا يلومنّ إلا نفسه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked that a man approach (have intercourse with) his wife while she is menstruating; if he does so and the child is born insane or with baraṣ (a skin disease, vitiligo or leprosy), let him blame none but himself.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Printed 'مجنون' without the accusative ending (grammatically مجنوناً; verified at high zoom). Editor: al-Faqīh reads 'مجذوماً' (a leper). 'إلا' is printed here without shadda and 'إمرأته' with hamza, both as in the print.</sub><br><sub>p. 302</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره أن يتكلّم الرجل مجذوماً إلاّ [أن] يكون بينه وبينه قدر ذراع.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked that a man speak with a leper unless there is [a distance of] a cubit's length between them.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Printed 'يتكلّم'; the editor notes al-Faqīh has 'يكلّم', which fits the accusative 'مجذوماً'. '[أن]' is supplied by the editor from al-Faqīh. Literally 'between him and him'.</sub><br><sub>p. 302</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وقال (عليه السلام): فرّ من المجذوم كفرارك من الأسد.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And he (a.s.) said: Flee from the leper as you would flee from a lion.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> At high zoom this ligature has the (عليه السلام) form. It differs from the ligature used for the Prophet on p.301. The speaker is presumably still the Prophet. The honorific is transcribed as printed. Editor: al-Faqīh reads 'فرارك'.</sub><br><sub>p. 303</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره أن يأتي الرجل أهله وقد احتلم حتى يغتسل من الاحتلام، فإن فعل ذلك وخرج الولد مجنوناً فلا يلومنّ إلاّ نفسه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked that a man come to his wife after he has had a wet dream, until he has performed ghusl (the ritual bath) for the wet dream; if he does that and the child is born insane, let him blame none but himself.<br><sub>p. 303</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره البول على شطّ نهر جارٍ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked urinating on the bank of a flowing river.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor glosses this as 'beside it while it is flowing'.</sub><br><sub>p. 303</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره أن يحدث الرجل تحت شجرة أو نخلة قد أثمرت.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked that a man relieve himself beneath a tree or a date palm that has borne fruit.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> yuḥdith = to relieve oneself (commit ḥadath); in this context excretion.</sub><br><sub>p. 303</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره أن يحدث الرجل وهو قائم.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked that a man relieve himself while standing.<br><sub>p. 303</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[وكره أن يتنعّل الرجل وهو قائم].</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[And He disliked that a man put on his sandals while standing.]<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The whole line is bracketed in print. The editor supplied it from al-Faqīh.</sub><br><sub>p. 303</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وكره أن يدخل الرجل بيتاً مظلماً إلاّ مع السّراج.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And He disliked that a man enter a dark house except with a lamp.<br><sub>p. 303</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، آفة الحسب الافتخار.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the bane of noble standing (ḥasab) is boasting.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> ḥasab = honour of lineage or merit.</sub><br><sub>p. 303</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من خاف الله عزّ وجلّ أخاف منه كلّ شي، ومن لم يخف الله عزّوجلّ أخافه من كلّ شي.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever fears Allah, Mighty and Majestic, [Allah] makes everything fear him; and whoever does not fear Allah, Mighty and Majestic, [Allah] makes him fear everything.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'شي' is printed without hamza, and the second 'عزّوجلّ' is printed joined. Editor: al-Faqīh reads 'خاف' for 'أخاف' ('everything fears him').</sub><br><sub>p. 303</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثمانية لا يقبل الله منهم الصلاة: العبد الآبق حتّى يرجع إلى مولاه، والناشز وزوجها عليها ساخط، ومانع الزكاة، وتارك الوضوء، والجارية المدركة تصلّي بغير خمار، وإمام قوم يصلّي بهم وهم له كارهون، والسكران والزنين ـ وهو الذي يدافع البول والغائط ـ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there are eight from whom Allah does not accept prayer: the runaway slave, until he returns to his master; the recalcitrant wife (nāshiz) whose husband is angry with her; the one who withholds zakat (obligatory alms); the one who neglects wuḍūʾ (ritual ablution); the girl who has reached maturity and prays without a head-covering (khimār); the leader of a people who leads them in prayer while they dislike him; the drunkard; and the zanīn, that is, one who holds back urine and excrement.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor: al-Faqīh reads 'الزبين' (with bāʾ), with the same meaning; the reading with nūn is the well-known one. The eight items count correctly.</sub><br><sub>p. 303</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أربع من كنّ فيه بنى الله تعالى له بيتاً في الجنّة: مَن آوى اليتيم، ورحم الضعيف، وأشفق على والديه، ورفق بمملوكه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever has four [traits], Allah the Exalted builds for him a house in Paradise: whoever shelters the orphan, shows mercy to the weak, is tender toward his parents, and is gentle with his slave.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Starts at the foot of p.303 and ends at the top of p.304.</sub><br><sub>p. 303</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثلاث من لقى الله عزّ وجلّ بهنّ فهو [من] أفضل الناس: مَن أتى الله بما افترض عليه فهو من أعبد الناس، ومَن ورع عن محارم الله عزّ وجلّ، فهو من أورع الناس، ومَن قنع بما رزقه الله، فهو من أغنى الناس.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever meets Allah, Mighty and Majestic, with three [traits] is [among] the best of people: whoever comes to Allah having done what He made obligatory upon him is among the most devoted in worship; whoever refrains from what Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has forbidden is among the most scrupulous of people; and whoever is content with what Allah has provided him is among the richest of people.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> '[من]' is supplied by the editor from al-Faqīh. 'لقى' is spelled thus in print.</sub><br><sub>p. 304</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثلاث لا تطيقها هذه الأمّة: المواساة للأخ في ماله، وإنصاف الناس من نفسه، وذكر الله على كلّ حال، وليس هو سبحان الله والحمد لله ولا إله إلا الله والله أكبر، ولكن إذا ورد على ما يحرم عليه خاف الله عزّ وجلّ عنده وتركه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there are three things this community is unable to bear: sharing one's wealth with one's brother; giving people their due from oneself; and remembering Allah in every state. And this is not [merely saying] 'Glory be to Allah, praise be to Allah, there is no god but Allah, and Allah is the Greatest'; rather, when he comes upon something forbidden to him, he fears Allah, Mighty and Majestic, at that moment and leaves it.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'المواساة للأخ في ماله' is literally 'sharing with the brother in his wealth'. The pronoun most likely refers to the one who shares, i.e. his own wealth. 'إنصاف الناس من نفسه' = treating people justly even against oneself.</sub><br><sub>p. 304</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثلاثة إن أنصفتهم ظلموك: السفلة وأهلك وخادمك.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there are three who wrong you if you treat them fairly: the base (lowly) people, your family, and your servant.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor's footnote explains that this describes how such people behave and does not permit abandoning fairness toward them. The point is to be patient with them.</sub><br><sub>p. 304</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">ثلاثة لا ينتصفون من ثلاثة: حرّ من عبد، وعالم من جاهل، وقويّ من ضعيف.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Three cannot obtain their due from three: a free man from a slave, a learned man from an ignorant one, and a strong man from a weak one.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Printed as a separate paragraph without 'يا عليّ'. 'لا ينتصفون من' = cannot exact fair treatment from.</sub><br><sub>p. 304</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، سبعة من كنّ فيه فقد استكمل حقيقة الايمان وأبواب الجنّة مفتّحة له: مَن أسبغ وضوءه، وأحسن صلاته، وأدّى زكاة ماله، وكفّ غضبه، وسجن لسانه، واستغفر الله لذنبه، وأدّت النصيحة لأهل بيت نبيّه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever has seven [traits] has perfected the reality of faith, and the gates of Paradise are open to him: whoever performs his wuḍūʾ thoroughly, performs his prayer well, pays the zakat on his wealth, restrains his anger, imprisons his tongue, seeks Allah's forgiveness for his sin, and fulfils sincere counsel (loyal goodwill) toward the Household of his Prophet.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The print clearly reads 'وأدّت' (feminine, verified at high zoom). That does not agree with the masculine 'مَن', so it is probably a misprint for 'وأدّى', and I translated it that way. Editor: 'الله' after 'استغفر' is absent in al-Faqīh. 'الايمان' is printed without hamza.</sub><br><sub>p. 304</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لعن الله ثلاثة: آكل زاده وحده، وراكب الفلاة وحده، والنائم في بيت وحده.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, Allah has cursed three: the one who eats his provisions alone, the one who rides through the open desert alone, and the one who sleeps in a house alone.<br><sub>p. 304</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثلاث يتخوّف منهنّ الجنون: التغوّط بين القبور، والمشي في خفّ واحد، والرجل ينام وحده.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there are three things from which insanity is feared: defecating among the graves, walking in a single khuff (leather boot), and a man sleeping alone.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Starts at the foot of p.304 and ends at the top of p.305.</sub><br><sub>p. 304</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثلاثة يحسن فيهنّ الكذب: المكيدة في الحرب، وعدتك زوجتك، والإصلاح بين الناس.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there are three [situations] in which lying is good: stratagem in war, your promise to your wife, and making peace between people.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'عدتك زوجتك' = your making a promise to your wife.</sub><br><sub>p. 305</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وثلاثة مجالستهم تميت القلب: مجالسة الأنذال، ومجالسة الأغنياء، والحديث مع النساء.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And there are three whose company deadens the heart: sitting with the vile, sitting with the rich, and conversing with women.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor glosses 'الأنذال' as the base and contemptible, those fallen in religion or lineage.</sub><br><sub>p. 305</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثلاثة من حقائق الايمان: الإنفاق من الإقتار، وإنصافك الناس من نفسك، وبذل العلم للمتعلّم.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, three things are among the realities of faith: spending [on others] while in straitened means, giving people their due from yourself, and imparting knowledge freely to the learner.<br><sub>p. 305</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثلاث من لم يكن فيه لم يتمّ عمله: ورع يحجزه عن معاصي الله، وخلق يداري به الناس، وحلم يردّ به جهل الجهّال.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever lacks three [traits], his deeds are not complete: scrupulousness that holds him back from disobeying Allah, a character with which he treats people courteously, and forbearance with which he repels the ignorance of the ignorant.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor: al-Faqīh reads 'الجاهل' (singular).</sub><br><sub>p. 305</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثلاث فرحات للمؤمن في الدنيا: لقاء الإخوان، وتفطير الصائم، والتهجّد في آخر الليل.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there are three joys for the believer in this world: meeting his brethren, giving a fasting person food to break his fast, and keeping night vigil in prayer (tahajjud) in the last part of the night.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor: al-Faqīh reads 'من' for 'في'.</sub><br><sub>p. 305</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أنهاك عن ثلاث خصال: الحسد والحرص والكبر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, I forbid you three traits: envy, greed, and arrogance.<br><sub>p. 305</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أربع خصال من الشقاء: جمود العين، وقساوة القلب، وبُعد الأمل، وحبّ البقاء.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, four traits come from wretchedness: dryness of the eye (inability to weep), hardness of heart, far-reaching hopes, and love of remaining [in this world].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor: al-Faqīh reads 'الشقاوة'.</sub><br><sub>p. 305</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثلاث درجات، وثلاث كفّارات، وثلاث مهلكات، وثلاث منجيات؛</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there are three [things that raise one in] degrees, three expiations, three destroyers, and three [means of] salvation:<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Heading-like opening of one saying. The four explanatory paragraphs follow on p.306, and I have given each its own segment. 'درجات' = degrees of rank, understood as things that raise one's rank.</sub><br><sub>p. 305</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">فأمّا الدرجات: فإسباغ الوضوء في السبرات، وانتظار الصلاة بعد الصلاة، والمشي بالليل والنهار إلى الجماعات.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">As for the degrees: performing wuḍūʾ thoroughly in the bitter cold, waiting for [the next] prayer after the prayer, and walking by night and day to the congregational prayers.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor glosses al-sabarāt (plural of sabra) as intense cold, or alternatively the cold morning. Continues the saying begun on p.305.</sub><br><sub>p. 306</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وأما الكفّارات: فإفشاء السلام، وإطعام الطعام، والتهجّد في الليل والناس نيّام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">As for the expiations: spreading the greeting of peace, feeding [others], and keeping vigil in prayer at night while people are asleep.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'وأما' is printed here without shadda. Editor: al-Faqīh reads 'بالليل'.</sub><br><sub>p. 306</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وأمّا المهلكات: فشحّ مطاع، وهوى متّبع، وإعجاب المرء بنفسه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">As for the destroyers: avarice that is obeyed, desire that is followed, and a person's admiration of himself.<br><sub>p. 306</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وأمّا المنجيات: فخوف الله تعالى في السرّ والعلانية، والقصد في الغناء والفقر، وكلمة العدل في الرضا والسخط.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">As for the means of salvation: fear of Allah the Exalted in private and in public, moderation in wealth and in poverty, and a just word in [times of] contentment and anger.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'الغناء' is printed with hamza, but the pairing with 'الفقر' shows it means al-ghinā (wealth), not singing.</sub><br><sub>p. 306</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لا رضاع بعد فطام، ولا يتم بعد احتلام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there is no nursing after weaning, and no orphanhood after puberty.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> In legal terms: suckling after weaning has no effect in establishing foster kinship, and orphan status ends when the child reaches puberty (iḥtilām, lit. 'nocturnal emission').</sub><br><sub>p. 306</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، سر سنتين برّ والديك، سر سنة صل رحمك، سر ميلاً عد مريضاً، سر ميلين شيّع جنازة، سر ثلاث أميال أجب دعوة، سر أربعة أميال زر أخا في الله، سر خمسة أميال أجب الملهوف، سر ستّة أميال انصر المظلوم وعليك بالاستغفار.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, travel two years [to] be dutiful to your parents; travel a year [to] maintain ties with your kin; travel a mile [to] visit a sick person; travel two miles [to] escort a funeral; travel three miles [to] accept an invitation; travel four miles [to] visit a brother in Allah; travel five miles [to] answer the one crying for help; travel six miles [to] help the oppressed; and hold fast to seeking forgiveness.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor explains the first clause: if being dutiful to them requires covering a distance that takes two years, do it. 'ثلاث أميال' and 'أخا' (no tanwin) are as printed.</sub><br><sub>p. 306</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، للمؤمن ثلاث علامات: الصلاة، والزكاة، والصيام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the believer has three signs: prayer, zakat, and fasting.<br><sub>p. 306</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وللمتكلّف ثلاث علامات: يتملّق إذا حضر، ويغتاب إذا غاب، ويشمت بالمعصية.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And the pretender (mutakallif) has three signs: he flatters [a person] when he is present, backbites [him] when he is absent, and gloats over [another's] sin.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> mutakallif = one who affects qualities he does not have. The subject of 'حضر'/'غاب' is ambiguous: most naturally the other person, but it could be the mutakallif himself. Editor: al-Faqīh and Biḥār read 'بالمصيبة' ('gloats over [another's] misfortune'), which is the more natural collocation with 'يشمت'.</sub><br><sub>p. 306</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وللظّالم ثلاث علامات: يقهر من دونه بالغلبة، ومن فوقه بالمصيبة، ويظاهر الظلمة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And the oppressor has three signs: he subdues those beneath him by domination, and those above him by [causing] calamity, and he backs the oppressors.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor: al-Faqīh reads 'بالمعصية', i.e. he [overcomes] those above him by disobedience or defiance. This edition's 'بالمعصية'/'بالمصيبة' in these two adjacent sayings appear swapped relative to al-Faqīh. 'بالمصيبة' is the harder reading, and I rendered it as 'by causing calamity'.</sub><br><sub>p. 306</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وللمرائي ثلاث علامات: ينشط إذا كان عند الناس، ويكسل إذا كان وحده، ويحبّ أن يحمد في جميع أُموره.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And the show-off (one who acts to be seen) has three signs: he is energetic when he is among people, lazy when he is alone, and he loves to be praised in all his affairs.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Starts at the foot of p.306 and ends at the top of p.307.</sub><br><sub>p. 306</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وللمنافق ثلاث علامات: إذا حدّث كذب، وإذا وعد أخلف، وإذا أئتمن خان.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And the hypocrite has three signs: when he speaks he lies, when he promises he breaks his promise, and when he is entrusted he betrays.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> At high zoom the passive verb looks like 'أئتمن' (hamza on alif plus hamza on a tooth). The standard spelling is 'اؤتمن'. The meaning is not affected.</sub><br><sub>p. 307</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، تسعة أشياء تورث النسيان: أكل التفّاح الحامض، وأكل الكزبرة والجبن، وسؤر الفارة، وقراءة كتابة القبور، والمشي بين إمرأتين، وطرح القمّلة، والحجامة في النقرة، والبول في الماء الراكد.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, nine things cause forgetfulness: eating sour apples; eating coriander and cheese; [consuming] what a mouse has left over; reading the inscriptions on graves; walking between two women; throwing away a louse; cupping at the nape of the neck; and urinating into stagnant water.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor glosses al-kuzbura as coriander and al-nuqra as the hollow at the back of the neck. su'r = the leftover of food or drink that an animal has touched. 'الفارة' is printed without hamza. The nine items count correctly.</sub><br><sub>p. 307</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، العيش في ثلاثة: دار قوراء نوراء، وجارية حسناء، وفرس قبّاء.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, [good] living lies in three things: a spacious, bright house; a beautiful slave-girl; and a lean-bellied horse.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor glosses qawrāʾ as spacious, and notes 'نوراء' is absent in al-Faqīh. jāriya can mean a slave-girl or a young woman. The author explains qabbāʾ in the next paragraph.</sub><br><sub>p. 307</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يقول مصنّف هذا الكتاب: سمعت رجلاً من أهل المعرفة باللغة في الكوفة يقول: الفرس القباء: الضامر البطن؛ يقال: فرس أقبّ وقبّاء، لأنّ الفرس يذكّر ويؤنّث؛ ويقال للأُنثى: قبّاء لا غير.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">The author of this book says: I heard a man in Kufa who was learned in the [Arabic] language say: al-faras al-qabbāʾ is [the horse] with a lean belly. One says faras aqabb and [faras] qabbāʾ, because faras (horse) is treated as both masculine and feminine; for the female, only qabbāʾ is said.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The first 'القباء' is printed without shadda and the later 'قبّاء' with it; both are transcribed as printed.</sub><br><sub>p. 307</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">قال ذو الرمّة:<br>[تنصبت حوله يوماً تُراقبهُ ... صحرا سما حيج في أحشائها قبب</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Dhū al-Rumma said:<br>[They stood erect] around him one day, watching him, tawny, long-bodied ones, with leanness in their flanks.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'قال ذو الرمّة:' is at the foot of p.307; the verse line is the only body text on p.308. In print '[' opens before 'تنصبت' but no closing bracket is visible. The editor says the word is supplied from al-Faqīh. The two hemistichs are separated by a printed gap, rendered here as ' ... '. 'سما حيج' is printed with a gap but is one word, سماحيج, as the author's gloss on p.309 shows. 'صحرا' = ṣuḥran, plural of aṣḥar (tawny). The subject is wild she-asses gathered round a stallion. The editor's footnote says this line conflates two verses of the ode.</sub><br><sub>p. 307</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">والصحر: جمع أصحر؛ وهو الذي يضرب لونه في الحمرة، وهذا اللون يكون في حمار الوحش، والسماحيج: الطوال: واحدها سمحج. والقبب: الضمر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Al-ṣuḥr is the plural of aṣḥar, which is [an animal] whose colour tends toward red; this colour is found in the wild ass. Al-samāḥīj means the long ones; its singular is samḥaj. And al-qubab means leanness.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This completes the author's paragraph begun on p.307, read from p.309 under the boundary rule. The chunk stops here; the next saying on p.309 begins 'يا عليّ، والله لو أنّ المتواضع'. 'الضمر' could be ḍumr (leanness) or ḍummar (lean ones).</sub><br><sub>p. 309</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، والله لو أنّ المتواضع في قعر بئر لبعث الله عزّ وجلّ إليه ريحاً ترفعه فوق الأخيار في دولة الأشرار.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, by Allah, if the humble person were at the bottom of a well, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, would send him a wind that would raise him above the good, [even] in the reign of the wicked.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Page 309 opens with the tail of a lexical gloss from p.308 (والصحر: جمع أصحر ... والقبب: الضمر); skipped per boundary rule. Editor's note: the base manuscript and al-Faqīh read الوضيع ('the lowly'), which the editor treats as a scribal corruption and corrects to المتواضع. دولة الأشرار = 'the time when the wicked hold power'.</sub><br><sub>p. 309</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من انتمى إلى غير مواليه فعليه لعنة الله، ومن منع أجيراً أجره فعليه لعنة الله، ومن أحدث حدثاً أو آوى محدثاً فعليه لعنة الله.<br>فقيل: يا رسول الله، وما ذلك الحدث؟ قال: القتل.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever attaches himself to other than his own mawālī (patrons), upon him is the curse of Allah; whoever withholds from a hired worker his wage, upon him is the curse of Allah; and whoever commits a ḥadath (a grave violation) or shelters one who has committed a ḥadath, upon him is the curse of Allah.<br>It was said: O Messenger of Allah, and what is that ḥadath? He said: Killing.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> مواليه: may mean the masters who freed him (a freedman claiming another patron) or more broadly his own people/lineage. ḥadath literally 'something newly brought about'; the text itself glosses it here as killing (murder).</sub><br><sub>p. 309</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، المؤمن من أمنه المسلمون على أموالهم ودمائهم، والمسلم من سلم المسلمون من يده ولسانه، والمهاجر من هاجر السيئات.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the believer is the one whom the Muslims trust with their property and their lives; the Muslim is the one from whose hand and tongue the Muslims are safe; and the emigrant (muhājir) is the one who forsakes evil deeds.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> هاجر السيئات: lit. 'emigrates from / abandons evil deeds'. Editor notes al-Faqīh reads هجر (same sense).</sub><br><sub>p. 309</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أوثق عرى الإيمان الحبّ في الله، والبغض في الله.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the firmest of the handholds of faith is love for the sake of Allah and hatred for the sake of Allah.<br><sub>p. 309</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من أطاع إمرأته أكبّه الله عزّ وجلّ على وجهه في النّار.<br>فقال عليّ (عليه السلام): وما تلك الطاعة؟<br>قال: يأذن لها في الذهاب إلى الجماعات والعرسات والنائحات، ولبس الثياب الرقاق.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever obeys his wife, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will throw him on his face into the Fire.<br>ʿAlī (a.s.) said: And what is that obedience?<br>He said: That he permits her to go to gatherings, wedding feasts, and the wailing women (professional mourners), and to wear thin garments.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> إمرأته printed with hamza under the alif. النائحات = women who wail for the dead; i.e. going to their mourning gatherings. الثياب الرقاق = thin/sheer clothing. Editor notes al-Faqīh and al-Khiṣāl read الحمّامات ('bathhouses') in place of الجماعات ('gatherings').</sub><br><sub>p. 309</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إن الله تبارك وتعالى قد أذهب بالإسلام نخوة الجاهلية وتفاخرها بآبائها، ألا إنّ الناس من آدم، وآدم من تراب، وأكرمهم عند الله أتقاهم.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, Allah, Blessed and Exalted, has through Islam done away with the arrogance of the Jāhiliyya (the pre-Islamic age of ignorance) and its boasting of its forefathers. Indeed, people are from Adam, and Adam is from dust, and the most honoured of them in the sight of Allah is the most God-fearing of them.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The last clause echoes Q 49:13 (إن أكرمكم عند الله أتقاكم) but is not a verbatim quotation.</sub><br><sub>p. 309</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من السحت: ثمة الميتة، وثمن الكلب، وثمن الخمر، ومهر الزانية، والرشوة في الحكم، وأجر الكاهن.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, among suḥt (unlawful earnings) are: the price of carrion, the price of a dog, the price of wine, the fee of a prostitute, a bribe in judgment, and the wage of a soothsayer.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The print clearly reads ثمة الميتة, almost certainly a misprint for ثمن الميتة ('the price of carrion', parallel to the following items); translated as ثمن. مهر الزانية lit. 'the dower of the fornicating woman', i.e. payment for illicit intercourse.</sub><br><sub>p. 310</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من تعلّم علماً ليماري به السفهاء، أو يجادل به العلماء، أو يدعو الناس إلى نفسه، فهو من أهل النار.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever learns knowledge in order to wrangle with the foolish by it, or to argue with the scholars by it, or to call people to himself, is among the people of the Fire.<br><sub>p. 310</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إذا مات العبد قال الناس: ما خلّف؟ وقالت الملائكة: ما قدّم؟</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, when a servant [of Allah] dies, people say: What did he leave behind? And the angels say: What did he send ahead?<br><sub>p. 310</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، الدنيا سجن المؤمن وجنّة الكافر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, this world is the prison of the believer and the paradise of the disbeliever.<br><sub>p. 310</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، موت الفجأة راحة للمؤمن وحسرة للكافر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, sudden death is a relief for the believer and a cause of anguish for the disbeliever.<br><sub>p. 310</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أوحى الله تبارك وتعالى إلى الدنيا، أخدمي من خدمني، وأتعبي من خدمك.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, Allah, Blessed and Exalted, revealed to the world: Serve whoever serves Me, and wear out whoever serves you.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> أخدمي printed with hamza on the alif. أتعبي = 'exhaust, weary'.</sub><br><sub>p. 310</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إن الدنيا لو عدلت عند الله تبارك وتعالى جناح بعوضة لما سقى الكافر منها شربة ماء.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, if this world were worth as much as a gnat's wing in the sight of Allah, Blessed and Exalted, He would not have given the disbeliever a single drink of water from it.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor notes al-Faqīh reads شربة من ماء (same meaning).</sub><br><sub>p. 310</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ما أحد من الأوّلين والآخرين إلاّ وهو يتمنّى يوم القيامة إنّه لم يعط من الدنيا إلاّ قوتاً.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there is no one among the first and the last but will wish on the Day of Resurrection that he had been given nothing of this world except bare sustenance.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Printed إنّه (with kasra-hamza) where أنّه is grammatically expected; kept as printed, meaning unaffected. قوت = just enough food to live on.</sub><br><sub>p. 310</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، شرّ الناس من اتّهم الله في قضائه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the worst of people is the one who holds Allah suspect in His decree.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> اتّهم = to accuse or suspect, i.e. to doubt the justice or wisdom of what Allah has decreed.</sub><br><sub>p. 310</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا علي، أنين المؤمن تسبيح، وصياحه تهليل، ونومه على الفراش عبادة، وتقلّبه من جنب إلى جنب جهاد في سبيل الله، فإن عوفي مشى وما عليه من ذنب.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the groaning of the believer [in illness] is tasbīḥ (glorifying Allah), his crying out is tahlīl (declaring that there is no god but Allah), his sleep on his bed is worship, and his turning from side to side is jihad in the way of Allah. If he is then restored to health, he walks with no sin upon him.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> يا علي printed here without shadda. The illness is implied by فإن عوفي ('if he is restored to health'); '[in illness]' is supplied. Editor notes al-Faqīh reads مشى في الناس ('walks among the people').</sub><br><sub>p. 310</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لو أُهدي إليّ كراع لقبلت، ولو دعيت إلى كراع لأجبت.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, if a trotter were given to me as a gift I would accept it, and if I were invited to [a meal of] a trotter I would accept.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> كراع = the lower leg (shank/trotter) of a sheep or cow, a cheap cut; the point is humility. The editor also records a reading in which the second كراع is Kurāʿ al-Ghamīm, a place between Mecca and Medina, i.e. 'if I were invited [even from as far as] Kurāʿ al-Ghamīm'; and that Makārim al-Akhlāq and al-Biḥār read ذراع ('foreleg') in the second clause. Editor notes al-Faqīh reads لقبلته.</sub><br><sub>p. 310</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ليس على النساء جمعة ولا جماعة، ولا أذان ولا إقامة، ولا عيادة مريض، ولا اتّباع جنازة، ولا هرولة بين الصفا والمروة، ولا استلام الحجر، ولا حلق، ولا تولّي القضاء، ولا تستشار، ولا تذبح إلا عند الضرورة، ولا تجهر بالتلبية، ولا تقيم عند قبر، ولا تسمع الخطبة، ولا تتولّى التزويج بنفسها، ولا تخرج من بيت زوجها إلاّ بإذنه، فإن خرجت بغير إذنه لعنها الله وجبرئيل وميكائيل، ولا تعطي من بيت زوجها شيئاً إلاّ بإذنه، ولا تبيت وزوجها عليها ساخط وإن كان ظالماً لها.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, women are not required [to attend] the Friday prayer or the congregational prayer; nor [to give] the adhān (call to prayer) or the iqāma (call to stand for prayer); nor [to undertake] visiting the sick, following a funeral, the harwala (brisk walking) between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwa, touching the [Black] Stone, or shaving [the head in the pilgrimage]; nor [to take] the office of judge. She is not consulted; she does not slaughter except in necessity; she does not raise her voice in the talbiya (the pilgrim's cry); she does not stay by a grave; she does not listen to the sermon; she does not undertake the marriage contract herself; and she does not leave her husband's house except with his permission. If she leaves without his permission, Allah, Gabriel and Michael curse her. She does not give anything from her husband's house except with his permission, and she does not spend the night while her husband is angry with her, even if he is wronging her.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> From ولا تستشار on, the text switches from nouns governed by ليس على to verbs. Several are clearly indicative, not jussive (تتولّى, تعطي, تبيت; the jussive would be تتولَّ, تعطِ, تبِت), so they are normative negations ('she does not ...') rather than direct prohibitions; they could still be rendered 'she should not ...'. ولا تسمع الخطبة may be meant as 'she is not required to listen to the sermon'. ولا تقيم عند قبر may mean not staying/lingering at graves.</sub><br><sub>p. 311</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، الإسلام عريان ولباسه الحياء، وزينته الوقار، ومروّته العمل الصالح، وعماده الورع، ولكلّ شي أساس، وأساس الإسلام حبّنا أهل البيت.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, Islam is naked: its clothing is modesty, its adornment is dignified composure, its manliness (muruwwa) is righteous action, and its pillar is scrupulous piety (waraʿ). Everything has a foundation, and the foundation of Islam is love for us, the Ahl al-Bayt (the Household of the Prophet).<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> شي printed without final hamza. مروّة = manly virtue, honour. Editor notes al-Faqīh reads الوفاء ('loyalty') for الوقار.</sub><br><sub>p. 311</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، سوء الخلق شؤم، وطاعة المرأة ندامة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, bad character is an ill omen, and obeying a woman is regret.<br><sub>p. 311</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إن كان الشؤم في شي ففي لسان المرأة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, if ill omen is in anything, it is in a woman's tongue.<br><sub>p. 311</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، نجا المخفّون [وهلك المثقلون].</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the lightly burdened are saved [and the heavily burdened perish].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The bracketed clause was supplied by the editor from al-Faqīh and al-Biḥār. Lightly/heavily burdened = with little or much of this world, or of sins.</sub><br><sub>p. 311</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من كذب عليّ متعمّداً فليتبوّأ مقعده من النار.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever deliberately lies about me, let him take his seat in the Fire.<br><sub>p. 311</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثلاث يزدن في الحفظ، ويذهبن البلغم: اللبان والسواك وقراءة القرآن.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, three things increase memory and remove phlegm: frankincense, the siwāk (tooth-stick), and recitation of the Qur'an.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> اللبان = frankincense (the chewable resin).</sub><br><sub>p. 311</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، السواك من السنّة ومطهّر للفم، ويجلو البصر، ويرضي الرحمن، ويبيّض الأسنان، ويذهب بالحفر، ويشدّ اللثّة، ويشهّي الطعام، ويذهب بالبلغم، ويزيد في الحفظ، ويزاد الحسنات، وتفرح به الملائكة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the siwāk is part of the Sunna (the Prophet's practice) and a purifier of the mouth. It clears the eyesight, pleases the All-Merciful, whitens the teeth, removes decay, strengthens the gums, sharpens the appetite, removes phlegm, and increases memory; good deeds are increased [through it], and the angels rejoice in it.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Continues onto p.312 (from ويبيّض الأسنان). الحفر = decay/tartar on the teeth; the editor notes Makārim al-Akhlāq and al-Biḥār read البخر ('bad breath') and thinks that may be correct. ويزاد الحسنات is grammatically awkward as printed (a passive verb with an accusative-looking object; perhaps 'one is given more good deeds'); the editor notes al-Faqīh and al-Biḥār read يضاعف ('[good deeds] are multiplied').</sub><br><sub>p. 311</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، النوم أربعة: نوم الأنبياء (عليهم السلام) على أقفيتهم، ونوم المؤمنين على أيمانهم، ونوم الكفّار والمنافقين على أيسارهم، ونوم الشياطين على وجوههم.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, sleep is of four kinds: the prophets (a.s.) sleep on their backs, the believers sleep on their right sides, the disbelievers and hypocrites sleep on their left sides, and the devils sleep on their faces.<br><sub>p. 312</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ما بعث الله عزّ وجلّ نبيّاً إلا وجعل ذرّيته من صلبه، وجعل ذرّيتي من صلبك، ولولاك ما كانت لي ذرّيّة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has not sent any prophet without making his progeny from his own loins, but He has made my progeny from your loins; and were it not for you, I would have no progeny.<br><sub>p. 312</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أربعة من قواصم الظهر: إمام يعصي الله عزّ وجلّ ويطاع أمره، وزوجة يحفظها زوجها وهي تخونه، وفقر لا يجد صاحبه مداوياً، وجار سوء في دار المقام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, four things are among those that break the back: a leader (imām) who disobeys Allah, Mighty and Majestic, yet his command is obeyed; a wife whose husband guards her [honour] while she betrays him; poverty for which the one who suffers it finds no remedy; and a bad neighbour in one's permanent home.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> مداوياً = 'one who treats / a remedy'. دار المقام = the place of settled residence (as opposed to a place one is travelling through).</sub><br><sub>p. 312</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إنّ عبد المطّلب سنّ في الجاهلية خمس سنن وأجراها الله عزّوجلّ في الإسلام: حرّم نساء الآباء على الأبناء، فأنزل الله عزّ وجلّ: ﴿وَلاَ تَنكِحُوا مَا نَكَحَ آبَاؤُكُمْ مِنَ النِّسَاءِ﴾.<br>ووجد كنزاً فأخرج منه الخمس وتصدّق به، فأنزل الله عزّ وجلّ: ﴿وَاعْلَمُوا أَنَّمَا غَنِمْتُمْ مِنْ شَيٍ فَأَنَّ لِلَّهِ خُمُسَهُ وَلِلرَّسُولِ﴾ الآية.<br>ولمّا حفر بئر زمزم سمّاها سقاية الحاجّ، فأنزل الله تبارك وتعالى: ﴿أَجَعَلْتُمْ سِقَايَةَ الْحَاجِّ وَعِمَارَةَ الْمَسْجِدِ الْحَرَامِ كَمَنْ آمَنَ بِاللَّهِ وَالْيَوْمِ الآخِرِ﴾ الآية.<br>وسنّ في القتل مائة من الإبل، فأجرى الله عزّ وجلّ ذلك في الإسلام.<br>ولم يكن للطواف عدد عن قريش، فسنّ لهم عبد المطّلب سبعة أشواط، فأجرى الله عزّ وجلّ ذلك في الإسلام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib established five practices (sunan) in the Jāhiliyya, and Allah, Mighty and Majestic, made them continue in Islam. He forbade the wives of fathers to the sons, and Allah, Mighty and Majestic, sent down: {And do not marry the women whom your fathers married} [Q 4:22].<br>He found a treasure, took out one fifth (khums) of it and gave it in charity, and Allah, Mighty and Majestic, sent down: {And know that whatever you gain of anything, one fifth of it belongs to Allah and to the Messenger} [Q 8:41] — to the end of the verse.<br>When he dug the well of Zamzam, he named it 'the watering of the pilgrims', and Allah, Blessed and Exalted, sent down: {Do you make the watering of the pilgrims and the upkeep of the Sacred Mosque like [the deeds of] one who believes in Allah and the Last Day?} [Q 9:19] — to the end of the verse.<br>He set one hundred camels [as blood-money] for killing, and Allah, Mighty and Majestic, made that continue in Islam.<br>Quraysh had no set number [of circuits] for the ṭawāf (circumambulation of the Kaʿba), so ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib set seven circuits for them, and Allah, Mighty and Majestic, made that continue in Islam.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> One saying spanning pp.312–313 (the last two paragraphs, from وسنّ في القتل, are on p.313). The verses are quoted in partial form, as printed. In Q 8:41 the print appears to have شَيٍ (kasratan, no hamza); at this scan resolution the Quranic vowelling cannot be fully verified. عدد عن قريش: عن is odd; عند قريش ('among Quraysh') would be expected, and عن may be a misprint. The editor's footnote on Q 9:19 records a report that the verse was revealed about ʿAlī, al-ʿAbbās and Ṭalḥa/Shayba boasting; not part of the body text.</sub><br><sub>p. 312</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إنّ عبد المطلب كان لا يستقسم بالأزلام، ولا يعبد الأصنام، ولا يأكل ما ذبح على النصب، ويقول: أنا على دين أبي إبراهيم (عليه السلام).</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib did not seek divination with arrows (azlām), did not worship idols, and did not eat what was slaughtered on the stone altars (nuṣub), and he used to say: I follow the religion of my father Ibrāhīm (a.s.).<br><sub>p. 313</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أعجب الناس إيماناً وأعظمهم يقيناً قوم يكونون في آخر الزمان، لم يلحقوا النبيّ (صلى الله عليه وآله)، وحجب عنهم الحجّة فآمنوا بسواد على بياض.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the most wondrous of people in faith and the greatest of them in certainty are a people who will live at the end of time. They did not meet the Prophet (s.a.w.), and the Proof (al-ḥujja) was veiled from them, yet they believed in black on white.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'Black on white' = ink on paper, i.e. written texts. الحجّة here is read as the Imam as Allah's Proof, hidden from them (the Occultation). حجب is unvocalised: either passive ('was veiled') or active with Allah as implied subject ('He veiled the Proof from them').</sub><br><sub>p. 313</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثلاثة يقسين القلب: استماع اللهو، وطلب الصيد، وإتيان باب السلطان.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, three things harden the heart: listening to idle entertainment, going after game, and frequenting the door of the ruler.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Spans pp.313–314: the final words باب السلطان are the first line of p.314. اللهو = idle amusement (often understood as music). طلب الصيد = hunting (for sport).</sub><br><sub>p. 313</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لا تصلّ في جلد ما لا يشرب لبنه، ولا يؤكل لحمه، ولا تصلّ في ذات الجيش ولا في ذات الصلاصل ولا في ضجنان.</td>
