<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, do not pray in the skin of an animal whose milk is not drunk and whose flesh is not eaten; and do not pray at Dhāt al-Jaysh, nor at Dhāt al-Ṣalāṣil, nor at Ḍajnān.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Place names (per the editor's footnotes): Dhāt al-Jaysh is near Medina on the route to Badr; Dhāt al-Ṣalāṣil is a watering place of the Banū ʿAmr ibn Ḥanẓala; Ḍajnān is a mountain near Mecca. The editor adds that narrations describe all three as places where the earth swallows (khasf), and that the prohibition is read as one of disapproval (karāha). Editor notes al-Faqīh reads تشرب/تأكل ('you drink / you eat').</sub><br><sub>p. 314</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، كل من البيض ما اختلف طرفاه، ومن السمك ما كان له قشر، ومن الطير ما رفّ واترك منه ما صف، وكل من طير الماء ما كانت له قانصة أو صيصية.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, eat eggs whose two ends differ; fish that have scales; and birds that flap [their wings in flight], but leave those of them that glide. And eat water birds that have a gizzard or a spur.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> كل = imperative 'eat'. رفّ = beats its wings; صف (printed without shadda) = holds its wings outspread, gliding. Editor notes al-Faqīh reads ما دفّ (same sense: flaps). قانصة = gizzard; صيصية = the spur or hind toe on a bird's leg.</sub><br><sub>p. 314</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، كلّ ذي ناب من السّباع ومخلب من الطير فحرام لا تأكله.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, every beast of prey with a fang and every bird with a talon is unlawful; do not eat it.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor notes al-Faqīh reads فحرام أكله ('eating it is unlawful').</sub><br><sub>p. 314</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لا قطع في ثمر، ولا كنز.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, there is no amputation [of the hand for theft] of fruit, nor of kanz [؟].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> As printed, كنز = 'treasure', which makes little legal sense here. The editor notes al-Faqīh and al-Biḥār read كثر (kathar: the pith of the date palm, or its spathe), which fits the context (items taken from the tree). The likely intended meaning is 'nor of palm pith'; كنز is probably a corruption.</sub><br><sub>p. 315</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ليس على زان عقر، ولا حدّ في التعريض، ولا شفاعة في حدّ، ولا يمين في قطيعة رحم، ولا يمين ولد مع والده، ولا لامرأة مع زوجها، ولا لعبد مع مولاه، ولا صمت يوماً إلى الليل، ولا وصال في الصيام، ولا تعرّب بعد هجرة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, a fornicator owes no ʿuqr (compensation equal to a dower); there is no ḥadd (prescribed punishment) for insinuation; there is no intercession in [a case of] ḥadd; there is no [binding] oath to sever ties of kinship; there is no oath for a child against [the will of] his father, nor for a woman against [the will of] her husband, nor for a slave against [the will of] his master; there is no silence [as devotion] for a whole day until nightfall; there is no wiṣāl (joining fasts without breaking them) in fasting; and there is no reverting to desert life after hijra (emigration).<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> عقر = the payment owed for intercourse (e.g. in a doubtful marriage); here, none is owed for consensual fornication. التعريض = indirect or hinted accusation (as opposed to explicit qadhf), which carries no ḥadd. يمين ولد مع والده: the son's oath does not bind if his father objects (likewise the wife and the slave). تعرّب بعد هجرة = returning to Bedouin life, away from the community and religious learning, after having emigrated.</sub><br><sub>p. 315</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لا يقتل والد بولده.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, a father is not executed [in retaliation] for [killing] his child.<br><sub>p. 315</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لا يقبل الله تعالى دعاء قلب ساه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, Allah, the Exalted, does not accept the supplication of a heedless heart.<br><sub>p. 315</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، نوم العالم أفضل من عبادة العابد.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the sleep of the learned is better than the worship of the [unlearned] devotee.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> العالم = one with knowledge / scholar; العابد = one devoted to worship.</sub><br><sub>p. 315</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ركعتين يصلّيهما العالم أفضل من ألف ركعة يصلّيها العابد.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, two rakʿas (units of prayer) that the learned person prays are better than a thousand rakʿas that the devotee prays.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Printed ركعتين (accusative/genitive form) where the nominative ركعتان is grammatically expected; kept as printed.</sub><br><sub>p. 315</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لا تصوم المرأة تطوّعاً إلاّ بإذن زوجها، ولا يصوم العبد [تطوّعاً] إلاّ بإذن مولاه، ولا يصوم الضيف تطوّعاً إلاّ بإذن صاحبه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, a woman does not fast voluntarily except with her husband's permission, a slave does not fast [voluntarily] except with his master's permission, and a guest does not fast voluntarily except with his host's permission.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The bracketed تطوّعاً was supplied by the editor from al-Faqīh. The verbs are indicative (normative negation); they could equally be rendered 'should not fast'.</sub><br><sub>p. 315</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، صوم يوم الفطر حرام، وصوم يوم الأضحى حرام، [وصوم الوصال حرام،] وصوم الصمت حرام، نذر المعصية حرام، وصوم الدهر حرام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, fasting on the day of [ʿĪd al-]Fiṭr is unlawful, fasting on the day of [ʿĪd al-]Aḍḥā is unlawful, [the wiṣāl fast is unlawful,] the fast of silence is unlawful, a vow to [commit] a sin is unlawful, and perpetual fasting is unlawful.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The bracketed clause was supplied by the editor from al-Biḥār. نذر المعصية has no preceding و in the print; it may be a separate item ('a vow of sin') or a remnant of صوم نذر المعصية ('a fast vowed [in thanks] for a sin'). صوم الدهر = fasting every day of the year, including the forbidden days.</sub><br><sub>p. 315</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، في الزّنا ست خصال: ثلاث منها في الدنيا وثلاث منها في الآخرة؛ فأمّا التي في الدنيا فيذهب بالبهاء، ويعجّل الفناء، ويقطع الرزق.<br>وأمّا التي في الآخرة فسوء الحساب، وسخط الرحمن، والخلود في النار.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, in fornication there are six consequences: three of them in this world and three of them in the Hereafter. As for those in this world, it takes away radiance, hastens destruction, and cuts off provision.<br>As for those in the Hereafter, they are an evil reckoning, the displeasure of the All-Merciful, and remaining forever in the Fire.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> خصال lit. 'traits'. يعجّل الفناء = hastens death / ruin.</sub><br><sub>p. 315</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، الربا سبعون جزءاً فأيسره، مثل أن ينكح الرجل أمّه في بيت الله الحرام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, ribā (usury) has seventy parts, and the least of them is like a man having intercourse with his mother in the Sacred House of Allah.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The comma after فأيسره is as printed.</sub><br><sub>p. 315</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، درهم ربا أعظم عند الله عزّ وجلّ من سبعين زنية كلّها بذات محرم في بيت الله الحرام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, a single dirham of usury is graver in the sight of Allah, Mighty and Majestic, than seventy acts of fornication, all of them with a woman of prohibited kinship (dhāt maḥram), in the Sacred House of Allah.<br><sub>p. 315</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، مَن منع قيراطاً من زكاة ماله، فليس بمؤمن ولا بمسلم، ولا كرامة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever withholds a single qīrāṭ of the zakat (obligatory alms) due on his wealth is neither a believer nor a Muslim, and [he has] no honour.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> قيراط = a very small unit (a fraction of a dinar). ولا كرامة is an idiom of dismissal: 'and no respect [is due to him]'.</sub><br><sub>p. 316</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، تارك الزكاة يسأل الله الرجعة إلى الدنيا، وذلك قول الله عزّ وجلّ: ﴿حَتَّى إِذَا جَاءَ أَحَدَهُمُ الْمَوْتُ قَالَ رَبِّ ارْجِعُونِ﴾ الآية.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the one who abandons zakat will ask Allah to be returned to this world, and that is the word of Allah, Mighty and Majestic: {Until, when death comes to one of them, he says: My Lord, send me back} [Q 23:99] — to the end of the verse.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor notes al-Biḥār reads الصلاة ('prayer') for الزكاة.</sub><br><sub>p. 316</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، تارك الحجّ وهو مستطيع كافر، يقول الله تبارك وتعالى: ﴿وَلِلَّهِ عَلَى النَّاسِ حِجُّ الْبَيْتِ مَنْ اسْتَطَاعَ إِلَيْهِ سَبِيلاً وَمَنْ كَفَرَ فَإِنَّ اللَّهَ غَنِيٌّ عَنْ الْعَالَمِينَ﴾.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the one who abandons the hajj while able [to perform it] is a disbeliever. Allah, Blessed and Exalted, says: {And pilgrimage to the House is a duty owed to Allah by the people, for whoever is able to find a way to it; and whoever disbelieves — then indeed Allah is free of need of the worlds} [Q 3:97].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Quranic vowelling transcribed as it appears in the print (e.g. مَنْ اسْتَطَاعَ, عَنْ الْعَالَمِينَ with sukūn rather than the connecting vowel); it cannot be fully verified at this scan resolution.</sub><br><sub>p. 316</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، مَن سوّف الحجّ حتّى يموت، بعثه الله يوم القيامة يهوديّاً أو نصرانيّاً.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, whoever keeps putting off the hajj until he dies, Allah will raise him on the Day of Resurrection as a Jew or a Christian.<br><sub>p. 316</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، الصدقة تردّ البلاء الذي قد أبرم إبراما.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, charity turns back a calamity even when it has been firmly decreed.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> أبرم إبراما = 'sealed with finality', i.e. irrevocably decreed. Editor notes al-Faqīh reads القضاء ('the decree') for البلاء.</sub><br><sub>p. 316</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، صلة الرحم تزيد في العمر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, maintaining ties of kinship lengthens one's life.<br><sub>p. 316</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، افتتح بالملح واختم بالملح، فإنّ فيه شفاء من إثنين وسبعين داء.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, begin [your meal] with salt and end with salt, for in it is a cure for seventy-two ailments.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> إثنين printed with hamza under the alif.</sub><br><sub>p. 316</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لو قدمت على المقام المحمود لشفعت في أبي وعمّي وأمّي وأخ كان لي في الجاهليّة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, if I were to reach the Praiseworthy Station (al-maqām al-maḥmūd), I would intercede for my father, my uncle, my mother, and a brother I had in the Jāhiliyya.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> قدمت على = 'arrive at, come to'. Editor notes al-Faqīh reads قد قمت ('I have stood [at]'). The uncle and brother are not named in the text.</sub><br><sub>p. 316</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[يا عليّ، أنا ابن الذبيحين.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[O ʿAlī, I am the son of the two who were [to be] sacrificed.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The opening bracket here begins a bracketed passage that closes at the end of the ʿaql saying on p.317 (…وبك أعاقب]). The editor's note there says the whole passage is absent from al-Biḥār. The two are Ismāʿīl and the Prophet's father ʿAbd Allāh (per the editor's footnote).</sub><br><sub>p. 316</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أنا دعوة أبي إبراهيم.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, I am the [answered] prayer of my father Ibrāhīm.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor links this to Ibrāhīm's prayer {and grant me a truthful mention among later generations} [Q 26:84]. It is more commonly linked to Q 2:129 ('Our Lord, raise up among them a messenger from among them'). Part of the bracketed passage absent from al-Biḥār.</sub><br><sub>p. 316</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أحسن العقل ما اكتسب به الجنة وطلب به رضا الرحمن.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the best intellect is that by which Paradise is earned and the pleasure of the All-Merciful is sought.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor notes أحسن is absent from al-Faqīh ('intellect is that by which ...'). Part of the bracketed passage absent from al-Biḥār.</sub><br><sub>p. 316</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إنّ أوّل خلق خلقه الله عزّ وجلّ العقل، فقال له: «أقبل»، فأقبل. ثمّ قال له: «أدبر»، فأدبر. فقال: وعزّتي وجلالي ما خلقت خلقاً هو أحبّ إليّ منك، بك آخذ، وبك أعطي، وبك أثيب، وبك أعاقب].</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAlī, the first creation that Allah, Mighty and Majestic, created was the intellect (ʿaql). He said to it: 'Come forward,' and it came forward. Then He said to it: 'Turn back,' and it turned back. Then He said: By My might and My majesty, I have not created any creation more beloved to Me than you. By you I take, by you I give, by you I reward, and by you I punish.]<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Spans pp.316–317; completed from the first two lines of p.317 per the boundary rule, stopping there. The closing bracket ends the passage begun at أنا ابن الذبيحين; the editor's note says it is absent from al-Biḥār. بك آخذ may mean 'by you I take [people] to account' or 'by you I seize [punish]'; rendered literally.</sub><br><sub>p. 316</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لا صدقة وذو رحم محتاج.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, there is no charity while a relative is in need.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Page 317 opens with the tail of the previous narration (the hadith of the intellect: «قال له: أدبر، فأدبر...وبك أعاقب]»); skipped per boundary rule. Sense: charity to others is not proper (or not rewarded) while one's own needy kin are left unaided.</sub><br><sub>p. 317</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، درهم في الخضاب خيرٌ من ألف درهم ينفق في سبيل الله، وفيه أربع عشر خصلة: يطرد الريح من الأذنين، ويجلو البصر، ويلين الخياشيم، ويطيب النكهة، ويشدّ اللثّة، ويذهب بالضنى، ويقلّ وسوسة الشيطان، وتفرح به الملائكة، ويستبشر به المؤمن، ويغيظ به الكافر وهو زينته وطيبه، ويستحيي منه منكر ونكير، وهو براءة له في قبره.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, a dirham spent on khiḍāb (dyeing the hair and beard) is better than a thousand dirhams spent in the way of Allah. It has fourteen merits: it drives wind out of the ears, clears the eyesight, softens the nasal passages, sweetens the breath, strengthens the gums, removes wasting weakness, and lessens the whispering of Satan; the angels rejoice at it, the believer is gladdened by it, and the disbeliever is enraged by it; it is his adornment and his fragrance; Munkar and Nakīr (the two angels who question the dead) are abashed before him; and it is an acquittal for him in his grave.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> بالضنى: the editor's footnote says the base manuscript has بالصنان (a copyist's error), al-Kāfī has بالغثيان (nausea), and the printed reading is taken from al-Faqīh; الضنى = illness, emaciation, weakness. 'Fourteen' reckoned as: ears, sight, nasal passages, breath, gums, wasting, Satan's whispering, angels, believer, disbeliever, adornment, fragrance, Munkar and Nakīr, acquittal. ويستحيي منه may also be read 'are abashed before it' (the dye).</sub><br><sub>p. 317</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لا خير في القول إلاّ مع الفعل، ولا من المنظر إلاّ مع المخبر، ولا في المال إلاّ مع الجود، ولا في الصدق إلا مع الوفاء، ولا في الفقه إلاّ مع الورع، ولا في الصدقة إلاّ مع النيّة، ولا في الحياة إلاّ مع الصحّة، ولا في الوطن إلاّ مع الأمن والسرور.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, there is no good in speech except with action, nor in outward appearance except with inner substance, nor in wealth except with generosity, nor in truthfulness except with keeping faith, nor in jurisprudence (fiqh) except with scrupulous piety (waraʿ), nor in charity except with [sincere] intention, nor in life except with health, nor in a homeland except with security and happiness.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The print has «ولا من المنظر» (with من, not في). المخبر = what a person turns out to be on testing, their inner reality, as opposed to المنظر, how they look. الوفاء = fulfilling promises and commitments.</sub><br><sub>p. 317</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، حرّم [الله] من الشاة سبعة أشياء: الدم، والمذاكير، والمثانة، والنخاع، والغدد، والطحال، والمرارة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, [Allah] has forbidden seven things of the sheep: the blood, the genitals, the bladder, the spinal cord, the glands, the spleen, and the gall bladder.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> [الله] is the editor's bracketed insertion. المذاكير = male genital organs (penis and testicles).</sub><br><sub>p. 317</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لا تماكس في أربعة أشياء: في شراء الأضحية، والكفن، والنسمة، والكراء إلى مكة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, do not haggle over four things: in buying the sacrificial animal, the shroud, and a slave [to be freed], and in hiring transport to Mecca.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> النسمة (lit. 'a soul, a person') here means a slave bought for manumission. الكراء إلى مكة = hiring a mount or passage for the journey to Mecca (for Hajj).</sub><br><sub>p. 317</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ألا أخبركم بأشبهكم بي خلقاً، قال: بلى يا رسول الله. قال: أحسنكم خلقاً، وأعظمكم حلماً، وأبرّكم بقرابته، وأشدّكم من نفسه إنصافاً.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, shall I not tell you which of you most resembles me in character? He said: Certainly, O Messenger of Allah. He said: The best of you in character, the greatest of you in forbearance, the most dutiful of you to his kin, and the most ready of you to do justice against his own self.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> خلقاً is unvocalized; read both times as khuluqan (character). The first could also be khalqan (physical form), but the answer lists moral traits. أخبركم is plural even though ʿAli is addressed. أشدّكم من نفسه إنصافاً, lit. 'the most intense of you in giving fairness from himself', i.e. the one who most readily gives others their due even against his own interest.</sub><br><sub>p. 317</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ: أمان لأمّتي من الغرق إذا ركبوا [هم] السفن، فقرأوا: ﴿وَمَا قَدَرُوا اللَّهَ حَقَّ قَدْرِهِ وَالأَرْضُ جَمِيعًا قَبْضَتُهُ يَوْمَ الْقِيَامَةِ وَالسَّمَاوَاتُ مَطْوِيَّاتٌ بِيَمِينِهِ سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى عَمَّا يُشْرِكُونَ﴾ ﴿بِسْمِ اللَّهِ مَجْرَاهَا وَمُرْسَاهَا إِنَّ رَبِّي لَغَفُورٌ رَحِيمٌ﴾.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, my community's safeguard against drowning, when they board ships, is that they recite: 'They have not valued Allah as He truly deserves, while the whole earth will be within His grasp on the Day of Resurrection and the heavens will be rolled up in His right hand. Glorified and exalted is He above what they associate [with Him]' [Q 39:67]; 'In the name of Allah be its sailing and its anchoring. Indeed, my Lord is All-Forgiving, All-Merciful' [Q 11:41].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The print has a colon after يا عليّ. [هم] is the editor's insertion from al-Faqīh. فقرأوا is past tense in form ('and they recited') but conditional in sense, so it is rendered 'is that they recite'.</sub><br><sub>p. 318</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أمان لأمّتي من السرق: ﴿قُلْ ادْعُوا اللَّهَ أَوْ ادْعُوا الرَّحْمَانَ أَيًّامَّا تَدْعُوا فَلَهُ الأَسْمَاءُ الْحُسْنَى﴾ إلى آخر السورة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, my community's safeguard against theft is: 'Say: Call upon Allah or call upon the All-Beneficent; whichever you call upon, to Him belong the Most Beautiful Names' [Q 17:110], to the end of the sura.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The print runs أيا ما together with no space (standard Uthmani: أَيًّا مَّا); at scan resolution it is unclear whether the mark over the yāʾ is tanwīn or a plain fatḥa with shadda. The Quranic vocalization as printed has sukūn on قُلْ and أَوْ before ادْعُوا.</sub><br><sub>p. 318</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أمان لأمتي من الهدم: ﴿إِنَّ اللَّهَ يُمْسِكُ السَّمَاوَاتِ وَالأَرْضَ أَنْ تَزُولاَ وَلَئِنْ زَالَتَا إِنْ أَمْسَكَهُمَا مِنْ أَحَدٍ مِنْ بَعْدِهِ إِنَّهُ كَانَ حَلِيمًا غَفُورًا﴾.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, my community's safeguard against [being crushed under] collapsing buildings is: 'Indeed, Allah holds the heavens and the earth lest they cease [to exist]; and if they should cease, no one could hold them after Him. Indeed, He is Forbearing, All-Forgiving' [Q 35:41].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> الهدم = the collapse or demolition of a building (being buried under falling walls). The print has لأمتي here without shadda.</sub><br><sub>p. 318</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أمان لأمّتي من الهمّ: «لا حول ولا قوّة إلاّ بالله، لا ملجأ ولا منجا من الله إلاّ إليه».</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, my community's safeguard against anxiety is: 'There is no power and no strength except through Allah; there is no refuge and no escape from Allah except to Him.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> الهمّ = worry, anxiety, care.</sub><br><sub>p. 318</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أمان لأمّتي من الحرق: ﴿إِنَّ وَلِيِّيَ اللَّهُ الَّذِي نَزَّلَ الْكِتَابَ وَهُوَ يَتَوَلَّى الصَّالِحِينَ﴾ ﴿وَمَا قَدَرُوا اللَّهَ حَقَّ قَدْرِهِ﴾ إلى آخر الآية.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, my community's safeguard against burning is: 'Indeed, my Protector is Allah, who sent down the Book, and He takes care of the righteous' [Q 7:196]; and 'They have not valued Allah as He truly deserves' [Q 39:67], to the end of the verse.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor notes that some sources read الغرق (drowning) instead of الحرق (burning).</sub><br><sub>p. 318</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من خاف السباع فليقرأ: ﴿لَقَدْ جَاءَكُمْ رَسُولٌ مِنْ أَنْفُسِكُمْ عَزِيزٌ عَلَيْهِ مَا عَنِتُّمْ حَرِيصٌ﴾ إلى آخر السورة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, whoever fears wild beasts, let him recite: 'There has certainly come to you a Messenger from among yourselves; grievous to him is whatever distresses you; he is deeply concerned [for you]' [Q 9:128], to the end of the sura.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The verse is quoted only up to حريص; 'to the end of the sura' covers 9:128–129.</sub><br><sub>p. 318</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من استصعبت عليه دابّة فليقرأ في أذنها اليمنى: ﴿وَلَهُ أَسْلَمَ مَنْ فِي السَّمَاوَاتِ وَالأَرْضِ طَوْعًا وَكَرْهًا وَإِلَيْهِ يُرْجَعُونَ﴾.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, whoever finds his riding animal unruly, let him recite into its right ear: 'To Him submits whoever is in the heavens and the earth, willingly or unwillingly, and to Him they will be returned' [Q 3:83].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor's footnotes: the base manuscript has استصعب, and al-Faqīh has دابّته ('his animal').</sub><br><sub>p. 319</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من كان في بطنه ماء أصفر فليكتب على بطنه آية الكرسي وليشربه، فإنّه يبرأ بإذن الله عزّ وجلّ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, whoever has yellow water in his belly, let him write the Verse of the Throne (Āyat al-Kursī, Q 2:255) on his belly and drink it, for he will be cured by the permission of Allah, Mighty and Majestic.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> ماء أصفر ('yellow water') probably means an abdominal fluid disorder such as dropsy or ascites. 'Write ... on his belly and drink it' is literal. The instruction to drink suggests writing the verse and drinking the water it is washed off in, and على بطنه may also be read as 'for (the ailment of) his belly'. The masculine pronoun in وليشربه has no explicit antecedent.</sub><br><sub>p. 319</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من خاف ساحراً أو شيطاناً فليقرأ: ﴿إِنَّ رَبَّكُمْ اللَّهُ الَّذِي خَلَقَ السَّمَاوَاتِ وَالأَرْضَ﴾ إلى آخر الآية.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, whoever fears a sorcerer or a devil, let him recite: 'Indeed, your Lord is Allah, who created the heavens and the earth' [Q 7:54], to the end of the verse.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The print appears to show sukūn on رَبَّكُمْ (standard: رَبَّكُمُ); transcribed as seen.</sub><br><sub>p. 319</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، حقّ الولد على والده أن يحسن اسمه وأدبه، ويضعه موضعاً صالحاً، وحقّ الوالد على ولده أن لا يسمّيه باسمه، ولا يمشي بين يديه، ولا يجلس أمامه، ولا يدخل معه الحمّام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, the child's right over his father is that he give him a good name and a good upbringing, and set him in a sound position; and the father's right over his child is that he not call him by his name, not walk ahead of him, not sit in front of him, and not enter the bathhouse with him.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> يضعه موضعاً صالحاً ('places him in a sound place') is open: it may mean a good station in life or occupation, or good surroundings and company. أن يحسن اسمه وأدبه may be read yuḥsin or yuḥassin ('make good'); the sense is the same.</sub><br><sub>p. 319</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، ثلاثة من الوسواس: أكل الطين، وتقليم الأظفار بالأسنان، وأكل اللحية.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, three things come from obsessive whispering (waswās): eating clay, trimming the nails with the teeth, and chewing the beard.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> الوسواس may mean Satanic whispering or obsessive compulsion (both senses overlap in the tradition). أكل اللحية, lit. 'eating the beard' = chewing or gnawing at one's beard.</sub><br><sub>p. 319</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لعن الله والدين حملا ولدهما على عقوقهما.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, may Allah curse parents who drive their child to undutifulness toward them.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> عقوق = undutifulness or disobedience toward parents, a grave sin.</sub><br><sub>p. 319</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، يلزم الوالدين من عقوق ولدهم ما يلزم الولد لهما من عقوقهما.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, parents incur, for undutifulness toward their child, the same [liability] that the child incurs toward them for undutifulness toward them.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Sense: parents who wrong their child bear the same guilt as a child who wrongs his parents. The print has the plural ولدهم ('their child') here, where the neighbouring sayings have the dual ولدهما.</sub><br><sub>p. 319</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، رحم الله والدين حملا ولدهما على برّهما.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, may Allah have mercy on parents who help their child to be dutiful toward them.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> حملا ... على = induced, led (the counterpart of the curse above).</sub><br><sub>p. 319</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من أحزن والديه فقد عقّهما.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, whoever grieves his parents has been undutiful to them.<br><sub>p. 319</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من أغتيب عنده أخوه المسلم فاستطاع نصره فلم ينصره خذله الله تعالى في الدنيا والآخرة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, whoever hears his Muslim brother backbitten in his presence, and is able to defend him but does not, Allah the Exalted will forsake him in this world and the Hereafter.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The print has أغتيب with hamza on the alif (standard: اُغتيب).</sub><br><sub>p. 319</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من كفى يتيماً في نفقته بماله حتّى يستغني وجبت له الجنّة البتّة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, whoever provides for an orphan's maintenance from his own wealth until the orphan becomes self-sufficient, Paradise is certainly due to him.<br><sub>p. 319</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من مسح يده على رأس يتيم ترحّماً له، أعطاه الله عزّ وجلّ بكلّ شعرة نوراً يوم القيامة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, whoever passes his hand over an orphan's head out of compassion for him, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will give him a light for every hair on the Day of Resurrection.<br><sub>p. 320</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لا فقر أشدّ من الجهل، ولا مال أعود من العقل، ولا وحدة أوحش من العجب، ولا عقل كالتدبير، ولا ورع كالكف عن محارم الله، ولا حسب كحسن الخلق، ولا عبادة مثل التفكّر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, there is no poverty more severe than ignorance, no wealth more profitable than intellect, no loneliness more desolate than self-admiration (ʿujb), no intellect like forethought, no piety like refraining from what Allah has forbidden, no nobility (ḥasab) like good character, and no worship like reflection.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor notes al-Faqīh reads وحشة ('no desolation more desolate'). أعود = more beneficial or profitable. التدبير = planning, considering the consequences of one's affairs. حسب = inherited or acquired nobility and honour.</sub><br><sub>p. 320</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، آفة الحديث الكذب، وآفة العلم النسيان، وآفة العبادة الفترة، وآفة الجمال الخيلاء، وآفة العلم الحسد.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, the bane of speech is lying, the bane of knowledge is forgetfulness, the bane of worship is slackening, the bane of beauty is vanity, and the bane of knowledge is envy.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The print clearly repeats آفة العلم ('the bane of knowledge') for both forgetfulness and envy. The second may be a misprint for another noun (e.g. الحلم or العالِم); translated as printed. الحديث could also mean 'hadith, reports' ('the bane of reporting is lying'). الفترة = lapse, slackening after zeal.</sub><br><sub>p. 320</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، أربعة يذهبن ضياعاً الأكل على الشبع، والسّراج في القمر، والزرع في السبخة، والصنيعة عند غير أهلها.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, four things go to waste: eating on a full stomach, a lamp in the moonlight, planting in salty ground, and a kindness done to someone unworthy of it.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor's footnote glosses السبخة as land that is wet and salty. الصنيعة = a favour or good deed done for someone.</sub><br><sub>p. 320</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، من نسي الصلاة عليّ فقد أخطأ طريق الجنّة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, whoever forgets to invoke blessings upon me has missed the path to Paradise.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> الصلاة عليّ = the ṣalawāt, invoking Allah's blessings on the Prophet (not the ritual prayer).</sub><br><sub>p. 320</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إيّاك ونقرة الغراب وفريسة الأسد.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, beware of the crow's peck and the lion's prey.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Probably about prayer postures: 'the crow's peck' = a hasty prostration that barely touches the ground, and 'the lion's ...' = spreading the forearms flat on the ground in prostration. The print reads فريسة ('prey'); a reading such as فرشة/افتراش الأسد ('the lion's spreading [of its forelimbs]') would fit that sense better, so فريسة may be a corruption. Translated literally as printed.</sub><br><sub>p. 320</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، لأن أدخل يدي في فم التنين إلى المرافق أحبّ إليّ من أن أسأل مَن لم يكن ثمّ كان.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, to put my hand into the mouth of a dragon up to the elbow is dearer to me than to ask [anything] of someone who was nothing and then became something.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor's footnote glosses التنين as a huge serpent or dragon-like beast. The print has the plural المرافق ('elbows'); al-Faqīh has the singular المرفق. مَن لم يكن ثمّ كان = one who had no standing or wealth and then acquired it (a parvenu); it could also be read as asking of a created being at all, but the parvenu sense is more usual.</sub><br><sub>p. 320</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إنّ أغنى[؟] الناس على الله عزّ وجلّ القاتل غير قاتله، والضارب غير ضاربه، ومن تولّى غير مواليه، فقد كفر بما أنزل الله عزّ وجلّ [عليّ].</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, the most insolent of people toward Allah, Mighty and Majestic, is the one who kills someone other than his own killer, and the one who strikes someone other than the one who struck him; and whoever takes as his masters others than his rightful masters (mawālī) has disbelieved in what Allah, Mighty and Majestic, sent down [upon me].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> UNCERTAIN: the print clearly reads أغنى ('richest'), which makes no sense here. It is almost certainly a misprint for أعتى ('most insolent, most defiant'), which is what is translated. القاتل غير قاتله = one who kills someone other than the person who wronged him, e.g. taking revenge on an innocent. تولّى غير مواليه: in legal usage, a freed slave who claims patronage from someone other than his emancipators; the Shia reading is taking as guardians others than the rightful Imams. [عليّ] ('upon me', ʿalayya, not the name ʿAli) is the editor's insertion from al-Faqīh.</sub><br><sub>p. 320</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، تختّم باليمين فإنها فضيلة من الله عزّ وجلّ للمقرّبين. قال: بما أتختم يا رسول الله؟ قال: بالعقيق الأحمر، فإنّه أوّل جبل أقرّ لله تعالى بالربوبيّة، ولي بالنبوّة، ولك بالوصيّة، ولولدك بالإمامة، وشيعتك بالجنّة، ولأعدائك بالنار.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, wear your ring on the right hand, for it is a merit from Allah, Mighty and Majestic, for those brought near [to Him]. He said: With what [stone] shall I wear a ring, O Messenger of Allah? He said: With red agate (ʿaqīq), for it was the first mountain to affirm Lordship for Allah the Exalted, prophethood for me, successorship (waṣiyya) for you, the Imamate for your descendants, Paradise for your Shia, and the Fire for your enemies.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This saying runs from the last line of p.320 onto the top of p.321 and is kept as one segment. The print has بما (not بمَ) and وشيعتك without the preposition لـ that the other items have. العقيق = agate, here likely the mountain or quarry it comes from, as the next clause says. الوصيّة = being the designated successor (waṣī).</sub><br><sub>p. 320</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إنّ الله عزّ وجلّ أشرف على [أهل] الدنيا فاختارني منها على رجال العالمين، ثمّ اطّلع الثانية فاختارك على رجال العالمين، ثمّ اطّلع الثالثة فاختار الائمّة من ولدك على رجال العالمين، ثمّ اطّلع الرّابعة فاختار فاطمة على نساء العالمين.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, looked down upon [the people of] the world and chose me from it over the men of all the worlds; then He looked a second time and chose you over the men of all the worlds; then He looked a third time and chose the Imams from your descendants over the men of all the worlds; then He looked a fourth time and chose Fatima over the women of all the worlds.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> [أهل] is the editor's insertion from al-Faqīh. The print has منها (feminine, referring to الدنيا). The print has الائمّة without hamza.</sub><br><sub>p. 321</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إني رأيت إسمك مقروناً باسمي في أربع مواطن، فانست بالنظر إليه، إنّي لمّا بلغت بيت المقدس في معراجي إلى السماء وجدت على صخرتها لا إله إلاّ الله، محمّد رسول الله أيّدته بوزيره ونصرته بوزيره، فقلت لجبرئيل: مَن وزيري؟ فقال: عليّ بن أبي طالب. فلمّا انتهيت إلى سدرة المنتهى وجدت مكتوباً عليها: أنّي أنا الله لا إله إلاّ أنا وحدي، محمّد صفوتي من خلقي، أيّدته بوزيره ونصرته بوزيره. فقلت لجبرئيل: مَن وزيري؟ فقال: عليّ بن أبي طالب (عليه السلام). فلمّا جاوزت سدرة المنتهى إنتهيت إلى عرش ربّ العالمين جلّ جلاله، فوجدت مكتوباً على قوائمه: أنّي أنا الله لا إله إلاّ أنا وحدي ومحمّد حبيبي أيّدته بوزيره ونصرته بوزيره. [فلمّا رفعت رأسي فوجدت على بطنان العرش مكتوباً: لا إله إلاّ أنا وحدي، محمّد عبدي ورسولي، أيّدته بوزيره ونصرته بوزيره].</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, I saw your name joined with my name in four places, and I found comfort in looking at it. When I reached Bayt al-Maqdis (Jerusalem) on my ascension (miʿrāj) to heaven, I found on its Rock: 'There is no god but Allah; Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah. I supported him with his minister (wazīr) and aided him with his minister.' So I said to Jabraʾīl: Who is my minister? He said: ʿAli ibn Abi Talib. When I came to the Lote Tree of the Utmost Boundary (Sidrat al-Muntahā), I found written on it: 'I am Allah; there is no god but Me, alone. Muhammad is My chosen one from My creation. I supported him with his minister and aided him with his minister.' So I said to Jabraʾīl: Who is my minister? He said: ʿAli ibn Abi Talib (a.s.). When I passed beyond the Lote Tree of the Utmost Boundary, I reached the Throne of the Lord of the worlds, Majestic is His majesty, and I found written on its legs: 'I am Allah; there is no god but Me, alone; and Muhammad is My beloved. I supported him with his minister and aided him with his minister.' [When I raised my head, I found written on the inner expanse of the Throne: 'There is no god but Me, alone. Muhammad is My servant and My Messenger. I supported him with his minister and aided him with his minister.']<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This saying runs from p.321 onto the first line of p.322 and is kept as one segment. The editor notes al-Faqīh has ثلاثة ('three' places), and the final bracketed passage (the fourth place) is marked as not in al-Faqīh. The print has أربع مواطن (the expected form is أربعة), إسمك and إنتهيت with hamzat qaṭʿ, and فانست without hamza (= فأنست, 'I took comfort'). أنّي أنا الله, lit. 'that I am Allah'. بطنان العرش = the inner part or middle expanse of the Throne. قوائمه = its legs or supporting pillars.</sub><br><sub>p. 321</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا عليّ، إن الله تبارك وتعالى أعطاني فيك [سبع] خصال: أنت أوّل من ينشقّ عنه القبر معي، وأنت أوّل من يقف على الصراط معي، [وأنت أول من يكسي إذا كسيت، ويحيى إذا حييت،] وأنت أوّل من يسكن معي في علّيّين، وأنت أوّل من يشرب معي من الرحيق المختوم الذي ختامه مسك.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O ʿAli, Allah, Blessed and Exalted, has granted me [seven] qualities concerning you: you are the first, with me, for whom the grave will split open; you are the first to stand on the Bridge (al-Ṣirāṭ) with me; [you are the first to be clothed when I am clothed, and to be greeted when I am greeted;] you are the first to dwell with me in ʿIlliyyīn (the loftiest station of Paradise); and you are the first to drink with me of the sealed nectar whose seal is musk.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> [سبع] and the bracketed clause are the editor's insertions from al-Faqīh. Only six qualities are listed although seven are announced. The print spells يكسي (for يُكسى). ويحيى إذا حييت is ambiguous: yuḥayyā idhā ḥuyyītu ('greeted when I am greeted', chosen because it pairs with being clothed) or yuḥyā idhā ḥayītu ('brought to life when I am brought to life'). The final clause echoes Q 83:25–26. The editor's footnote marks the end here of the Prophet's counsels to ʿAli (as in al-Faqīh 4:271).</sub><br><sub>p. 322</sub></td>
</tr>
</table>


### Then he (s.a.w.) said to Salmān al-Fārisī, may Allah have mercy on him:

<p dir="rtl" lang="ar"><b>ثمّ قال صلّى الله عليه وآله لسلمان الفارسي رحمة الله عليه:</b></p>

<table>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا سلمان، إنّ لك في علّتك إذا أعتللت ثلاث خصال: أنت من الله تبارك وتعالى بذكر، ودعاءك فيها مستجاب، ولا تدع العلّة عليك ذنباً إلاّ [حطّته] متّعك الله بالعافية إلى إنقضاء أجلك.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O Salmān, when you fall ill, there are three benefits for you in your illness: you are in the remembrance of Allah, Blessed and Exalted; your supplication during it is answered; and the illness leaves no sin upon you without [shedding it away]. May Allah grant you the enjoyment of well-being until your appointed term ends.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> [حطّته] is the editor's insertion from al-Faqīh. The print has أعتللت and إنقضاء with hamzat qaṭʿ, and ودعاءك. أنت من الله بذكر, lit. 'you are, with Allah, in remembrance', i.e. Allah keeps you in mind. متّعك الله ... is read as a closing prayer for Salmān, not a fourth benefit; grammatically it could also be read as a statement ('Allah grants you...').</sub><br><sub>p. 322</sub></td>
</tr>
</table>


### Then the Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.) said to Abū Dharr, may Allah have mercy on him:

<p dir="rtl" lang="ar"><b>ثمّ قال رسول الله صلّى الله عليه وآله لأبي ذر رحمة الله عليه:</b></p>

<table>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا أبا ذر، إيّاك والسؤال، فإنّه ذلّ حاضر، وفقر متعجّل وفيه حساب طويل يوم القيامة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O Abū Dharr, beware of begging, for it is present humiliation and hastened poverty, and in it is a long reckoning on the Day of Resurrection.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> السؤال here means asking people for things (begging), not asking questions. The editor notes al-Faqīh has تتعجّله ('which you hasten upon yourself').</sub><br><sub>p. 322</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا أبا ذر تعيش وحدك، وتموت وحدك، وتدخل الجنّة وحدك، يسعد بك قوم من أهل العراق يتولّون غسلك وتجهيزك ودفنك.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O Abū Dharr, you will live alone, die alone, and enter Paradise alone. A group of people from Iraq will be blessed through you: they will take charge of washing your body, preparing you [for burial], and burying you.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The print has no comma after ذر. يسعد بك = they will gain good fortune or felicity through you.</sub><br><sub>p. 323</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا أبا ذرّ لا تسأل بكفّك وإن أتاك شيء، فاقبله.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O Abū Dharr, do not beg with your palm; but if something comes to you [unasked], accept it.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The comma placement is as printed. وإن could also be read concessively ('even if something comes to you'), but the following فاقبله makes it conditional: 'if something comes, accept it'.</sub><br><sub>p. 323</sub></td>
</tr>
</table>


### Then he (s.a.w.) said to his Companions:

<p dir="rtl" lang="ar"><b>ثمّ قال صلى الله عليه وآله لأصحابه:</b></p>

<table>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">ألا أخبركم بشراركم؟ قالوا: بلى يا رسول الله. قال: المشّاوؤن بالنميمة، المفرّقون بين الأحبّة، الباغون [للبراء] العيب.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Shall I not tell you who are the worst of you? They said: Certainly, O Messenger of Allah. He said: Those who go about spreading slander, those who separate loved ones, those who seek out faults in the innocent.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The print spells المشّاوؤن (= المشّاؤون, 'those who go about'). [للبراء] is the editor's insertion from al-Faqīh and Biḥār, filling a blank in the base manuscript; the editor glosses the phrase as 'those who seek faults for one who is innocent of them'. العيب is from al-Faqīh; the base manuscript has الغيب. This narration ends the chunk; p.323 has no further body text after it.</sub><br><sub>p. 323</sub></td>
</tr>
</table>


## From the concise admonitions of the Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.)

<p dir="rtl" lang="ar"><b>من مواعظ رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله) الموجزة</b></p>

<table>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">ومن ألفاظ رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله) الموجزة التي لم يسبق إليها:</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Among the concise expressions of the Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.) that no one had used before him:<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'lam yusbaq ilayhā' = lit. 'to which he was not preceded', i.e. phrases he coined first. The numbering of the sayings in this edition begins at [2]; this introductory line carries no printed number.</sub><br><sub>p. 324</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٢] اليد العليا خير من اليد السفلى.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[2] The upper hand is better than the lower hand.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The upper hand = the one that gives; the lower = the one that receives.</sub><br><sub>p. 324</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٣] ما قلّ وكفى خير ممّا [كثر] وألهى.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[3] What is little and suffices is better than what is abundant and distracts.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor's footnote: the base manuscript reads 'ما قيل وكفى ممّا... الهي', which the editor considers a scribal corruption; the bracketed [كثر] and the corrected wording come from al-Faqīh (Man lā yaḥḍuruhu al-faqīh).</sub><br><sub>p. 324</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٤] خير الزّاد التقوى.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[4] The best provision is God-wariness (taqwā).<br><sub>p. 324</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٥] رأس الحلم مخافة الله عزّ وجلّ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[5] The head of forbearance is fear of Allah, Mighty and Majestic.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor's footnote: al-Faqīh and the other sources read 'رأس الحكمة' ('the head of wisdom') instead of 'الحلم' (forbearance).</sub><br><sub>p. 324</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٦] خير ما أُلقي في القلب اليقين.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[6] The best thing cast into the heart is certainty.<br><sub>p. 324</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٧] الارتياب من الكفر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[7] Doubt is part of disbelief.<br><sub>p. 324</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٨] النياحة من عمل الجاهليّة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[8] Wailing [over the dead] is one of the practices of the Jāhiliyya (the pre-Islamic age of ignorance).<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The print has a stray space before the final full stop; normalized.</sub><br><sub>p. 324</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٩] السكر جمر النار.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[9] Intoxication is the embers of the Fire.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Unvowelled: read as al-sukr (drunkenness/intoxication). It could also be read al-sakar ('an intoxicating drink'), which gives 'Intoxicating drink is the embers of the Fire'.</sub><br><sub>p. 324</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٠] الشعر من إبليس.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[10] Poetry is from Iblīs.<br><sub>p. 324</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١١] الخمر جِماع الاثام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[11] Wine is the gathering-point of all sins.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> jimāʿ = that which gathers/combines, i.e. the source of all sins. Editor's footnote glosses it this way: 'i.e. a cause of all of them, for when reason departs, no sin seems ugly to a person'. 'الاثام' is printed without hamza/madda (= الآثام).</sub><br><sub>p. 324</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٢] النساء [حبائل] إبليس.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[12] Women are the snares of Iblīs.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor's footnote: the word is blank in the base manuscript and was supplied by the editor; al-Faqīh reads 'النساء حبالة الشيطان' ('Women are the snare of Satan').</sub><br><sub>p. 325</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٣] الشباب شعبة من الجنون.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[13] Youth is a branch of madness.<br><sub>p. 325</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٤] شرّ المكاسب الرّبا.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[14] The worst of earnings is ribā (usury).<br><sub>p. 325</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٥] شر المآكل مال اليتيم ظلماً.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[15] The worst of foods is an orphan's property [consumed] unjustly.<br><sub>p. 325</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٦] السعيد من وعظ بغيره.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[16] The fortunate one is he who takes admonition from [what happens to] others.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Unvowelled 'وعظ' is read as passive, wuʿiẓa: 'was admonished by another (by another's example)'.</sub><br><sub>p. 325</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٧] الشّقي من شقي في بطن أمّه، [والسعيد من سعد في بطن أمّه].</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[17] The wretched one is he who was wretched in his mother's womb, [and the fortunate one is he who was fortunate in his mother's womb].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor's footnote: the bracketed second clause is absent from the base manuscript and from al-Faqīh; the editor added it from Biḥār al-anwār.</sub><br><sub>p. 325</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٨] مصيركم إلى أربعة أذرع.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[18] Your end is [a space of] four cubits.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> i.e. the grave. Editor's footnote: the base manuscript and al-Faqīh read this way; Biḥār and the other sources read 'وإنما يصير أحدكم إلى موضع أربعة أذرع' ('Each of you will only end up in a place of four cubits').</sub><br><sub>p. 325</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٩] أربى الربا الكذب.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[19] The worst form of usury is lying.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Lit. 'the most usurious of usury'. arbā (from the same root as ribā) can also mean 'greatest, most excessive', i.e. 'the gravest ribā is lying'.</sub><br><sub>p. 325</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٢٠] سباب المؤمن فسوق، قتال المؤمن كفر، أكل لحمه [من] معصية الله تعالى، حرمة ماله كحرمة دمه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[20] Reviling a believer is wickedness (fusūq), fighting a believer is disbelief, eating his flesh is [an act of] disobedience to Allah the Exalted, and the sanctity of his property is like the sanctity of his blood.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'Eating his flesh' = backbiting him (cf. Q 49:12). Editor's footnote: the bracketed [من] is supplied from al-Faqīh; with it, the phrase literally means 'is among the acts of disobedience to Allah'.</sub><br><sub>p. 325</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٢١] من يكظم الغيظ يأجره الله.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[21] Whoever restrains his anger, Allah will reward him.<br><sub>p. 325</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٢٢] من يصبر على الرزيّة يعوّضه الله.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[22] Whoever is patient in calamity, Allah will compensate him.<br><sub>p. 325</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٢٣] الآن حمي الوطيس.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[23] Now the oven has grown hot!<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> An idiom for 'the battle is now at its fiercest'. Editor's footnote (quoting al-Majlisī from al-Nihāya): it was said on the day of Ḥunayn. al-waṭīs is something like an oven, or 'the fierce blows in war', or 'the trampling that crushes people'. al-Aṣmaʿī: round stones that, once heated, nobody can tread on. The phrase was not heard from anyone before the Prophet (s.a.w.).</sub><br><sub>p. 325</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٢٤] لا يلسع المؤمن من جحر مرّتين.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[24] A believer is not stung from the same hole twice.<br><sub>p. 326</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٢٥] [لا يجني على المرء إلاّ يده.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[25] [Nothing brings harm upon a person except his own hand.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor's square bracket opens here and closes at the end of [26]. The editor's footnote says both sayings were supplied from al-Faqīh.</sub><br><sub>p. 326</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٢٦] الشديد من غلب نفسه].</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[26] The strong one is he who overcomes his own self.]<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The bracket closes the insertion that began at [25] (from al-Faqīh).</sub><br><sub>p. 326</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٢٧] ليس الخبر كالمعاينة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[27] Hearing news is not like seeing with one's own eyes.<br><sub>p. 326</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٢٨] اللهم بارك لأُمّتي في بكورها يوم سبتها وخميسها.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[28] O Allah, bless my community in its early mornings on its Saturday and its Thursday.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Alternative reading: 'in its early mornings, [and on] its Saturday and its Thursday', taking the blessing to cover the days themselves and not only their early hours.</sub><br><sub>p. 326</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٢٩] المجالس بالأمانة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[29] Gatherings are held in trust.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> i.e. what is said in a gathering is a trust and must not be divulged. Editor's footnote gives the fuller version in al-Ṭūsī's Amālī (via Biḥār): 'Gatherings are held in trust except three: a gathering in which unlawful blood is shed, a gathering in which an unlawful sexual relation is made permissible, and a gathering in which property is taken without right.'</sub><br><sub>p. 326</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٣٠] سيّد القوم خادمهم.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[30] The master of a people is their servant.<br><sub>p. 326</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٣١] لو بغى جبلاً [على جبل] لجعله الله دكّاً.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[31] If a mountain were to transgress [against another mountain], Allah would level it to dust.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor's footnote: [على جبل] is supplied from al-Faqīh. The print has جبلاً in the accusative (tanwīn fatḥ), although the subject of بغى would normally be nominative (جبلٌ). Read as printed without the bracket, it could mean 'If [someone] transgressed against a mountain, Allah would level it'. The subject reading is the more defensible one and is followed here.</sub><br><sub>p. 326</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٣٢] أبدأ بمن تعول.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[32] Begin with those whom you support.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> i.e. spend first on your own dependants. The print writes the first word with hamzat qaṭʿ (أبدأ), which grammatically reads 'I begin'. The well-known imperative wording is 'ابدأ' ('Begin'), and the translation follows that.</sub><br><sub>p. 326</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٣٣] الحرب خدعة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[33] War is deception.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor's footnote (from al-Nihāya via al-Majlisī) gives three vocalizations: khadʿa (the most eloquent and soundest): war is decided by a single deception; khudʿa: the noun 'deception'; khudaʿa: war deceives men and raises their hopes but does not fulfil them.</sub><br><sub>p. 326</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٣٤] المسلم مرآة لأخيه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[34] The Muslim is a mirror to his brother.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor's footnote: Biḥār reads 'المؤمن' (the believer) instead of 'المسلم'.</sub><br><sub>p. 327</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٣٥] مات حتف أنفه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[35] He died a natural death.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Idiom, lit. 'he died the death of his nose', i.e. in his bed, without being killed.</sub><br><sub>p. 327</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٢٦] البلاء موكل بالمنطق.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[36] Affliction is tied to speech.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The print numbers this saying [٢٦], an obvious misprint for [٣٦] (it falls between [35] and [37]). The Arabic keeps the printed number; the English uses 36. Meaning: a person's words can bring misfortune upon him. Lit. 'is entrusted to / assigned to speech'.</sub><br><sub>p. 327</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٣٧] الناس كأسنان المشط سواء.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[37] People are equal, like the teeth of a comb.<br><sub>p. 327</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٣٨] أيّ داء أدوء من البخل.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[38] What disease is more diseased than stinginess?<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'أدوء' as printed (the standard form is أدوى, 'more diseased/harmful'). The sentence is a rhetorical question: no ailment is worse than stinginess.</sub><br><sub>p. 327</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٣٩] الحياء خير كلّه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[39] Modesty (ḥayāʾ) is good, all of it.<br><sub>p. 327</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٤٠] اليمين الفاجرة تذرّ الديار من أهلها بلاقع.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[40] A sinful false oath leaves homes emptied of their people, desolate wastelands.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> al-yamīn al-fājira = a deliberately false oath. 'تذرّ' is printed with shadda (the usual form is تذر, 'leaves'). balāqiʿ = barren, desolate lands.</sub><br><sub>p. 327</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٤١] أعجل الشرّ عقوبة البغي.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[41] The evil that is punished most swiftly is transgression.<br><sub>p. 327</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٤٢] أسرع الخير ثواباً البر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[42] The good deed that is rewarded most swiftly is kindness (birr).<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> birr = righteousness/dutiful kindness, especially towards parents and kin.</sub><br><sub>p. 327</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٤٣] المسلمون عند شروطهم.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[43] Muslims are bound by their conditions.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> i.e. they must honour the stipulations they agree to. Editor's footnote: some sources read 'المؤمنون' (believers).</sub><br><sub>p. 327</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٤٤] إنّ من الشعر لحكمة، وإنّ من البيان لسحرا.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[44] Indeed, some poetry is wisdom, and indeed, some eloquence is magic.<br><sub>p. 327</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٤٥] ارحم مَن في الارض يرحمك من في السماء.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[45] Show mercy to whoever is on earth, and the One in heaven will show mercy to you.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'من في السماء' may also be read as 'those in heaven' (i.e. the angels). 'الارض' is printed without hamza.</sub><br><sub>p. 327</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٤٦] من قتل دون ماله فهو شهيد.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[46] Whoever is killed defending his property is a martyr.<br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٤٧] [العائد في هبته كالعائد في قيئه].</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[47] [One who takes back his gift is like one who goes back to his vomit.]<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor's footnote: the bracketed saying is supplied from al-Faqīh.</sub><br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٤٨] لا يحلّ للمؤمن أن يهجر أخاه المؤمن فوق ثلاث.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[48] It is not permissible for a believer to shun his believing brother for more than three [days].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor's footnote: some sources read 'للمسلم ... المسلم' (Muslim) instead of 'للمؤمن ... المؤمن'.</sub><br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٤٩] من لا يَرحم لا يُرحم.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[49] Whoever does not show mercy will not be shown mercy.<br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٥٠] الندم توبة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[50] Remorse is repentance.<br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٥١] الولد للفراش وللعاهر الحجر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[51] The child belongs to the [marriage] bed, and for the fornicator is the stone.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> A legal maxim: paternity goes to the husband (or owner) of the bed. 'The stone' is read either literally (stoning) or as an idiom for getting nothing, i.e. no claim to the child.</sub><br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٥٢] الدال على الخير كفاعله.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[52] The one who guides to a good deed is like the one who does it.<br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٥٣] حبّك للشي يعمى ويُصمّ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[53] Your love for a thing makes [you] blind and deaf.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'للشي' is printed without the final hamza (= للشيء). 'يعمى' is printed thus, although the causative يُعمي is expected alongside يُصمّ.</sub><br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٥٤] لا يشكر الله من لا يشكر الناس.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[54] Whoever does not thank people has not thanked Allah.<br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٥٥] لا يؤدّي الضالة إلاّ الضال.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[55] None carries off a stray [animal] except one who is himself astray.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Uncertain. As printed, yuʾaddī literally means 'conveys/delivers'. Editor's footnote: al-Faqīh reads 'يؤوي' ('gives shelter to, takes in'). That gives the well-known sense: 'none takes in a stray except one who is in error', i.e. whoever keeps a lost animal without announcing it errs.</sub><br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٥٦] اتقوا النار ولو بشقّ تمرة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[56] Shield yourselves from the Fire, even with half a date.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> i.e. by giving even half a date in charity.</sub><br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٥٧] الأرواح جنود مجنّدة فما تعارف منها ائتلف وما تناكر منها اختلف.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[57] Souls are assembled troops: those among them that knew one another come together in affinity, and those that did not know one another fall into discord.<br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٥٨] [مطل الغني ظلم].</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[58] [A wealthy person's delay [in paying what he owes] is injustice.]<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor's footnote: the bracketed saying is supplied from al-Faqīh.</sub><br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٥٩] السفر قطعة من السقر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[59] Travel is a piece of Saqar (Hell).<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Saqar is one of the names of Hell (cf. Q 74:26-27). Editor's footnotes: al-Faqīh reads 'العذاب', and so do all the sources cited, giving 'Travel is a piece of torment'.</sub><br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٦٠] الناس معادن كمعادن الذهب والفضّة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[60] People are mines, like mines of gold and silver.<br><sub>p. 328</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٦١] صاحب المجلس أحقّ بصدر مجلسه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[61] The host of a gathering has the most right to the head of his gathering.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> ṣāḥib al-majlis = the owner of the sitting place / the host; ṣadr = the seat of honour.</sub><br><sub>p. 329</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٦٢] احثوا في وجوه المدّاحين التراب.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[62] Throw dust in the faces of flatterers.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> al-maddāḥīn = those who habitually praise others to their faces.</sub><br><sub>p. 329</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٦٣] استنزلوا الرزق بالصدقة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[63] Bring down sustenance through charity.<br><sub>p. 329</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٦٤] ادفعوا البلاء بالدّعاء.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[64] Ward off affliction through supplication.<br><sub>p. 329</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٦٥] جبلت القلوب على حبّ من أحسن إليها وبغض من أساء إليها.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[65] Hearts are created with a natural love for whoever treats them well and a hatred for whoever treats them badly.<br><sub>p. 329</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٦٦] ما نقص مالٌ من صدقة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[66] No wealth has ever been diminished by charity.<br><sub>p. 329</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٦٧] لا صدقة وذو رحم محتاج.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[67] There is no [voluntary] charity while a relative is in need.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'Voluntary' is added for clarity. The saying means charity to others is not valid or preferred while one's own kin are in need.</sub><br><sub>p. 329</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٦٨] الصحّة والفراغ نعمتان مكفورتان.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[68] Health and free time are two blessings for which people are ungrateful.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> makfūratān = 'not thanked / taken for granted'.</sub><br><sub>p. 329</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٦٩] عفو المَلك أبقى للمُلك.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[69] A king's pardon better preserves his kingship.<br><sub>p. 329</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٧٠] هيبة الرّجل لزوجته تزيد في عفّتها.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[70] A man's awe-inspiring dignity in his wife's eyes increases her chastity.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Editor's footnote: the base manuscript reads 'هيبة' as printed; al-Wasāʾil reads 'هبة' ('a man's gift to his wife'); some sources read 'تهنئة' ('a man's gladdening of his wife'); the editor judges the printed reading correct. haybat al-rajul li-zawjatihi could also be read as 'a man's reverence/regard for his wife'.</sub><br><sub>p. 329</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٧١] لا طاعة لمخلوق في معصية الخالق.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[71] There is no obedience to a created being in disobedience to the Creator.<br><sub>p. 329</sub></td>
</tr>
</table>


## Imam ʿAlī (a.s.) and an Old Man from the People of al-Shām (Syria)

<p dir="rtl" lang="ar"><b>الإمام علي (عليه السلام) وشيخ من أهل الشام</b></p>

<table>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[٧٢] وروى لي محمّد بن إبراهيم بن إسحاق رضي الله عنه، عن أحمد بن محمّد بن سعيد الهمداني قال: حدّثنا الحسن بن القاسم قراءة قال: [حدّثنا علي بن إبراهيم بن المُعلّى، قال: حدّثنا أبو عبد الله محمّد بن خالد، قال:] حدّثنا عبد الله بن بكر المُراديّ، عن موسى بن جعفر، عن أبيه، عن جدّه، عن علي بن الحسين، عن أبيه (عليهم السلام) قال: بينا أمير الؤمنين (عليه السلام) ذات يوم جالس مع أصحابه يعبّأهم للحرب، إذ أتاه شيخ عليه شجّة السفر فقال: أين أمير المؤمنين (عليه السلام)؟ فقيل: هو ذا، فسلّم عليه ثمّ قال: يا أمير المؤمنين، إنّي أتيتك من ناحية الشام، وأنا شيخ كبير قد سمعت [فيك] من الفضل ما لا أحصي، وإنّي أظنّك ستُغتال، فعلّمني ممّا علّمك الله.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[72] Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Isḥāq (may Allah be pleased with him) narrated to me, from Aḥmad ibn Muḥammad ibn Saʿīd al-Hamdānī, who said: al-Ḥasan ibn al-Qāsim narrated to us by way of reading (qirāʾa), he said: [ʿAlī ibn Ibrāhīm ibn al-Muʿallā narrated to us, he said: Abū ʿAbd Allāh Muḥammad ibn Khālid narrated to us, he said:] ʿAbd Allāh ibn Bakr al-Murādī narrated to us, from Mūsā ibn Jaʿfar, from his father, from his grandfather, from ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn, from his father (a.s.), who said: One day, while Amir al-Mu'minin (a.s.) was sitting with his companions, drawing them up for battle, an old man came to him bearing the worn marks of travel. He said: Where is Amir al-Mu'minin (a.s.)? He was told: There he is. So he greeted him with salām (the greeting of peace) and then said: O Amir al-Mu'minin, I have come to you from the region of al-Shām. I am a very old man, and I have heard more excellence [about you] than I can count, and I think you will be assassinated. So teach me some of what Allah has taught you.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The words in square brackets (two isnad links, and فيك 'about you') are the editor's additions; his footnotes give al-Amālī as the source. 'قراءة' refers to the transmission method: the student read the text aloud to the teacher. The first 'أمير الؤمنين' is printed without the م, a misprint for المؤمنين; it is kept as printed. 'يعبّأهم' is printed this way (the usual spelling is يعبّئهم), meaning 'mobilizing / drawing them up in ranks'. 'شجّة السفر' literally means 'the head-wound (scar) of travel'. The editor's footnote gives al-Faqīh's reading 'شحبة', i.e. the pallor or change of colour that travel causes, so it is rendered 'worn marks of travel'. 'ستُغتال' means 'you will be killed by stealth or treachery'. The ligature after 'عن أبيه' seems to be the plural form (عليهم السلام), covering the chain of Imams, and is rendered '(a.s.)'.</sub><br><sub>p. 330</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">قال (عليه السلام): نعم يا شيخ، من اعتدل يوماه فهو مغبون، ومن كانت الدنيا همّه اشتدّت حسرته على فِراقها، ومن كان غده شرّ من يوميه فهو محروم، ومن لم يبال بما زوي من آخرته إذا سَلِمت له دنياه فهو هالك، ومن لم يتعاهد النقص من نفسه غلب عليه الهوى، [ومن كان في نقص فالموت أهون له.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">He (a.s.) said: Yes, old man. Whoever's two days are equal has been cheated. Whoever's chief concern is this world, his anguish at parting from it will be intense. Whoever's tomorrow is worse than his two days is deprived. Whoever does not care about what is withheld from him of his hereafter, so long as his worldly life stays safe for him, is ruined. Whoever does not keep watch over the deficiency within himself, desire (hawā, base inclination) overpowers him. [And whoever is in decline, death is easier for him.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Continues narration [72]. 'يوماه' means 'his two days', i.e. yesterday and today: the person whose today is no better than his yesterday has been cheated. 'غده شرّ' is printed in the nominative (grammatically one expects شرًّا). 'زوي' means 'withheld or taken away from'. 'أهون له' literally means 'easier / lighter for him', i.e. death is better for him than living on in decline. The square bracket opens here and closes at the end of the 'night raid' saying two segments below; the editor marks the whole passage as supplied from al-Amālī.</sub><br><sub>p. 331</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا شيخ، إنّ الدنيا خَضِرةٌ حلوة ولها أهل، وإنّ الآخرة لها أهل ظَلِفت أنفسهم عن مفاخرة أهل الدنيا، لا يتنافسون في الدنيا، ولا يفرحون بغضارتها، ولا يحزنون لبؤسها.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O old man, this world is green and sweet, and it has its people. The hereafter also has its people, whose souls hold back from vying in boasts with the people of this world. They do not compete with one another over this world, they do not rejoice in its lush comforts, and they do not grieve at its hardship.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Continues narration [72], inside the editor's bracketed passage. 'خَضِرةٌ حلوة' means lush and sweet, i.e. alluring. 'ظَلِفت أنفسهم عن' means their souls refrained from; the editor's footnote glosses it as كفّت. 'غضارة' is freshness, ease, or abundance of living.</sub><br><sub>p. 331</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا شيخ، من خاف من البيات قلّ نومه، ما أسرع الليالي والأيام في عمر العبد! فاخزُن لسانك، وعد كلامك إلاّ بخير].</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O old man, whoever fears a night raid sleeps little. How swiftly the nights and days pass in a servant's life! So guard your tongue, and count your words, [letting them be] only for good.]<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Continues narration [72]. The editor's bracketed passage from al-Amālī closes here. 'البيات' is a surprise attack by night. 'فاخزُن لسانك' literally means 'store away your tongue'. 'وعد' is unvocalized. It is read as عُدَّ 'count', but the construction with إلاّ بخير seems elliptical, roughly 'count your words, and do not speak except with good'. That reading is uncertain.</sub><br><sub>p. 331</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">يا شيخ، إرض للناس ما ترضى لنفسك، وأئت للناس ما تُحبّ أن يُؤتى إليك.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">O old man, be pleased for people with what you are pleased with for yourself, and do for people what you would like to be done for you.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Continues narration [72]. 'إرض' is printed with a hamza under the alif (normally ارضَ, with hamzat al-waṣl). 'وأئت' is printed in this spelling: it is the imperative of أتى, 'bring / render to', i.e. treat people as you would like to be treated.</sub><br><sub>p. 331</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">ثمّ أقبل على أصحابه فقال: أيها الناس، أما ترون أنّ أهل الدنيا يُمسون ويُصبحون على أحوال شتّى، فبين صريع يتلوّى، وبين عائدٍ ومَعُود، وآخر بنفسه يجود، وآخر لا يُرجى، وآخر مسجّى، وطالب الدنيا والموت يطلبه، وغافل وليس بمغفول عنه، وعلى أثر الماضي يصير الباقي.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Then he turned to his companions and said: O people, do you not see that the people of this world spend their evenings and mornings in varied states? Among them is one struck down and writhing; there are the visitor of the sick and the sick one being visited; another is giving up his soul; another is beyond hope; another lies covered in a shroud; there is the seeker of this world while death is seeking him; and there is the heedless one who is not himself overlooked. And in the footsteps of the one who has gone, the one who remains follows.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Continues narration [72]. This segment runs from p. 331 to p. 332, and its opening clause 'ثمّ أقبل على أصحابه فقال:' is printed in bold on its own line. 'عائد ومعود' is the visitor of the sick and the one being visited (editor: المعود is the sick person whom people visit). 'بنفسه يجود' literally means 'is generous with his soul', i.e. in the throes of death. 'مسجّى' means covered with a cloth, i.e. laid out dead (editor: مدّ عليه ثوباً يستره). 'وليس بمغفول عنه' means he is not forgotten (by death / by Allah).</sub><br><sub>p. 331</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">فقال له زيد بن صوحان العبدي: يا أمير المؤمنين أيّ سلطان أغلب وأقوى؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): الموت.<br>قال: فأيّ ذلّ أذلّ؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): الحرص على الدنيا.<br>قال: فأيّ فقر أشد؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): الكفر بعد الإيمان.<br>قال: فأيّ دعوة أضلّ؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): الداعي بما لا يكون.<br>قال: فأيّ عمل أفضل؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): التقوى.<br>قال: فأيّ عمل أنجح؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): طلب ما عند الله عزّ وجلّ.<br>[قال: فأيّ صاحب لك شرّ؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): المُزيّن لك معصية الله عزّ وجلّ].<br>قال: فأيّ الخلق أشقى؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): من باع دينه بدنيا غيره.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Then Zayd ibn Ṣūḥān al-ʿAbdī said to him: O Amir al-Mu'minin, which power is most overwhelming and strongest?<br>He (a.s.) said: Death.<br>He said: Then which abasement is most abject?<br>He (a.s.) said: Greed for this world.<br>He said: Then which poverty is most severe?<br>He (a.s.) said: Disbelief after faith.<br>He said: Then which supplication is most misguided?<br>He (a.s.) said: That of the one who supplicates for what will not come to be.<br>He said: Then which deed is best?<br>He (a.s.) said: Taqwa (God-wariness).<br>He said: Then which deed is most successful?<br>He (a.s.) said: Seeking what is with Allah, Mighty and Majestic.<br>[He said: Then which companion is worst for you?<br>He (a.s.) said: The one who makes disobedience to Allah, Mighty and Majestic, look attractive to you.]<br>He said: Then which of creation is most wretched?<br>He (a.s.) said: The one who sells his religion for someone else's worldly gain.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Continues narration [72]; the dialogue lines are separated by line breaks. The editor notes that al-Faqīh has 'الهوى' (desire) where this text has 'الموت' (death). 'أشد' is printed without a shadda. 'دعوة ... الداعي بما لا يكون' can also be read as 'which call (summons) is most misguided? The one who calls to what will not be'. The supplication reading was chosen because دعا بـ usually means 'to pray for'. There is a small stray mark beneath the alif of 'الداعي', possibly a print artifact, and it is not transcribed. The bracketed exchange about the companion is the editor's addition from al-Faqīh and al-Amālī.</sub><br><sub>p. 332</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">قال: فأيّ الخلق أقوى؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): الحليم.<br>قال: فأيّ الخلق أشحّ؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): من أخذ المال من غير حله فجعله [في غير حقّه].<br>قال: فأي الناس أكيس؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): من أبصر رشده من غيّه فمال إلى رشده.<br>قال: فمن أحلم الناس؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): الذي لا يغضب.<br>قال: فأيّ الناس أثبت رأياً؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): من لم تغرّه الناس من نفسه ولم تغرّه الدنيا بتشوّقها.<br>قال: فأيّ الناس أحمق؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): المغترّ بالدنيا وهو يرى ما بها من تقلّب أحوالها.<br>قال: فأيّ الناس أشدّ حسرة؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): الذي حُرِم الدنيا والآخرة ذلك هو الخسران المبين.<br>قال: فأيّ الخلق أعمى؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): الذي عَمِل لغير الله يطلب بعمله الثواب من عند الله عزّ وجلّ.<br>قال: فأيّ القنوع أفضل؟<br>قال (عليه السلام): القانع بما أعطاه الله عزّ وجلّ.</td>
