<td width="50%" valign="top">[99] Al-Ḥasan ibn Faḍḍāl narrated from Ghālib ibn ʿUthmān, from Shuʿayb al-ʿAqarqūfī, from al-Ṣādiq Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad (a.s.), who said: 'Whoever masters himself when he desires and when he fears, when he craves and when he is angry, and then when he is pleased, Allah forbids his body to the Fire.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The print clearly has فإذا رضي ('then when he is pleased'), where one would expect the fifth item of the list to be وإذا رضي. I have translated it as the last item of the list. An alternative reading takes فإذا رضي as the start of a new clause ('and if he is [thus], Allah forbids...'), but that is strained.</sub><br><sub>p. 354</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٠٠] وسئل الصادق (عليه السلام) عن الزّاهد في الدنيا قال: الذي يترك حلالها مخافة حسابه، ويترك حرامها مخافة عذابه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[100] Al-Ṣādiq (a.s.) was asked about the one who renounces this world (al-zāhid). He said: 'The one who leaves its lawful things for fear of being called to account for them, and leaves its unlawful things for fear of being punished for them.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> حسابه / عذابه carry a masculine pronoun. I have referred it to the 'lawful' and 'unlawful' (the reckoning for it, the punishment for it). It could also refer to Allah ('for fear of His reckoning ... His punishment').</sub><br><sub>p. 354</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٠١] وروى محمّد بن سنان، عن عبد الله بن مسكان، عن أبي عبد الله الصادق (عليه السلام) قال: إنّ أحقّ النّاس بأن يتمنى للناس الغنى البخلاء لأنّ الناس إذا استغنوا كفّوا عن أموالهم وإنّ أحقّ الناس بأن يتمنّى للناس الصلاح أهل العيوب، لأن النّاس إذا صلحوا كفّوا عن تتبّع عيوبهم، وأنّ أحقّ الناس بأن يتمنّى للنّاس الحلم أهل السفه الذين يحتاجون أن يعفى عن سفههم، فأصبح أهل البخل يتمنّون فقر الناس، وفي الفقر الحاجة إلى البخيل، وفي الفساد طلب عورة أهل العيوب، وفي السفه المكافاة بالذنوب.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[101] Muḥammad ibn Sinān narrated from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muskān, from Abū ʿAbd Allāh al-Ṣādiq (a.s.), who said: 'The people with the most reason to wish wealth for others are the misers, because when people become rich they leave the misers' wealth alone. The people with the most reason to wish righteousness for others are those who have faults, because when people become righteous they stop hunting out others' faults. And the people with the most reason to wish forbearance for others are the foolish, who need their foolishness to be pardoned. Yet the misers have come to wish for people's poverty, when in poverty there is need of the miser; [and those with faults wish for corruption, when] in corruption there is the seeking out of the shameful secrets of those with faults; [and the foolish wish for foolishness, when] in foolishness there is requital for offences.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This narration runs from the foot of page 354 onto 355. كفّوا عن أموالهم is literally 'they refrain from their wealth'. The pronoun most naturally refers to the misers' wealth (people no longer ask them for it). The bracketed words in the last sentence spell out the implied parallel and are not in the Arabic. Literally it reads: 'So the misers came to wish for people's poverty, and in poverty is need of the miser, and in corruption is seeking the ʿawra (shameful secrets) of those with faults, and in foolishness is requital for offences.' المكافاة بالذنوب could also mean 'being repaid in kind for one's offences'.</sub><br><sub>p. 354</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٠٢] وروي عن أبي هاشم الجعفري أنّه قال: أصابتني ضيقة شديدة فصرت إلى أبي الحسن عليّ بن محمّد فاستأذنت عليه فأذن لي، فلمّا جلست قال: يا أبا هاشم، أي نِعم الله عليك تريد أن تؤدّي شكرها؟ قال أبو هاشم: فوجمت فلم أدر ما أقول له فابتدأني (عليه السلام) فقال: إنّ الله عزّوجلّ رزقك الإيمان فحرّم به بدنك على النّار ورزقك العافية [فأعانك على الطاعة، ورزقك القنوع فصانك عن التبذّل. يا أبا هاشم، إنّما ابتدأتك بهذا لأنّي ظننت أنّك تريد أن تشكو لي مَن فعل بك هذا، قد أمرت لك بمائة دينار فخذها.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[102] It is narrated that Abū Hāshim al-Jaʿfarī said: I was struck by severe hardship, so I went to Abū al-Ḥasan ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad, asked permission to come in to him, and he gave me permission. When I sat down he said: 'O Abū Hāshim, which of Allah's blessings upon you do you wish to render thanks for?' Abū Hāshim said: I was struck silent and did not know what to say to him. So he (a.s.) spoke first and said: 'Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has provided you with faith, and by it has forbidden your body to the Fire. He has provided you with well-being, and has thereby helped you to obey Him. And He has provided you with contentment, and has thereby kept you from demeaning yourself (tabadhdhul, i.e. degrading oneself by begging from people). O Abū Hāshim, I began with this only because I thought you wanted to complain to me about the One who did this to you. I have ordered a hundred dinars for you, so take them.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Abū al-Ḥasan ʿAlī ibn Muḥammad is Imam al-Hādī. No honorific is printed after his name at first mention, but (عليه السلام) follows فابتدأني. An opening square bracket is printed before فأعانك and is not closed until the end of narration 107 on page 357 ('...رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله)]'). The editor's footnote on 357 says that the passage between the brackets (about two pages) had fallen out of the base manuscript and was restored from al-Faqīh and Biḥār al-Anwār. So the text from 'فأعانك' through the end of 107 is editorially restored. The brackets are kept as printed. 'The One who did this to you': من فعل بك هذا could mean Allah, who decreed the hardship (so the complaint would be against Him, and the Imam reminds him of his blessings first). It could also mean whoever caused his hardship. The capitalised reading is the more common understanding, but the other is possible. أي is printed without shadda.</sub><br><sub>p. 355</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٠٣] وروى محمّد بن سنان، عن طلحة بن زيد قال: سمعت أبا عبد الله الصادق (عليه السلام) يقول: العامل على غير بصيرة كالسائر على غير الطريق فلا تزيده سرعة السير من الطريق إلّا بُعداً.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[103] Muḥammad ibn Sinān narrated from Ṭalḥa ibn Zayd, who said: I heard Abū ʿAbd Allāh al-Ṣādiq (a.s.) say: 'One who acts without insight is like one who travels off the road: the faster he goes, the farther he gets from the road.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Literally: 'the speed of travel only increases him in distance from the road.'</sub><br><sub>p. 355</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٠٤] وقال الصادق (عليه السلام): والنوم راحة للجسد، والنطق راحة للرّوح، والسكوت راحة للعقل.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[104] Al-Ṣādiq (a.s.) said: '...And sleep is rest for the body, speech is rest for the spirit, and silence is rest for the intellect.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The quoted saying begins with 'و' (والنوم) as printed. This suggests it is an excerpt from a longer statement, so I marked it with an ellipsis.</sub><br><sub>p. 356</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٠٥] وروى محمّد بن سنان، عن المفضّل بن عمر قال: قال الصادق جعفر بن محمّد (عليهما السلام): من لم يكن له واعظٌ من قلبه وزاجر من نفسه، ولم يكن له قرين مرشد استمكن عدوّه من عنقه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[105] Muḥammad ibn Sinān narrated from al-Mufaḍḍal ibn ʿUmar, who said: al-Ṣādiq Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad (a.s.) said: 'Whoever has no admonisher from his own heart and no restrainer from his own self, and has no rightly guiding companion, his enemy gets a firm grip on his neck.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'His enemy' is unspecified. It is commonly understood as Satan, and could also include the lower self.</sub><br><sub>p. 356</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٠٦] وروى جعفر بن محمّد بن مالك الفزاريّ الكوفيّ قال: حدّثنا جعفر بن محمّد بن سهل، عن سعيد بن محمّد، عن مسعدة قال: قال لي أبو الحسن موسى بن جعفر (عليهما السلام): إنّ عيال الرجل أسراؤه، فمن أنعم الله عليه نعمة فليوسّع على اسرائه، فإن لم يفعل أوشك أن تزول تلك النعمة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[106] Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad ibn Mālik al-Fazārī al-Kūfī narrated, saying: Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad ibn Sahl narrated to us from Saʿīd ibn Muḥammad, from Masʿada, who said: Abū al-Ḥasan Mūsā ibn Jaʿfar (a.s.) said to me: 'A man's dependents are his captives. So whoever Allah has favoured with a blessing, let him be generous in providing for his captives. If he does not, that blessing is liable to pass away.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> فليوسّع على is literally 'let him make [provision] ample for'. اسرائه is printed without hamza on the alif, while أسراؤه earlier in the sentence has it.</sub><br><sub>p. 356</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٠٧] وروى صفوان بن يحيى، عن أبي الصباح الكناني قال: قلت للصادق جعفر بن محمّد (عليهما السلام): أخبرني عن هذا القول قول من هو؟ «أسأل الله الايمان والتقوى، وأعوذ بالله من شر عاقبة الأمور، إنّ أشرف الحديث ذكر الله تعالى، ورأس الحكمة طاعته، وأصدق القول وابلغ الموعظة وأحسن القصص كتاب الله، وأوثق العرى الايمان بالله وخير الملل ملّة إبراهيم (عليه السلام)، وأحسن السنن سنّة الأنبياء، وأحسن الهدى هدى محمّد، وخير الزّاد التقوى، وخير العلم ما نفع، وخير الهدى ما اتّبع، وخير الغنى غنى النفس، وخير ما ألقي في القلب اليقين، وزينة الحديث الصدق، وزينة العلم الإحسان، وأشرف الموت قتل الشهادة، وخير الأمور خيرها عاقبة، وما قلّ وكفى خيرٌ ممّا كثر وألهى، والشقيّ من شقي في بطن أمّه، والسعيد من وعظ بغيره، وأكيس الكيس التقى، وأحمق الحمق الفجور، وشرّ الروايا روايا الكذب، وشرّ الأمور محدثاتها، وشرّ العمى عمى القلب، وشرّ الندامة ندامة يوم القيامة، وأعظم المخطئين عند الله عزّ وجلّ لسان الكذّاب، وشرّ الكسب الرّبا، وشرّ المآكل أكل مال اليتيم ظلماً، وأحسن زينة الرّجل السكينة مع الإيمان، ومن تتبّع المَشمَعة يشمّع الله به، ومن يعرف البلاء يصبر عليه، ومن لا يعرفه ينكره، والرّيب كفر، ومن يستكبر يضعه الله، ومن يطع الشيطان يعص الله، ومن يعص الله يعذّبه الله، ومن يشكره يزده الله، ومن يصبر على الرزيّة يغنيه الله، ومن يتوكّل على الله فحسبه الله، ومن يتوكل على الله يؤجره الله، لا تسخطوا الله برضا أحد من خلقه، ولا تتقرّبوا إلى أحد من الخلق بتباعد من الله، فإنّ الله عزّوجلّ ليس بينه وبين أحد من الخلق شيء فيعطيه به خيراً أو يصرف به عنه سوءاً إلّا بطاعته وابتغاء مرضاته، إنّ طاعة الله تبارك وتعالى نجاح كلّ خير يبتغى ونجاة من كلّ شرّ يتّقى، وإنّ الله عزّ وجلّ يعصم من أطاعه، ولا يعتصم منه من عصاه، ولا يجد الهارب من الله مهرباً فإنّ أمر الله تعالى ذكره نازل باذلاله ولو كره الخلائق، وكلّما هو آت قريب، ما شاء الله كان، وما لم يشأ لم يكن، تعاونوا على البرّ والتقوى ولا تعاونوا على الإثم والعدوان واتّقوا الله إنّ الله شديد العقاب». فقال الصادق جعفر بن محمّد (عليهما السلام): هذا قول رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله)].</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[107] Ṣafwān ibn Yaḥyā narrated from Abū al-Ṣabbāḥ al-Kinānī, who said: I said to al-Ṣādiq Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad (a.s.): 'Tell me about this saying: whose saying is it? «I ask Allah for faith and God-wariness (taqwā), and I seek refuge in Allah from the evil of how affairs end. The noblest discourse is the remembrance of Allah, the Exalted, and the head of wisdom is obedience to Him. The truest speech, the most eloquent admonition and the best of narratives is the Book of Allah. The firmest of handholds is faith in Allah. The best of creeds is the creed (milla) of Ibrāhīm (a.s.). The best of ways (sunan) is the way of the prophets, and the best of guidance is the guidance of Muḥammad. The best provision is God-wariness. The best knowledge is what benefits, and the best guidance is what is followed. The best wealth is the self-sufficiency of the soul. The best thing cast into the heart is certainty. The adornment of speech is truthfulness, and the adornment of knowledge is excellent conduct (iḥsān). The noblest death is being slain as a martyr. The best of affairs is the one best in outcome. What is little and suffices is better than what is much and distracts. The wretched one is he who was wretched in his mother's womb, and the fortunate one is he who takes admonition from others. The shrewdest shrewdness is piety, and the most foolish folly is licentiousness (fujūr). The worst of contrivances are the contrivances of lying. The worst of affairs are the newly invented ones. The worst blindness is blindness of the heart. The worst regret is regret on the Day of Resurrection. The gravest of wrongdoers in the sight of Allah, Mighty and Majestic, is the tongue of the liar. The worst earning is usury (ribā). The worst of eating is consuming the property of the orphan unjustly. The finest adornment of a man is tranquillity together with faith. Whoever goes after mockery, Allah will make a mockery of him. Whoever recognises affliction [for what it is] bears it patiently, and whoever does not recognise it rejects it. Doubt is unbelief. Whoever is arrogant, Allah brings him low. Whoever obeys Satan disobeys Allah, and whoever disobeys Allah, Allah punishes him. Whoever thanks Him, Allah gives him more. Whoever is patient under calamity, Allah enriches him. Whoever relies on Allah, Allah suffices him; and whoever relies on Allah, Allah rewards him. Do not anger Allah to please any of His creatures, and do not seek nearness to any of the creatures by distancing yourselves from Allah. For there is nothing between Allah, Mighty and Majestic, and any of the creatures by which He grants him good or turns evil away from him, except obedience to Him and seeking His good pleasure. Obedience to Allah, Blessed and Exalted, is success in every good that is sought and deliverance from every evil that is guarded against. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, protects whoever obeys Him, and whoever disobeys Him finds no protection from Him. The one who flees from Allah finds no place to flee to, for the command of Allah, exalted be His mention, descends upon him to humble him, even if all creatures dislike it. Everything that is coming is near. What Allah wills comes to be, and what He does not will does not come to be. Cooperate in righteousness and God-wariness, and do not cooperate in sin and aggression, and be wary of Allah; indeed Allah is severe in punishment [Q 5:2].»' Then al-Ṣādiq Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad (a.s.) said: 'This is the saying of the Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.).'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This narration spans pages 356–357. The closing ']' after (صلى الله عليه وآله) ends the restored passage that opens in 102 (see note there); it is kept in the Arabic. Individual items: (1) 'the best provision is God-wariness' echoes Q 2:197, 'the best of narratives' echoes Q 12:3, and 'whoever relies on Allah, Allah suffices him' echoes Q 65:3. The closing sentence is Q 5:2. (2) الروايا: the editor's footnote glosses it as the plural of rawiyya, 'what a person deliberates within himself of word or deed'. I have rendered it 'contrivances'. Another common reading is 'the worst narrations are lying narrations'. (3) محدثاتها: the editor glosses it as innovations in religion, i.e. whatever did not exist in the time of the Prophet and the Imams. (4) المَشمَعة (printed with shīn and fatḥas) and يشمّع: the editor glosses mashmaʿa as joking and laughter, here meaning mocking and ridiculing people. So 'whoever makes a practice of mocking people, Allah will make him a laughing-stock'. (5) 'Whoever recognises affliction': the editor's footnote says this means recognising the reward that follows it, or recognising that it is from Allah, who wills only what is best. (6) The two consecutive clauses 'whoever relies on Allah, Allah suffices him; and whoever relies on Allah, Allah rewards him (يؤجره)' are printed exactly so. The repetition of يتوكل looks like a possible textual corruption (e.g. of 'whoever lends to Allah'), but I have translated what is printed. (7) نازل باذلاله is printed without hamza. I read it as bi-idhlālihi, 'descends to humble/abase him'; the pronoun could also refer back to the command itself. (8) أعظم المخطئين: 'the gravest of those who err/sin'. (9) يغنيه is printed with yāʾ where the jussive would be يغنه. (10) 'Being slain as a martyr' renders قتل الشهادة, literally 'the killing of martyrdom'.</sub><br><sub>p. 356</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٠٨] وقال رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله): قال الله جلّ جلاله: أيّما عبد أطاعني لم أكله إلى غيري، وأيّما عبد عصاني وكلته إلى نفسه ثمّ لم أبال في أيّ واد هلك.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[108] The Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.) said: Allah, Majestic is His majesty, said: 'Any servant who obeys Me, I do not leave him to anyone other than Me. And any servant who disobeys Me, I leave him to himself, and then I do not care in which valley he perishes.'<br><sub>p. 358</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٠٩] وروي محمّد بن أبي عمير، عن عيسى الفرّاء، عن عبد الله بن أبي يعفور قال: سمعت أبا عبد الله (عليه السلام) يقول: قال أبو جعفر الباقر (عليه السلام): من كان ظاهره أرجح من باطنه خفّ ميزانه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[109] Muḥammad ibn Abī ʿUmayr narrated from ʿĪsā al-Farrāʾ, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Yaʿfūr, who said: I heard Abū ʿAbd Allāh (a.s.) say: Abū Jaʿfar al-Bāqir (a.s.) said: 'Whoever's outward [self] outweighs his inward [self], his scale is light.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The verb is printed وروي (with dotted yāʾ, which would be passive, 'it was narrated'), but a nominative narrator's name follows. It is almost certainly وروى ('narrated'), as in the neighbouring entries, and is translated so. ظاهره/باطنه: his outward conduct/appearance versus his inner reality.</sub><br><sub>p. 358</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١١٠] وقال رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله): قال الله جلّ جلاله: إذا عصاني من خلقي من يعرفني سلّطت عليه من خلقي من لا يعرفني.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[110] The Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.) said: Allah, Majestic is His majesty, said: 'When one of My creatures who knows Me disobeys Me, I give power over him to one of My creatures who does not know Me.'<br><sub>p. 358</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١١١] وروى ابن أبي عمير، عن إسحاق بن عمّار قال: قال الصادق (عليه السلام): يا إسحاق صانع المنافق بلسانك، واخلص ودّك للمؤمن، وإن جالسك يهودي فاحسن مجالسته.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[111] Ibn Abī ʿUmayr narrated from Isḥāq ibn ʿAmmār, who said: al-Ṣādiq (a.s.) said: 'O Isḥāq, humour the hypocrite with your tongue, make your affection sincere for the believer, and if a Jew sits with you, be good company to him.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> صانع: to treat courteously and diplomatically, to humour, i.e. deal with outward civility. واخلص and فاحسن are printed without hamza.</sub><br><sub>p. 358</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١١٢] وروى المفضّل بن عمر، عن الصادق جعفر بن محمّد (عليهما السلام)، عن أبيه، عن جدّه (عليهم السلام) قال: قيل للحسين بن عليّ (عليه السلام): كيف أصبحت يابن رسول الله؟ قال: أصبحت ولي ربّ فوقي والنار أمامي، والموت يطلبني والحساب محدّق بي، وأنا مرتهن بعملي، لا آخذ ما أحبّ ولا أدفع ما أكره، والأمور بيد غيري، فإن شاء عذّبني وإن شاء عفا عنّي، فأيّ فقير أفقر منّي.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[112] Al-Mufaḍḍal ibn ʿUmar narrated from al-Ṣādiq Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad (a.s.), from his father, from his grandfather (a.s.), who said: It was said to al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī (a.s.): 'How have you begun your morning, O son of the Messenger of Allah?' He said: 'I have begun my morning with a Lord above me and the Fire before me, with death seeking me and the Reckoning surrounding me, and I am held in pledge for my deeds. I cannot take what I love, nor ward off what I dislike, and affairs are in the hand of Another: if He wills He punishes me, and if He wills He pardons me. So what poor man is poorer than I?'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This narration spans pages 358–359. 'His grandfather' is Imam ʿAlī Zayn al-ʿĀbidīn. The ligature after جدّه looks like the plural (عليهم السلام), the same as after آبائه in 116, so it may be meant to cover the whole chain. The ligature after الحسين بن عليّ is the singular. Editor's footnotes on the printed wording: the base manuscript reads والحسنات ('and good deeds') where the printed text, following al-Faqīh and the other sources, has والحساب. Al-Faqīh reads لا أجد ('I do not find') for لا آخذ ('I do not take'). يابن is printed as one word (يا ابن). 'In the hand of Another' renders بيد غيري ('in the hand of other than me').</sub><br><sub>p. 358</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١١٣] وروى المفضّل، عن الصادق (عليه السلام) إنّه قال: وقع بين سلمان الفارسي رحمة الله عليه وبين رجل خصومة فقال الرجل لسلمان: من أنت وما أنت؟ فقال سلمان: أما أوّلي وأوّلك فنطفة قذرة، وأما آخري وآخرك فجيفة منتنة، فإذا كان يوم القيامة ونصبت الموازين فمن ثقلت موازينه فهو الكريم، ومن خفّت موازينه فهو اللّئيم.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[113] Al-Mufaḍḍal narrated from al-Ṣādiq (a.s.) that he said: A dispute arose between Salmān al-Fārisī (may Allah have mercy on him) and a man, and the man said to Salmān: 'Who are you, and what are you?' Salmān said: 'As for my beginning and yours, it is a filthy drop of sperm; and as for my end and yours, it is a stinking corpse. Then, when the Day of Resurrection comes and the scales are set up, whoever's scales are heavy is the noble one, and whoever's scales are light is the base one.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'Heavy scales / light scales' echoes the Quranic imagery of Q 7:8–9, Q 23:102–103 and Q 101:6–8 (not a direct quotation). إنّه is printed with hamza below the alif.</sub><br><sub>p. 359</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">قال المفضّل: وسمعت الصادق (عليه السلام) يقول: بليّة الناس علينا عظيمة إن دعوناهم لم يجيبونا، وإن تركناهم لم يهتدوا بغيرنا.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Al-Mufaḍḍal said: And I heard al-Ṣādiq (a.s.) say: 'Our trial with people is a great one: if we call them, they do not answer us, and if we leave them, they will not be guided by anyone other than us.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This is an unnumbered continuation after 113, with its own source footnote, so I made it a separate segment. بليّة الناس علينا is literally 'the affliction of the people upon us', i.e. the burden that people place on us.</sub><br><sub>p. 359</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١١٤] وقال أمير المؤمنين (عليه السلام): جمع الخير كلّه في ثلاث خصال: النظر والسكوت والكلام، فكلّ نظر ليس فيه اعتبار فهو السهو، وكلّ كلام ليس فيه ذكر فهو لهو، وكلّ سكوت ليس فيه فكرة فهو غفلة، فطوبى لمن كان نظره عبراً، وسكوته فكراً، وكلامه ذكراً، وبكى على خطيئته، وأمن الناس شرّه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[114] Amir al-Mu'minin (a.s.) said: 'All good has been gathered in three traits: looking, silence and speech. Every look in which there is no taking of lessons is inattention; every speech in which there is no remembrance [of Allah] is idle diversion; and every silence in which there is no reflection is heedlessness. So blessed is the one whose looking is [the taking of] lessons, whose silence is reflection and whose speech is remembrance, who weeps over his sin, and from whose evil people are safe.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> جمع could also be vocalised as the passive jumiʿa ('has been gathered', as translated) or the verbal noun jamʿ ('the sum of all good is in three traits'); the sense is the same. عبراً is the plural of ʿibra, 'lessons/admonitions'. The editor notes that al-Faqīh reads لغو ('idle talk') for لهو ('diversion'). السهو: 'distraction, inattention'.</sub><br><sub>p. 359</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وقال الصادق (عليه السلام): أوحى الله عزّ وجلّ إلى آدم: يا آدم إني أجمع لك الخير كلّه في أربع كلمات واحدة لي وواحدة لك، وواحدة فيما بيني وبينك، وواحدة فيما بينك وبين النّاس، فأمّا التي لي: فتعبّدني لا تشرك [بي] شيئاً، وأمّا التي لك: فأجازيك بعملك أحوج ما تكون إليه، وأمّا التي فيما بيني وبينك: فعليك الدعاء وعليّ الإجابة، وأما التي فيما بينك وبين الناس فترضى للناس ما ترضى لنفسك.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Al-Ṣādiq (a.s.) said: Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed to Ādam: 'O Ādam, I shall gather all good for you in four words: one for Me, one for you, one between Me and you, and one between you and people. As for the one that is for Me: that you worship Me, associating nothing [with Me]. As for the one that is for you: I shall recompense you for your deeds when you are most in need of it. As for the one between Me and you: yours is to supplicate, and Mine is to answer. And as for the one between you and people: that you be content for people with what you are content with for yourself.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Unnumbered in the print (it follows 114 without a number). The bracketed [بي] ('with Me') was supplied by the editor from al-Faqīh, and the brackets are kept. أحوج ما تكون إليه: 'at the time you are most in need of it', i.e. on the Day of Judgement.</sub><br><sub>p. 360</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١١٥] وقال الصادق (عليه السلام): العافية نعمة خفية، إذا وجدت نسيت وإذا فقدت ذكرت.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[115] Al-Ṣādiq (a.s.) said: 'Well-being is a hidden blessing: when it is present it is forgotten, and when it is lost it is remembered.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> وجدت/نسيت/فقدت/ذكرت are unvocalised. I read them as passives with 'well-being' as subject. Another possible reading is second person: 'when you have it you forget [it], and when you lose it you remember [it]'. The meaning is the same.</sub><br><sub>p. 360</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١١٦] وروى السكوني، عن جعفر بن محمّد، عن أبيه، عن آبائه (عليهم السلام) قال: قال رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله): كلمتان غريبتان فاحتملوها كلمة حكمة من سفيه فاقبلوها وكلمة سفه من حكيم فاغفروها.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[116] Al-Sakūnī narrated from Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad, from his father, from his forefathers (a.s.), who said: The Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.) said: 'Two strange words — bear with them: a word of wisdom from a fool, accept it; and a word of folly from a wise man, forgive it.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This narration ends complete on page 360, so there was no need to read page 361.</sub><br><sub>p. 360</sub></td>
</tr>
</table>


## From the Counsels of Imam al-Bāqir (a.s.)

<p dir="rtl" lang="ar"><b>من وصايا الإمام الباقر (عليه السلام)</b></p>

<table>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١١٧] وروى عمر بن شمر، عن جابر بن يزيد الجعفي، عن أبي جعفر محمّد بن عليّ الباقر، عن أبيه، عن جدّه (عليهم السلام) أنّ أمير المؤمنين سلام الله عليه قال في خطبة خطبها بعد موت النبيّ (صلى الله عليه وآله):<br>أيّها الناس، إنه لا شرف أعلى من الإسلام، ولا كرم أعلى من التقوى، ولا معقل أحرز من الورع، ولا شفيع أنجح من التوبة، ولا كنز أنفع من العلم، ولا عزّ أرفع من الحلم، ولا حسب أبلغ من الأدب، ولا نصب أوضع من الغضب، ولا جمال أزين من العقل، ولا سوءة أسوء من الكذب، ولا حافظ أحفظ من الصمت، ولا لباس أجمل من العافية، ولا غائب أقرب من الموت.<br>أيّها الناس، إنه من مشى على وجه الأرض فإنه يصير إلى بطنها، والليل والنهار مسرعان في هدم الأعمار، ولكلّ ذي رمق قوّة، ولكلّ حبّة أكلٌ [؟]، وأنت قوت الموت، وإن من عرف الأيام لن يغفل عن الاستعداد، لن ينجو عن الموت غنيٌّ بماله، ولا فقير لإقلاله.<br>أيّها الناس، مَن خاف ربّه كفّ ظلمه، ومن لم يرع في كلامه أظهر هجره، ومن لم يعرف الخير من الشرّ فهو بمنزلة البهيم، ما أصغر المصيبة مع عظم الفاقة غد! وهيهات هيهات وما تناكرتم إلاّ لما فيكم من المعاصي والذنوب، فما أقرب الراحة من التعب، والبؤس من النعيم، وما شرّ بشرّ بعده الجنّة، وما خير بخير بعده النّار، وكلّ نعيم دون الجنّة محقور، وكلّ بلاء دون النار عافية.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">ʿUmar ibn Shimr narrated from Jābir ibn Yazīd al-Juʿfī, from Abū Jaʿfar Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī al-Bāqir, from his father, from his grandfather (a.s.), that Amir al-Mu'minin, peace of Allah be upon him, said in a sermon he delivered after the death of the Prophet (s.a.w.):<br>O people, there is no honour higher than Islam, no nobility higher than God-wariness (taqwā), no stronghold more secure than scrupulous piety (waraʿ), no intercessor more successful than repentance, no treasure more beneficial than knowledge, no might more exalted than forbearance, no noble standing (ḥasab) more far-reaching than good conduct (adab), no rank more degrading than anger, no beauty more adorning than the intellect, no vileness viler than lying, no guardian more protective than silence, no garment more beautiful than well-being, and nothing absent nearer than death.<br>O people, whoever walks on the face of the earth will end up in its belly. Night and day are hastening to demolish lifespans. Every creature with a spark of life has its strength, every grain has its eater, and you are the food of death. Whoever knows the days will not neglect preparation. No rich man will escape death by his wealth, nor a poor man by his want.<br>O people, whoever fears his Lord holds back his wrongdoing; whoever does not guard his speech exposes his foul talk; and whoever cannot tell good from evil is like a beast. How small the calamity is beside the magnitude of need tomorrow! How far, how far! You have become estranged from one another only because of the sins and wrongdoings within you. How near rest is to toil, and misery to bliss! No evil is truly evil if Paradise comes after it, and no good is truly good if the Fire comes after it. Every bliss short of Paradise is contemptible, and every affliction short of the Fire is well-being.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> (1) The honorific after جدّه is a small ligature. I read it as the plural (عليهم السلام), which covers father and grandfather. (2) ولكلّ ذي رمق قوّة: the print clearly has قوّة (strength). But the parallel with وأنت قوت الموت ('you are the food of death') suggests قوت (sustenance) was meant: 'every living thing has its sustenance'. I translated the printed word. (3) حبّة أكلٌ: the mark over the alif looks like a hamza (أكل), but the sense needs آكِل ('eater'), which I followed. A madda may be rendered small in this font. (4) ولا نصب أوضع من الغضب: printed نصب, which I rendered 'rank' (naṣb/manṣib, station). A reading نَسَب would give 'no lineage more debasing than anger'. (5) من لم يرع في كلامه أظهر هجره: يرع is from رعى (to heed or guard). هُجر is obscene or senseless talk. (6) غد!: the print appears to have غد with no tanween alif (غداً expected). (7) ما أصغر المصيبة مع عظم الفاقة غد[اً]: possibly 'how small is [today's] calamity compared with the great destitution of tomorrow'. (8) تناكرتم: 'disowned or estranged one another'. It could also mean 'you have come to find [the truth] strange'. (9) Footnotes on this page record variants from al-Faqīh: أعز for the first أعلى, and أزين (in al-Faqīh) against أزيد (in the original manuscript).</sub><br><sub>p. 361</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١١٨] وفي رواية إسماعيل بن مسلم قال: قال رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله): ثلاث أخافهن على أمّتي من بعدي: الضلالة بعد الهدى، ومضلاّت الفتن، وشهوة البطن والفرج.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">In the narration of Ismāʿīl ibn Muslim, he said: The Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.) said: There are three things I fear for my community after me: going astray after guidance, the misleading trials (fitan, seditions), and the appetite of the belly and the private parts.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor's footnote notes that all sources except al-Faqīh read المعرفة ('knowledge') in place of الهدى ('guidance').</sub><br><sub>p. 362</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١١٩] ومرّ رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله) بقوم يتشائلون حجراً فقال:<br>ما هذا وما يدعوكم إليه؟<br>قالوا: لنعرف أشدّنا وأقوانا.<br>قال: أفلا أدلّكم على أشدّكم وأقواكم؟<br>قالوا: بلى يا رسول الله.<br>قال: أشدّكم وأقواكم الذي إذا رضي لم يدخله رضاه في إثم ولا باطل، وإذا سخط لم يخرجه سخطه من قول الحقّ، وإذا ملك لم يتعاط ما ليس له.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">The Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.) passed by some people who were lifting a stone [in a contest of strength], and he said: What is this, and what prompts you to do it?<br>They said: So that we may know who is the strongest and mightiest among us.<br>He said: Shall I not show you who is the strongest and mightiest of you?<br>They said: Yes indeed, O Messenger of Allah.<br>He said: The strongest and mightiest of you is the one who, when he is pleased, is not led by his pleasure into sin or falsehood; when he is angry, is not taken by his anger out of speaking the truth; and when he gains power, does not reach for what is not his.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> يتشائلون (printed with hamza on a yāʾ seat) is from شال/يشيل, 'to lift', and describes a contest of lifting a stone. The editor's footnote gives يربعون from Maʿānī al-Akhbār and al-Biḥār, with the same meaning. إذا ملك: 'when he has power or possession', also 'when he is in authority'.</sub><br><sub>p. 362</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وفي خبر آخر: إذا لم يتعاط ما ليس له بحقّ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">In another report: '... when he does not reach for what is not rightfully his.'<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This is a variant wording for the last clause of the preceding narration [119], with its own source footnote. I kept it as a separate segment.</sub><br><sub>p. 362</sub></td>
</tr>
</table>


## From the Admonitions and Wise Sayings of the Imams (a.s.)

<p dir="rtl" lang="ar"><b>من مواعظ وحكم الأئمّة (عليهم السلام)</b></p>

<table>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٢٠] روى الحسن بن محبوب، عن أبي ولاّد الحنّاط قال: سألت أبا عبد الله الصادق جعفر بن محمّد (عليه السلام) عن قول الله عزّ وجلّ: ﴿وَبِالْوَالِدَيْنِ إِحْسَانًا﴾ ما هذا الإحسان؟<br>فقال: الإحسان أن تحسن صحبتهما، وأن لا يكلّفا أن يسألاك عن شيئاً ممّا يحتاجان إليه إن كانا مستغنين، إنّ الله عزّ وجلّ يقول: ﴿لَنْ تَنَالُوا الْبِرَّ حَتَّى تُنْفِقُوا مِمَّا تُحِبُّونَ﴾.<br>ثم قال (عليه السلام): ﴿إِمَّا يَبْلُغَنَّ عِنْدَكَ الْكِبَرَ أَحَدُهُمَا أَوْ كِلاَهُمَا فَلاَ تَقُلْ لَهُمَا أُفٍّ ـ إن أضجراك ـ وَلاَ تَنْهَرْهُمَا ـ إن ضرباك ـ وَقُلْ لَهُمَا قَوْلاً كَرِيمًا ـ والقول الكريم أن تقول لهم: غفر الله لكما فذاك منك قول كريم ـ وَاخْفِضْ لَهُمَا جَنَاحَ الذُّلِّ مِنَ الرَّحْمَةِ﴾، وهو أن لا تملأ عينيك من النظر، وتنظر إليهما برحمة ورأفة، وأن لا ترفع صوتك فوق أصواتهما، ولا يدك فوق أيديهما، ولا تتقدّم قدامهما.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Al-Ḥasan ibn Maḥbūb narrated from Abū Wallād al-Ḥannāṭ, who said: I asked Abū ʿAbd Allāh al-Ṣādiq Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad (a.s.) about the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic: {and [be] good to parents} [Q 17:23]. What is this goodness (iḥsān)?<br>He said: Goodness is that you keep good company with them, and that they are not burdened with having to ask you for anything they need, [even] if they are self-sufficient. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says: {You will never attain righteousness until you spend from what you love} [Q 3:92].<br>Then he (a.s.) said: {If one or both of them reach old age in your care, do not say to them 'uff'}, if they vex you, {and do not scold them}, if they strike you, {and speak to them a noble word}. The noble word is that you say to them, 'May Allah forgive you both'; that is a noble word from you. {And lower to them the wing of humility out of mercy} [Q 17:23–24]: that is, you do not fill your eyes with staring [at them], but look at them with mercy and tenderness; you do not raise your voice above their voices, nor your hand above their hands; and you do not walk ahead of them.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> (1) The Imam inserts glosses between the Qurʾanic phrases, set off by dashes in the print: إن أضجراك, إن ضرباك, and the explanation of القول الكريم. In the English they sit outside the {} braces. (2) أن يسألاك عن شيئاً: printed with عن followed by an accusative شيئاً, which is ungrammatical. The sense is 'that they ask you for something'. (3) إن كانا مستغنين has no preceding و, so it literally reads 'if they are self-sufficient'. I added [even] because that is clearly the intended sense (do not make them ask, even when they have means). This is interpretive. (4) أن تقول لهم: the print has the plural لهم, not the dual لهما. (5) The editor's footnote cites the second verse as Āl ʿImrān 3:93. In standard numbering it is 3:92. (6) وهو أن لا تملأ عينيك من النظر: literally 'not fill your eyes from looking', i.e. not to stare hard at them.</sub><br><sub>p. 363</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٢١] روى الحسن بن محبوب، عن مالك بن عطيّة، عن عابد الأحمسي، عن أبي حمزة الثّمالي قال: قال زين العابدين عليّ بن الحسين (عليه السلام): ألا إنّ أحبّكم إلى الله عزّ وجلّ أحسنكم عملاً، [وإنّ أعظمكم عند الله حظّاً] أعظمكم فيما عند الله رغبة، وإنّ أنجى الناس من عذاب الله أشدّهم لله خشية، وإنّ أقربكم إلى الله أوسعكم خلقاً، وإن أرضاكم عند الله أسبغكم على عياله، وإن أكرمكم عند الله أتقاكم.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Al-Ḥasan ibn Maḥbūb narrated from Mālik ibn ʿAṭiyya, from ʿĀbid al-Aḥmasī, from Abū Ḥamza al-Thumālī, who said: Zayn al-ʿĀbidīn ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn (a.s.) said: Indeed, the most beloved of you to Allah, Mighty and Majestic, is the best of you in deeds; [the greatest of you in share with Allah] is the greatest of you in longing for what is with Allah; the one among people most saved from Allah's punishment is the one who fears Allah most intensely; the nearest of you to Allah is the most expansive of you in character; the most pleasing of you to Allah is the one who provides most amply for his dependants; and the noblest of you with Allah is the most God-wary of you.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> (1) The bracketed phrase [وإنّ أعظمكم عند الله حظّاً] is the editor's insertion from al-Faqīh. (2) The editor's footnote gives عائذ for the narrator name, which would make him ʿĀʾidh al-Aḥmasī, against عابد in the original. (3) أسبغكم على عياله is the editor's reading from al-Faqīh. The original manuscript has أوسعكم ('the most generous to his family'). (4) أوسعكم خلقاً: 'broadest in character', i.e. the most good-natured and tolerant.</sub><br><sub>p. 364</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٢٢] روى الحسن بن محبوب، عن سعد بن أبي خلف، عن أبي الحسن موسى بن جعفر (عليه السلام) أنّه قال لبعض ولده:<br>يا بُنيّ، إيّاك أن يراك الله عزّ وجلّ في معصية نهاك عنها، وإيّاك أن يفقدك الله تعالى عند طاعة أمرك بها، وعليك بالجدّ، ولا تخرجنّ نفسك عن التقصير من عبادة الله، فإنّ الله عزّ وجلّ لا يعبد حقّ عبادته، وإيّاك والمزاح فإنّه يذهب بنور إيمانك ويستخفّ بمروّتك، وإيّاك والكسل والضجر فإنّهما يمنعاك حظّك من الدنيا والآخرة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Al-Ḥasan ibn Maḥbūb narrated from Saʿd ibn Abī Khalaf, from Abū al-Ḥasan Mūsā ibn Jaʿfar (a.s.), that he said to one of his sons:<br>My dear son, beware lest Allah, Mighty and Majestic, see you in a sin He has forbidden you, and beware lest Allah the Exalted find you absent from an act of obedience He has commanded you. Hold to earnest effort, and never consider yourself free of falling short in the worship of Allah, for Allah, Mighty and Majestic, is not worshipped as He truly deserves. Beware of joking, for it takes away the light of your faith and cheapens your dignity (muruwwa, manly honour). Beware of laziness and restless impatience, for both deprive you of your share of this world and the Hereafter.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> (1) ولا تخرجنّ نفسك عن التقصير: literally 'do not take yourself out of [the category of] shortcoming', i.e. never think you have done enough. (2) الضجر: irritability, weariness, or boredom with effort. (3) يمنعاك is printed without the nūn (يمنعانك).</sub><br><sub>p. 364</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٢٣] روى عليّ بن الحكم، عن هشام بن سالم، عن الصادق جعفر بن محمّد (عليه السلام) قال: الدنيا طالبة ومطلوبة، فمن طلب الدنيا طلبه الموت حتى يخرجه منها، ومن طلب الآخرة طلبته الدنيا، حتى توفيه رزقه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥakam narrated from Hishām ibn Sālim, from al-Ṣādiq Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad (a.s.), who said: The world both seeks and is sought. Whoever seeks the world, death seeks him until it takes him out of it; and whoever seeks the Hereafter, the world seeks him until it pays him his provision in full.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> This narration begins on page 364 and ends on page 365.</sub><br><sub>p. 364</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٢٤] وقال الصادق (عليه السلام): حسب المؤمن من الله نصرة أن يرى عدوّه يعمل بمعاصي الله عزّ وجلّ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Al-Ṣādiq (a.s.) said: It is help enough for the believer from Allah that he sees his enemy committing acts of disobedience to Allah, Mighty and Majestic.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The editor's footnote records كفى for حسب in al-Biḥār. The meaning is the same.</sub><br><sub>p. 365</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٢٥] وقال نبيّ الله (صلى الله عليه وآله): بادروا إلى رياض الجنّة.<br>قالوا: يا رسول الله، وما رياض الجنّة؟<br>قال: حلق الذكر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">The Prophet of Allah (s.a.w.) said: Hasten to the meadows of Paradise.<br>They said: O Messenger of Allah, and what are the meadows of Paradise?<br>He said: The circles of remembrance (dhikr, remembrance of Allah).<br><sub>p. 365</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٢٦] وروى محمّد بن أحمد بن يحيى، عن محمّد بن آدم، عن أبيه، عن أبي الحسن الرضا، عن آبائه (عليهم السلام)، عن عليّ (عليه السلام) قال: قال رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله) لعليّ:<br>يا عليّ، لا تشاورنّ جباناً فإنّه يضيق عليك المخرج، ولا تشاورنّ بخيلاً فإنّه يقصر بك عن غايتك، ولا تشاورنّ حريصاً فإنّه يزيّن لك شرّها، واعلم أنّ الجبن والبخل والحرص غريزة يجمعها سوء الظن.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Muḥammad ibn Aḥmad ibn Yaḥyā narrated from Muḥammad ibn Ādam, from his father, from Abū al-Ḥasan al-Riḍā, from his forefathers (a.s.), from ʿAlī (a.s.), who said: The Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.) said to ʿAlī:<br>O ʿAlī, never consult a coward, for he will narrow your way out; never consult a miser, for he will hold you back from your goal; and never consult a greedy person, for he will make its evil look attractive to you. And know that cowardice, miserliness, and greed are a single innate trait, united by thinking ill (sūʾ al-ẓann).<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> (1) يزيّن لك شرّها: the feminine pronoun has no explicit antecedent. It most likely refers to greed (as a disposition, غريزة) or to worldly gain or the matter under consultation. 'Its evil' keeps the ambiguity. (2) سوء الظن is unqualified here. It is commonly understood as thinking ill of Allah, i.e. distrust of His provision. I did not add 'of Allah' because it is not in the printed text. (3) Honorific after آبائه: plural ligature (عليهم السلام).</sub><br><sub>p. 365</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٢٧] روى الحسن بن محبوب، عن الهيثم بن واقد قال: سمعت الصادق جعفر بن محمّد (عليه السلام) يقول: من أخرجه الله عزّ وجلّ من ذلّ المعاصي إلى عزّة التقوى أغناه الله بلا مال، وأعزّه بلا عشيرة، وآنسه بلا أنيس، ومن خاف الله عزّوجلّ أخاف الله منه كلّ شيء، ومن لم يخف الله عزّ وجلّ أخافه الله من كلّ شيء، ومن رضي من الله عزّ وجلّ باليسير من الرّزق رضي الله عنه باليسير من العمل، ومن لم يستح من طلب المعاش خفّت مؤونته ونعم أهله، ومن زهد في الدنيا أثبت الله الحكمة في قلبه، وأنطق بها لسانه، وبصّره عيوب الدنيا داءها ودواءها، وأخرجه من الدنيا سالماً إلى دار السلام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Al-Ḥasan ibn Maḥbūb narrated from al-Haytham ibn Wāqid, who said: I heard al-Ṣādiq Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad (a.s.) say: Whomever Allah, Mighty and Majestic, brings out of the humiliation of sins into the honour of God-wariness, Allah makes rich without wealth, mighty without a clan, and gives companionship without a companion. Whoever fears Allah, Mighty and Majestic, Allah makes everything fear him; and whoever does not fear Allah, Mighty and Majestic, Allah makes him fear everything. Whoever is content with a little provision from Allah, Mighty and Majestic, Allah is content with a little deed from him. Whoever is not ashamed to seek a livelihood, his burden of expenses becomes light and his family live in comfort. Whoever renounces the world, Allah establishes wisdom in his heart and makes his tongue speak it, gives him insight into the faults of the world, its sickness and its cure, and brings him out of the world safe and sound to the Abode of Peace.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> (1) This narration begins on page 365 and ends on page 366. (2) خفّت مؤونته: 'his provisioning burden becomes light', i.e. he can meet his expenses. (3) ونعم أهله: read as نَعِمَ, 'his family enjoy ease'. (4) عزّوجلّ is printed without a space in one place; I kept it as printed.</sub><br><sub>p. 365</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٢٨] وروى أبو حمزة الثمالي قال: قال لي أبو جعفر (عليه السلام):<br>لمّا حضرت أبي (عليه السلام) الوفاة ضمّني إلى صدره ثمّ قال: يا بُنيّ، اصبر على الحقّ وإن كان مرّاً يوف إليك أجرك بغير حساب.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Abū Ḥamza al-Thumālī narrated, saying: Abū Jaʿfar (a.s.) said to me:<br>When death approached my father (a.s.), he held me to his chest and then said: My dear son, be patient upon the truth even if it is bitter, and your reward will be paid to you in full without measure.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> بغير حساب: 'without reckoning' or 'beyond measure', echoing Q 39:10. يوف is jussive, in the apodosis of the imperative.</sub><br><sub>p. 366</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٢٩] وروى ابن مسكان، عن عبد الله بن أبي يعفور قال: قال الصادق جعفر بن محمّد (عليه السلام) لرجل:<br>اجعل قلبك قريناً تزاوله، واجعل علمك والداً تتبعه، واجعل نفسك عدوّاً تجاهده، واجعل مالك كعارية تردّها.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Ibn Muskān narrated from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Yaʿfūr, who said: Al-Ṣādiq Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad (a.s.) said to a man:<br>Make your heart a companion you constantly attend to, make your knowledge a father you follow, make your self (nafs, the lower self) an enemy you struggle against, and make your wealth like a borrowed thing you will give back.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> (1) قريناً is the editor's reading from al-Faqīh. The original manuscript has قريباً ('a relative' or 'someone near'). (2) تزاوله: from زاول, 'to occupy oneself with, to deal with constantly'. It could also mean 'a companion you keep working on'.</sub><br><sub>p. 366</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٣٠] وقال (عليه السلام): جاهد هواك كما تجاهد عدوّك.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And he (a.s.) said: Struggle against your desires as you struggle against your enemy.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'He' refers back to al-Ṣādiq (a.s.) in the preceding narration.</sub><br><sub>p. 366</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٣١] روى الحسن بن راشد، عن أبي حمزة الثمالي، عن أبي جعفر (عليه السلام) قال: أتى رجل رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله) فقال: علّمني يا رسول الله شيئاً.<br>فقال (صلى الله عليه وآله): عليك باليأس ممّا في أيدي النّاس، فإنّه الغني الحاضر.<br>قال: زدني يا رسول الله.<br>فقال (صلى الله عليه وآله): إيّاك والطمع، فإنّه الفقر الحاضر.<br>قال: زدني يا رسول الله.<br>فقال (صلى الله عليه وآله): إذا هممت بأمر فتدبّر عاقبته، فإن يك خيراً أو رشداً اتّبعته، وإن يك شرّاً أو غيّاً تركته.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Al-Ḥasan ibn Rāshid narrated from Abū Ḥamza al-Thumālī, from Abū Jaʿfar (a.s.), who said: A man came to the Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.) and said: Teach me something, O Messenger of Allah.<br>He (s.a.w.) said: Hold to despairing of what is in people's hands, for that is ready wealth.<br>He said: Give me more, O Messenger of Allah.<br>He (s.a.w.) said: Beware of covetousness, for it is ready poverty.<br>He said: Give me more, O Messenger of Allah.<br>He (s.a.w.) said: When you set out to do something, consider its outcome carefully: if it is good or right guidance, follow it; and if it is evil or error, leave it.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> (1) This narration begins on page 366 and ends on page 367. (2) الغني is printed with dotted yāʾ but read al-ghinā ('wealth, self-sufficiency'). (3) الحاضر: 'present' or 'ready at hand', i.e. immediate. (4) اتّبعته / تركته are past-tense verbs in the apodosis of the conditional, which I rendered as imperatives ('follow it', 'leave it'). The editor's footnotes record al-Biḥār's readings فاتبعه and فدعه, which are explicit imperatives.</sub><br><sub>p. 366</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٣٢] وروى الحسين بن يزيد، عن عليّ بن غراب قال: الصادق جعفر بن محمد (عليه السلام): من خلا بذنب فراقب الله تعالى ذكره فيه، واستحى من الحفظة غفر الله عزّ وجلّ له جميع ذنوبه وإن كانت مثل ذنوب الثقلين.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Al-Ḥusayn ibn Yazīd narrated from ʿAlī ibn Ghurāb, who said: Al-Ṣādiq Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad (a.s.) [said]: Whoever is alone with a sin and is mindful of Allah, exalted be His mention, in it, and feels shame before the guardian angels who record deeds (al-ḥafaẓa), Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will forgive him all his sins, even if they are like the sins of the two weighty beings (al-thaqalān, jinn and humankind).<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> (1) The print reads قال: الصادق جعفر بن محمد (عليه السلام): with no second قال, so I supplied [said]. (2) بذنب is the editor's reading from the sources. The original manuscript has لذنب. (3) من خلا بذنب فراقب الله فيه: most naturally, 'whoever finds himself alone with [the opportunity for] a sin and, mindful of Allah, [refrains]'. The text itself does not say whether he refrains or commits it. (4) غراب vocalised as Ghurāb (not marked in the print).</sub><br><sub>p. 367</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٣٣] وروى العبّاس بن بكّار الضبّي [قال: حدّثنا محمّد بن سليمان الكوفي البزّاز] قال: حدّثنا عمرو بن خالد، عن زيد بن عليّ، عن أبيه عليّ بن الحسين، عن أبيه الحسين بن عليّ، عن أبيه أمير المؤمنين عليّ بن أبي طالب (عليهم السلام) قال: من مات يوم الخميس بعد زوال الشمس إلى يوم الجمعة وقت الزوال وكان مؤمناً أعاذه الله عزّ وجلّ من ضغطة القبر، وقبل شفاعته في مثل ربيعة ومضر.<br>ومن مات يوم السبت من المؤمنين لم يجمع الله بينه وبين اليهود في النار أبداً.<br>[ومن مات يوم الأحد من المؤمنين لم يجمع الله بينه وبين النصارى في النار أبداً].<br>ومن مات يوم الاثنين من المؤمنين لم يجمع الله عزّ وجلّ بينه وبين أعدائنا من بني أميّة في النار أبداً.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Al-ʿAbbās ibn Bakkār al-Ḍabbī narrated, [saying: Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān al-Kūfī al-Bazzāz narrated to us,] saying: ʿAmr ibn Khālid narrated to us from Zayd ibn ʿAlī, from his father ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn, from his father al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī, from his father Amir al-Mu'minin ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib (a.s.), who said: Whoever dies from Thursday after the sun has passed its zenith until Friday at the time of the zenith, being a believer, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will protect him from the squeezing of the grave and will accept his intercession for [as many people as] Rabīʿa and Muḍar.<br>And whoever of the believers dies on Saturday, Allah will never gather him together with the Jews in the Fire.<br>[And whoever of the believers dies on Sunday, Allah will never gather him together with the Christians in the Fire.]<br>And whoever of the believers dies on Monday, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will never gather him together with our enemies from the Banū Umayya in the Fire.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> (1) This narration continues onto page 368 (Tuesday, Wednesday, and further sayings). Per the boundary rule I stopped at the end of the Monday paragraph, which ends on page 368 with من بني أميّة في النار أبداً. The rest belongs to the next chunk. (2) Both bracketed passages are the editor's insertions from al-Faqīh: the second transmitter in the isnad, and the Sunday sentence. (3) The editor's footnote on page 368 says the original manuscript reads النصارى where al-Faqīh has بني أميّة in the Monday sentence. (4) The honorific after أبي طالب is a small ligature. I read it as the plural (عليهم السلام), covering the chain of Imams. (5) ربيعة ومضر are two very large Arab tribal confederations. The phrase is an idiom for a vast number of people.</sub><br><sub>p. 367</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">ومن مات يوم الثلاثاء من المؤمنين حشره الله عزّ وجلّ معنا في الرفيق الأعلى.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And whoever of the believers dies on Tuesday, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will gather him with us in the Highest Companionship (al-rafīq al-aʿlā).<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Continuation of narration [133] (begun on p. 367: Amir al-Mu'minin (a.s.) on the reward of believers who die on each day of the week). The leading fragment of p. 368 ('من بني أميّة في النار أبداً.'), which ends the Monday clause, belongs to the previous chunk and was skipped. 'With us' = the Ahl al-Bayt.</sub><br><sub>p. 368</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">ومن مات يوم الأربعاء من المؤمنين وقاه الله تعالى [نحس] يوم القيامة، وأسعده بمجاورته، وأحلّه دار المقامة من فضله، لا يمسّه فيها نصب ولا يمسّه فيها لغوب.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">And whoever of the believers dies on Wednesday, Allah the Exalted will protect him from the [ill-fortune] of the Day of Resurrection, will gladden him with His nearness, and will settle him in the Abode of Permanence out of His bounty; no toil will touch him there, and no weariness will touch him there.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> [نحس] is the editor's addition from al-Faqīh. The last clause echoes Q 35:35. 'بمجاورته' (lit. 'by his/His neighbourhood'): taken as nearness to Allah; the pronoun could also refer to the Prophet (s.a.w.).</sub><br><sub>p. 368</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">ثم قال (عليه السلام): المؤمن على أيّ الحالات مات، وفي أيّ يوم وساعة قبض، فهو صدّيق شهيد، ولقد سمعت حبيبي رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله) يقول: لو أنّ المؤمن خرج من الدنيا وعليه مثل ذنوب أهل الأرض لكان الموت كفّارة لتلك الذنوب.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Then he (a.s.) said: The believer, in whatever state he dies, and on whatever day and at whatever hour his soul is taken, is a truthful one (ṣiddīq) and a martyr. I heard my beloved, the Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.), say: If the believer were to leave this world bearing sins like the sins of all the people of the earth, death would be an expiation for those sins.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Speaker: Amir al-Mu'minin (a.s.), continuing [133].</sub><br><sub>p. 368</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">ثمّ قال (عليه السلام): من قال «لا إله إلاّ الله» بإخلاص فهو بريء من الشرك، ومن خرج من الدنيا لا يشرك بالله شيئاً دخل الجنّة، ثمّ تلا هذه الآية: ﴿إِنَّ اللَّهَ لاَ يَغْفِرُ أَنْ يُشْرَكَ بِهِ وَيَغْفِرُ مَا دُونَ ذَلِكَ لِمَنْ يَشَاءُ﴾ من شيعتك ومحبّيك يا عليّ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Then he (a.s.) said: Whoever says 'There is no god but Allah' with sincerity is free of shirk (associating partners with Allah), and whoever leaves this world without associating anything with Allah enters Paradise. Then he recited this verse: 'Indeed, Allah does not forgive that partners be associated with Him, but He forgives what is less than that for whomever He wills' [Q 4:116] — of your Shīʿa and those who love you, O ʿAlī.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The printed honorific is the (عليه السلام) ligature, but the closing address 'O ʿAlī' shows the speaker here is the Prophet (s.a.w.), apparently continuing the quotation introduced by 'I heard my beloved, the Messenger of Allah, say'. 'من شيعتك ومحبّيك' glosses 'for whomever He wills'. The editor cites Q 4:116; Q 4:48 has the same opening wording.</sub><br><sub>p. 368</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">قال أمير المؤمنين (عليه السلام): فقلت: يا رسول الله، هذا لشيعتي؟ قال: إي وربّي إنّه لشيعتك، وإنهم ليخرجون يوم القيامة من قبورهم وهم يقولون: لا إله إلاّ الله، محمّد رسول الله، عليّ بن أبي طالب حجّة الله، فيؤتون بحلل خضر من الجنّة، وأكاليل من الجنّة، [وتيجان من الجنّة،] ونجائب من الجنّة، فيلبس كلّ واحد منهم حلّة خضراء، ويوضع على رأسه تاج الملك وإكليل الكرامة، ثمّ يركبون النجائب فتطير بهم إلى الجنّة، لا يحزنهم الفزع الأكبر ﴿وَتَتَلَقَّاهُمُ الْمَلاَئِكَةُ هَذَا يَوْمُكُمُ الَّذِي كُنتُمْ تُوعَدُونَ﴾.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Amir al-Mu'minin (a.s.) said: So I said: O Messenger of Allah, is this for my Shīʿa? He said: Yes, by my Lord, it is for your Shīʿa. They will come out of their graves on the Day of Resurrection saying: 'There is no god but Allah, Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah, ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib is the Proof (ḥujja) of Allah.' Then green robes from Paradise will be brought to them, and circlets from Paradise, [and crowns from Paradise,] and noble mounts from Paradise. Each one of them will be clothed in a green robe, and on his head will be placed the crown of kingship and the circlet of honour. Then they will mount the noble mounts, which will fly with them to Paradise. The Greatest Terror will not grieve them, 'and the angels will meet them, [saying]: This is your day which you were promised' [Q 21:103].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Runs onto p. 369 and ends narration [133]. [وتيجان من الجنّة] is the editor's addition from al-Faqīh; editor's footnote: 'إكليل' as in al-Faqīh, the original MS has 'وفي إكليل'. Akālīl/iklīl rendered 'circlets' and tāj/tījān 'crowns' to keep the two words apart. Najāʾib = noble, swift riding-camels or mounts. 'لا يحزنهم الفزع الأكبر' is also Quranic wording (Q 21:103, the first half of the verse).</sub><br><sub>p. 368</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٣٤] وسئل الصادق (عليه السلام): ما حدّ حسن الخلق؟ قال: تليّن جانبك، وتطيّب كلامك، وتلقى أخاك ببشرٍ حسن. وسئل (عليه السلام): ما حدّ السخاء؟ قال: تخرج من مالك الحقّ الذي أوجبه الله عزّ وجلّ عليك [فتضعه] في موضعه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[134] Al-Ṣādiq (a.s.) was asked: What is the definition of good character? He said: That you be gentle in your dealings, make your speech pleasant, and meet your brother with a good, cheerful face. And he (a.s.) was asked: What is the definition of generosity? He said: That you take out of your wealth the right (ḥaqq) that Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has made obligatory upon you, [and place it] in its proper place.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'حدّ' = defining limit or measure. 'تليّن جانبك' is literally 'you soften your side'. [فتضعه] is the editor's addition from al-Faqīh. The two questions share one number, though the editor cites them separately (Maʿānī al-akhbār pp. 253 and 256).</sub><br><sub>p. 369</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٣٥] وروى يعقوب بن يزيد، عن أحمد بن الحسن الميثمي، عن الحسين بن أبي حمزة قال: سمعت أبا عبد الله (عليه السلام) يقول: أنفق وأيقن بالخلف، واعلم أنّه مَن لم ينفق في طاعة [الله] ابتلي بأن ينفق في معصية الله عزّ وجلّ، ومَن لم يمش في حاجة ولي الله ابتلي بأن يمشي في حاجة عدوّ الله عزّوجلّ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[135] Yaʿqūb ibn Yazīd narrated from Aḥmad ibn al-Ḥasan al-Maythamī, from al-Ḥusayn ibn Abī Ḥamza, who said: I heard Abū ʿAbd Allāh (a.s.) say: Spend, and be certain of the replacement. And know that whoever does not spend in obedience to [Allah] will be afflicted with spending in disobedience to Allah, Mighty and Majestic; and whoever does not walk [to meet] the need of a friend (walī) of Allah will be afflicted with walking [to meet] the need of an enemy of Allah, Mighty and Majestic.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> al-khalaf = what Allah gives back in place of what is spent (cf. Q 34:39). [الله] is the editor's addition from al-Faqīh and al-Biḥār. 'عزّوجلّ' at the end is printed without a space.</sub><br><sub>p. 369</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٣٦] وروى أحمد بن إسحاق بن سعد، عن عبد الله بن ميمون، عن الصادق [جعفر بن محمّد، عن أبيه] (عليهما السلام) قال: قال الفضل بن عبّاس: أهدي إلى رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله) بغلة أهداها له كسرى أو قيصر، فركبها النبيّ (صلى الله عليه وآله) بجلّ من شعر فأردفني خلفه، ثمّ قال لي: يا غلام، احفظ الله يحفظك، واحفظ الله تجده أمامك، تعرّف إلى الله عزّ وجلّ [في الرخاء] يعرفك في الشدّة، إذا سألت فاسأل الله، وإذا استعنت فاستعن بالله عزّ وجلّ، فقد مضى القلم بما هو كائن، فلو جهد الناس أن ينفعوك بأمر لم يكتبه الله لك لم يقدروا عليه، ولو جهدوا أن يضرّوك بأمر لم يكتبه الله عليك لم يقدروا عليه، فإن استطعت أن تعمل بالصبر مع اليقين فافعل، فإن لم تستطع فاصبر، فإنّ [في] الصبر على ما تكره خيراً كثيراً، واعلم أنّ النصر مع الصبر، وأنّ الفرج مع الكرب، وأنّ مع العسر يُسراً، [أنّ مع العسر يُسراً].</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[136] Aḥmad ibn Isḥāq ibn Saʿd narrated from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Maymūn, from al-Ṣādiq [Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad, from his father] (a.s.), who said: Al-Faḍl ibn ʿAbbās said: A mule was given as a gift to the Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.); Chosroes or Caesar had presented it to him. The Prophet (s.a.w.) rode it on a saddle-cloth of hair and seated me behind him. Then he said to me: Young man, be mindful of Allah and He will guard you; be mindful of Allah and you will find Him before you. Make yourself known to Allah, Mighty and Majestic, [in times of ease] and He will know you in times of hardship. When you ask, ask Allah; and when you seek help, seek help from Allah, Mighty and Majestic, for the Pen has already run with all that is to be. Were people to strive to benefit you with something Allah has not written for you, they would not be able to; and were they to strive to harm you with something Allah has not written against you, they would not be able to. If you can act with patience together with certainty, then do so; and if you cannot, then be patient, for [in] patience over what you dislike there is much good. And know that victory comes with patience, that relief comes with distress, and that with hardship comes ease, [with hardship comes ease].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Starts at the foot of p. 369 and continues on p. 370. The bracketed parts [جعفر بن محمّد، عن أبيه], [في الرخاء], [في] and the final [أنّ مع العسر يُسراً] are the editor's additions from al-Faqīh. The honorific after 'عن أبيه]' is read as the dual ligature (عليهما السلام); it is small and not fully clear. 'احفظ الله' is literally 'guard/keep Allah', i.e. observe His commands and limits. Editor's note: the phrase 'وأنّ الفرج مع الكرب' stands at the very end of the hadith in the original MS. The ending echoes Q 94:5-6.</sub><br><sub>p. 369</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٣٧] روى محمّد بن عليّ الكوفي، عن إسماعيل بن مهران، عن مرازم، عن جابر بن يزيد، عن جابر بن عبد الله الأنصاري قال: رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله): إذا وقع الولد في جوف بطن أُمّه صار وجهه قبل ظهر أُمّه إن كان ذكراً، وإن كانت أُنثى صار وجهها قبل بطن أُمّها، ويداه على وجنتيه، وذقنه على ركبتيه كهيئة الحزين المهموم، فهو كالمصرور بنوط بمعاء من سرّته إلى سرّة أُمّه، فبتلك السرّة يغتذي من طعام أُمّه وشرابها إلى الوقت المقدّر لولادته، فيبعث الله عزّ وجلّ ملكاً فيكتب على جبهته شقي أو سعيد، مؤمن أو كافر، غني أو فقير، ويكتب أجله ورزقه، وسُقمه وصحّته، فإذا انقطع الرزق المقدّر له من سرّة أُمّه زجره الملك زجرة، فانقلب جزعاً من الزجرة، وصار رأسه قبل المخرج.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[137] Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī al-Kūfī narrated from Ismāʿīl ibn Mihrān, from Murāzim, from Jābir ibn Yazīd, from Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Anṣārī, who said: The Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.) [said]: When the child settles in the depths of its mother's womb, if it is male its face is turned toward its mother's back, and if it is female its face is turned toward its mother's belly. Its hands are on its cheeks and its chin on its knees, in the posture of one who is grieved and troubled. It is like something bound up, tied by a cord of gut from its navel to its mother's navel. Through that navel it is nourished from its mother's food and drink until the time appointed for its birth. Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, sends an angel, who writes on its forehead: wretched or blessed, believer or unbeliever, rich or poor; and he writes its term of life and its provision, its sickness and its health. When the provision appointed for it through its mother's navel is cut off, the angel gives it a sharp rebuke, and it turns over in alarm at the rebuke, so that its head is toward the exit.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The printed text reads 'قال: رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله):' with the colon after قال; understood as 'قال رسول الله' ('The Messenger of Allah said'). مرازم and إسماعيل بن مهران are the editor's corrections from al-Faqīh (the MS has رزام and حمران). 'في جوف' is not in al-Faqīh. 'كالمصرور بنوط بمعاء': maṣrūr = tied up in a bundle; nawṭ = a cord by which something hangs; miʿāʾ = gut, i.e. the umbilical cord. 'زجره زجرة' = shouts at it or drives it with a single rebuke. This is the first of several segments of the long narration [137], which runs to p. 372.</sub><br><sub>p. 370</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">فإذا وقع إلى الأرض وقع إلى هول عظيم وعذاب أليم، إن أصابته ريح أو مشقّة أو مسّته يد وجد لذلك من الألم ما وجده المسلوخ عنه جلده، يجوع فلا يقدر على الاستطعام، ويعطش فلا يقدر على [الاستسقاء، ويتوجّع فلا يقدر على] الاستغاثة، فيوكّل الله تبارك وتعالى برحمته والشفقة عليه، والمحبّة له أُمّه فتقيه الحرّ والبرد بنفسها، وتكاد تفديه بروحها، وتصبر من التعطّف عليه بحال لا تبالي أن يجوع إذا شبع، وتعطّش إذا روى، وتعرّى إذا كسى.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">When it drops to the earth, it drops into great terror and painful torment. If a wind or some hardship strikes it, or a hand touches it, it feels from that a pain like the pain felt by one whose skin has been flayed from him. It is hungry but cannot ask for food; it is thirsty but cannot ask for drink; [it is in pain but cannot] cry for help. So Allah, Blessed and Exalted, charges its mother with His mercy toward it, and with compassion and love for it. She shields it from heat and cold with her own body, and would all but ransom it with her own life. Out of tenderness toward it she comes to such a state that she does not mind going hungry when it is full, going thirsty when it has drunk its fill, and going bare when it is clothed.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Continuation of [137]. 'مشقّة' is not in al-Faqīh. [الاستسقاء، ويتوجّع فلا يقدر على] is the editor's addition from al-Faqīh. 'تفديه' is the al-Faqīh reading adopted by the editor; the MS has 'تغذّيه' ('nourish it'). The printed 'وتصبر' ('and she endures') is translated as if it read وتصير ('she comes to be ... in a state'), which the following 'بحال' calls for; with تصبر it would mean 'she endures, out of tenderness toward it, a state in which...'. The printed 'أن يجوع' has ي, but the parallel verbs تعطّش/تعرّى, and the sense, call for تجوع ('that she go hungry'); it is translated that way.</sub><br><sub>p. 371</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وجعل الله تعالى ذكره رزقه في ثدي أُمّه في أحديهما شرابه، وفي الأُخرى طعامه وحتّى إذا وضع آتاه الله عزّ وجلّ في كلّ يوم بما قدّر فيه من رزق، فإذا أدرك [فهمّه] الأهل والمال والشرّ والحرص ثمّ هو معذلك بمعرض الآفات والعاهات والبليّات من كلّ وجه، والملائكة تهديه وترشده، والشياطين تضلّه وتغويه، فهو هالك إلاّ أن ينجيه الله عزّ وجلّ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Allah, exalted be His mention, placed its provision in its mother's breasts: in one of them its drink and in the other its food. Then, when it is weaned, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, brings it each day the provision He has decreed for that day. When he reaches maturity, [his concern becomes] family, wealth, evil, and greed; and on top of that he is exposed to afflictions, blights, and trials from every side. The angels guide him and direct him aright, while the devils lead him astray and seduce him. So he is doomed unless Allah, Mighty and Majestic, saves him.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Continuation of [137]; runs onto p. 372. 'أحديهما' is printed thus (standard إحداهما). 'وضع' (per the MS; al-Faqīh has 'رضع') is taken as wuḍiʿa, 'is set down [from the breast]', i.e. weaned. It could also be read as 'is born', though the birth was described above. With al-Faqīh's 'رضع' the clause would read 'when it suckles'. [فهمّه] is the editor's addition from al-Faqīh. 'الشرّ' is printed with shadda ('evil'); al-sharah ('avidity, gluttony') would fit the list better but is not what is printed. 'معذلك' is printed without a space (= مع ذلك). Editor's footnote: al-Faqīh has 'يعرض للآفات'. The pronoun switches from 'it' to 'he' at maturity only for English readability; the Arabic is masculine throughout.</sub><br><sub>p. 371</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">وقد ذكر الله تبارك وتعالى ذكره نسبة الإنسان في محكم كتابه فقال الله عزّوجلّ: ﴿وَلَقَدْ خَلَقْنَا الإِنْسَانَ مِنْ سُلاَلَةٍ مِنْ طِينٍ، ثُمَّ جَعَلْنَاهُ نُطْفَةً فِي قَرَارٍ مَكِينٍ، ثُمَّ خَلَقْنَا النُّطْفَةَ عَلَقَةً فَخَلَقْنَا الْعَلَقَةَ مُضْغَةً فَخَلَقْنَا الْمُضْغَةَ عِظَامًا فَكَسَوْنَا الْعِظَامَ لَحْمًا ثُمَّ أَنْشَأْنَاهُ خَلْقًا آخَرَ فَتَبَارَكَ اللَّهُ أَحْسَنُ الْخَالِقِينَ، ثُمَّ إِنَّكُمْ بَعْدَ ذَلِكَ لَمَيِّتُونَ، ثُمَّ إِنَّكُمْ يَوْمَ الْقِيَامَةِ تُبْعَثُونَ﴾.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Allah, Blessed and Exalted be His mention, has described the origin of the human being in the firm verses of His Book, where Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says: 'And We certainly created man from an extract of clay; then We placed him as a drop of fluid in a secure resting place; then We made the drop into a clinging clot, and We made the clot into a lump of flesh, and We made the lump into bones, and We clothed the bones with flesh; then We brought him forth as another creation. So blessed is Allah, the best of creators. Then after that you will surely die; then on the Day of Resurrection you will be raised' [Q 23:12-16].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Continuation of [137], still the Prophet's words (Jābir's question follows). 'نسبة الإنسان' is literally 'the lineage/derivation of man', i.e. his origin. 'في محكم كتابه' = in the decisive or firmly-worded [text] of His Book.</sub><br><sub>p. 372</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">قال جابر بن عبد الله الأنصاري: فقلت: يا رسول الله، هذا حالنا فكيف حالك وحال الأوصياء بعدك في الولادة؟ فسكت رسول الله (صلى الله عليه وآله) [مليّاً] ثمّ قال: يا جابر، لقد سألت عن أمر جسيم لا يحتمله إلاّ ذو حظّ عظيم؛ إنّ الأنبياء والأوصياء مخلوقون من نور عظمة الله جلّ ثنائه، يودّع الله أنوارهم أصلاباً طيّبة، وأرحاماً طاهرة، يحفظها بملائكته، ويزيّنها بحكمته، ويغفروها بعلمه، فأمرهم يجلّ عن أن يوصف، وأحوالهم يدقّ عن أن يعلم لأنّهم نجوم الله في أرضه، وأعلامه في بريّته، وخلفائه على عباده، وأنواره في بلاده، وحججه في خلقه. يا جابر، هذا من مكنون العلم ومخزونه فاكتمه إلاّ من أهله.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Anṣārī said: So I said: O Messenger of Allah, this is our condition; so what is your condition, and that of the successors (awṣiyāʾ) after you, in birth? The Messenger of Allah (s.a.w.) was silent [for a long while], then said: O Jābir, you have asked about a weighty matter that none can bear except one of great fortune. The prophets and the successors are created from the light of the majesty of Allah, exalted be His praise. Allah deposits their lights in goodly loins and pure wombs. He guards them through His angels, adorns them with His wisdom, and shelters them with His knowledge. Their affair is too exalted to be described, and their states too subtle to be known, for they are Allah's stars on His earth, His signposts among His creatures, His vicegerents over His servants, His lights in His lands, and His proofs among His creation. O Jābir, this is of the hidden and treasured knowledge, so conceal it except from those worthy of it.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> End of [137]. [مليّاً] is the editor's addition from al-Faqīh. 'جلّ ثنائه' and 'خلفائه' are printed thus (standard ثناؤه, خلفاؤه). 'ويزيّنها' (al-Faqīh: ويربّيها, 'nurtures them'). 'ويغفروها' as printed is grammatically odd (a plural verb with a singular subject); it is taken from غَفَرَ 'to cover, shelter', hence 'shelters them'. Al-Faqīh reads ويغذوها ('nourishes them'). 'وحججه في خلقه' (al-Faqīh: على خلقه, 'over His creation').</sub><br><sub>p. 372</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٣٨] وروى المفضّل بن عمر، عن ثابت الثمالي، عن حبّابة الوالبية رضي الله عنها قالت: سمعت مولاي أمير المؤمنين (عليه السلام) يقول: إنّا أهل بيت لا نشرب المسكّر، ولا نأكل الجرّي، ولا نمسح على الخفّين، فمن كان من شيعتنا فليقتد بنا وليستنّ، بسنّتنا.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[138] Al-Mufaḍḍal ibn ʿUmar narrated from Thābit al-Thumālī, from Ḥabbāba al-Wālibiyya (may Allah be pleased with her), who said: I heard my master Amir al-Mu'minin (a.s.) say: We are a household (Ahl al-Bayt) who do not drink intoxicants, do not eat the jirrī, and do not wipe over leather socks [in ablution]. So whoever is of our Shīʿa, let him follow our example and adhere to our Sunna (practice).<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> al-jirrī = a scaleless fish (catfish/eel type), forbidden in Imāmī law. 'المسح على الخفّين' = wiping over leather socks in wuḍūʾ instead of over the feet. The diacritic on 'المسكّر' looks like a shadda (perhaps a shadda-kasra cluster); the word is al-muskir. A comma is printed between 'وليستنّ' and 'بسنّتنا'.</sub><br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٣٩] روى حمّاد بن عثمان، عن الصادق (عليه السلام) قال في حكمة آل داود: ينبغي للعاقل أن يكون مقبلاً على شأنه، حافظاً للسانه، عارفاً لأهل زمانه.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[139] Ḥammād ibn ʿUthmān narrated from al-Ṣādiq (a.s.), who said: In the wisdom of the family of Dāwūd [it says]: The intelligent person should be attentive to his own affairs, guarding his tongue, and knowing the people of his time.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> 'قال في حكمة آل داود' can also be read 'he said, concerning the wisdom of the family of Dāwūd'. Either way the saying is attributed to the wisdom-literature of Dāwūd's family.</sub><br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٤٠] وروى صفوان بن يحيى ومحمّد بن أبي عمير، عن موسى بن بكر، عن زرارة، عن الصّادق جعفر بن محمّد (عليهما السلام) قال:</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[140] Ṣafwān ibn Yaḥyā and Muḥammad ibn Abī ʿUmayr narrated from Mūsā ibn Bakr, from Zurāra, from al-Ṣādiq Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad (a.s.), who said:<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Isnad only. The short sayings numbered [141]-[158] follow, printed as an indented list, and are taken to fall under this isnad; the editor numbered each one separately. The honorific is read as the dual ligature (عليهما السلام) (for Jaʿfar and his father Muḥammad).</sub><br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٤١] الصنيعة لا تكون صنيعة إلاّ عند ذي حسب أو دين.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[141] A favour is not [truly] a favour unless it is done to someone of good standing (ḥasab) or religion.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> ḥasab = inherited nobility or personal merit.</sub><br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٤٢] الصلاة قربان كلّ تقيّ.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[142] Prayer is the offering (qurbān) of every God-fearing person.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> qurbān = an offering by which one draws near to Allah.</sub><br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٤٣] الحجّ جهاد كلّ ضعيف.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[143] The Hajj is the jihad (striving in Allah's way) of every weak person.<br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٤٤] لكل شيء زكاة وزكاة الجسد الصيام.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[144] Everything has a zakat (alms-due; literally 'purification'), and the zakat of the body is fasting.<br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٤٥] جهاد المرأة حسن التبعّل [لزوجها].</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[145] A woman's jihad is being a good wife [to her husband].<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> ḥusn al-tabaʿʿul = conducting herself well as a wife. The bracket is the editor's.</sub><br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٤٦] استنزلوا الرّزق بالصدقة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[146] Bring down provision by giving charity (ṣadaqa).<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> The shadda in 'الرّزق' sits between ر and ز in the print; transcribed on the sun-letter ر.</sub><br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٤٧] من أيقن بالخلف جاد بالعطية.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[147] Whoever is certain of the replacement [from Allah] is generous in giving.<br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٤٨] إنّ الله تبارك وتعالى ينزل المعونة على قدر المؤونة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[148] Indeed, Allah, Blessed and Exalted, sends down help in proportion to the burden.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> al-maʾūna = the burden of provision or expenses (e.g. of dependents).</sub><br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٤٩] حصّنوا أموالكم بالزّكاة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[149] Fortify your wealth with zakat.<br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٥٠] التقدير نصف العيش.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[150] Careful budgeting is half of livelihood.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> taqdīr = measuring out, managing one's means prudently.</sub><br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٥١] ما عال امرء إقتصد</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[151] No man who is moderate [in spending] becomes destitute.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> Printed without a final full stop, and with the spellings 'امرء' (standard امرؤ) and 'إقتصد' (standard اقتصد).</sub><br><sub>p. 373</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٥٢] قلّة العيال أحد اليسارين.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[152] Having few dependents is one of the two [kinds of] ease.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> yasār = affluence, ease of means; i.e. few dependents is as good as having more wealth.</sub><br><sub>p. 374</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٥٣] الداعي بلا عمل كالرامي بلا وتر.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[153] One who calls [to good] without acting is like an archer without a bowstring.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> al-dāʿī can mean 'one who calls/invites [others]' or 'one who supplicates'. The second reading gives: 'one who prays without working is like an archer without a bowstring'.</sub><br><sub>p. 374</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٥٤] التودّد نصف العقل.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[154] Showing affection [to people] is half of intelligence.<br><sub>p. 374</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٥٥] الهمّ نصف الهرَم.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[155] Worry is half of old age.<br><sub>p. 374</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٥٦] إنّ الله تبارك وتعالى ينزل الصبر على قدر المصيبة.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[156] Indeed, Allah, Blessed and Exalted, sends down patience in proportion to the calamity.<br><sub>p. 374</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٥٧] من ضرب يده على فخذه عند المصيبة حبط أجره.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[157] Whoever strikes his hand on his thigh when calamity strikes, his reward is voided.<br><sub>p. 374</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td dir="rtl" lang="ar" width="50%" valign="top">[١٥٨] من أحزن والديه فقد عقّهما.</td>
<td width="50%" valign="top">[158] Whoever saddens his parents has been undutiful to them.<br><br><sub><b>Notes:</b> ʿaqq/ʿuqūq = undutifulness toward parents, a major sin.</sub><br><sub>p. 374</sub></td>
</tr>
<tr>
